### 🟢 `ACTION LM` — Article `50` is settled on CONTENT, unsettled on CHANNEL

🟢 **`8` independent non-vendor-blog channels agree: the AI Act's Article `50` transparency
obligations became applicable on `2` August `2026`.** 🔵 **They are a standards body (BSI), a
national AI service desk (`ai-at.eu`, Austria), a legal directory (Legal 500) and five
independent law firms (Cooley, Baker Botts, Morgan Lewis, DLA Piper, Sidley) — none of them
selling an AI product.**

| sub-point | status |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **Article `50`(1), (2), (5) applicable** | 🟢 **`2` Aug `2026`** — unanimous across `8` channels |
| 🟡 **transition for systems already on the market** | 🟡 **`2` Dec `2026`** for Article `50`(2) obligations *(one channel, uncontradicted)* |
| 🟡 **Commission guidelines** | 🟡 **CONTESTED**: one channel says guidelines were adopted `20` July `2026`; others still describe them as DRAFT. 🔵 Do not tell a client the guidance is final |
| 🟡 **penalties** | up to **€`15` M or `3`% of worldwide annual turnover**, whichever is higher *(one channel)* |
| 🔴 **primary text** | 🔴 **UNREACHABLE and now permanently so.** Not a eur-lex quirk — the whole open web is off this channel |

🟢 **Pattern `2`'s ordering (transparency before conformity) is CONFIRMED on content.** 🟡 **It
should still be presented to a client with its provenance: *"agreed by eight independent
secondary channels, primary text not verifiable from our environment"*.**

## Opportunities by region

🔵 **One subsection per region, same five values as the frontmatter vocabulary. Where a region has
no supply, that is stated — an informed gap is information.**

### North America

🟢 **Demand is REGULATORY and it is fragmenting by state, which is itself the opportunity.**
`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states in the `2026` session. Ohio requires every district
to hold a formal AI use policy from `1` July `2026`; Oklahoma and Maryland require human oversight
and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students; California AB `1159` would prohibit using
student data to train models; Idaho SB `1227` requires privacy protections for school AI tools;
New York City has a one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through `8`th grade. Federally,
H.R. `8747` *(K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act)* advanced out of committee on `21` July `2026`
— 🟡 **final status not established here.**

🟢 **Adoption is real:** `60`% of US K-12 teachers used AI tools in the `2024`-`25` school year,
`32`% at least weekly. 🟡 One market house puts North America at `36`% of global share.
🔵 **Named movers: Microsoft (Copilot + Teams for Education free to Washington classrooms for
three years), state education agencies, NC's AI Leadership Council and AI Accelerator, and the
NEA/AFT/CTA guidance.**

🟢 **The engagement shape: a POLICY-CONFORMANCE layer over an incumbent LMS.** A district cannot
buy one national compliance posture — it needs per-state policy as configuration, human-oversight
checkpoints on anything high-stakes, and an audit trail that shows a human decided.
🟢 **[`civil-ai`](https://github.com/zhangl1001/civil-ai)'s now-verified `assessment_policy_versions`
+ `agent_tool_receipts` schema is the closest thing on this shelf to that shape, and it is `MIT`.**
🔴 **Supply gap: `0` North-American ROUTE-grade permissive rows, for a fourth pass. The build
starts from an EMEA or APAC codebase.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Demand is COMPLIANCE-DATED, which makes it the most plannable region.** Article `50`
transparency has applied since `2` August `2026` *(confirmed on `8` channels this pass)*, with the
`2` December `2026` transition closing for systems already on the market, and Annex III high-risk
— access decisions, outcome evaluation, applicant screening, exam monitoring — landing
`2` December `2027`. Emotion inference in educational settings is prohibited. The AI Office and
national authorities are enforcing.

🟢 **Supply is also strongest here: `5` of the `6` ROUTE-grade permissive rows are EMEA**
*(`OpenOLAT`, `Artemis`, `tutor-mcp`, `mentingo`, `feedbacksystem`)*.

🟢 **The engagement shape: an Annex III readiness build on a permissive EMEA platform, started
now against the December `2027` date.** `OpenOLAT` *(`Apache-2.0`, `LTI 1.3`/`QTI`/`SCORM`)* for
the institutional route; `feedbacksystem` where automated grading is the high-risk surface —
🆕 🟢 **and it needs no IdP procurement** *(`ACTION LQ`)*.

### APAC

🟢 **Demand is REGULATORY and EARLY, with the region's hard deadline ahead of the EU's.**
South Korea's AI Basic Act has been in force since January `2026`, names education among
high-impact uses, and 🟡 **requires foreign providers to appoint a local representative** — a
corporate step, not an engineering one, and it is usually the long pole. Vietnam's Law
`134/2025/QH15` took effect `1` March `2026`, names automated assessment and behavioural
monitoring as high-risk education uses, **reaches foreign companies**, and its compliance date is
`1` March `2027` — 🟡 **nine months before the EU's Annex III date.** Japan stays light-touch and
voluntary; India and Australia are still drafting.

🟡 **Market:** one house projects APAC at `~$18.2` B by `2033` *(`28.1`% CAGR from `2026`)* —
🔴 **not reconcilable with the global figures above, so treat it as a direction, not a size.**
UNESCO notes readiness varies sharply, with China and Singapore holding formal AI-in-education
policies while others lack basic provision. Ipsos' `2026` Education Monitor finds lower support
for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets surveyed, and higher support in Australia and
New Zealand. Baidu's ERNIE has entered Indonesian and Thai education through partnerships.

🟢 **Supply: `4` APAC rows** *(`OpenTutor` ROUTE, `civil-ai` APPLIANCE+, `Bloom`, `Study-Mate`,
`universal-examprep-skill`)*. 🟢 **The engagement shape: a multi-jurisdiction assessment system
where the high-risk classification differs by country — policy as data, per-jurisdiction, with
the Vietnam date driving the schedule.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The richest DEMAND evidence of any region, and still `0` supply.** UNESCO IESALC's `2026`
study of `200` institutions across `19` countries finds **`87`% using AI in at least one area**
and `74`% in teaching and learning — but **only `26`% with a formal AI strategy**, with adoption
driven by individual academics and students rather than institutions. A Digital Education Council
faculty survey puts LATAM faculty AI use at `79`%, while `88`% report only minimal-to-moderate
engagement. 🔵 **Those two measure different things and should not be averaged.**

🟢 **Institutional movers are unusually concrete:** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for
Latin America and the Caribbean** launched April `2026` with CAF, CENIA *(Chile)*, CETIC.br
*(Brazil)*, ECLAC, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal
*(Uruguay)*; an August `2026` Mexico pilot with CONALEP and DGETI; a September `2026` ministerial
statement at Digital Learning Week. **Regulation is uneven:** Uruguay was the first LATAM country
to sign the Council of Europe AI framework convention *(2025)*; Colombia set national AI policy
via CONPES `4144` *(Feb 2025)*; Mexico's Congress is converging on a risk-based federal bill.

🔴 **Supply: `0` rows, and this pass makes that a MEASUREMENT.** `ACTION LP` searched in Spanish
and Portuguese and cloned every plausible candidate: the Spanish-language tutor is from Spain, the
Portuguese-language *"educational agent platform"* is a CRM, the remaining permissive candidate is
Maghreb-localised. 🔴 **`0` LATAM-origin, `0` LATAM-localised** *(no `es` or `pt-BR` catalogue on
any shelf row)*.

🟢 **The engagement shape, and it is the clearest commercial line in this file: the `87`/`26` gap
is a GOVERNANCE engagement, not a tooling one** — institutions already use AI and lack the
strategy, policy and audit trail to defend it. 🟢 **And because no shelf row carries a Spanish or
Portuguese catalogue, the localisation layer is an explicit, quotable line item on every LATAM
build, not an assumption.**

### Global

🟢 **Cross-region, the constant is that identity, tenancy and audit are the reliably absent
layers** *(see [`intel/trends.md`](trends.md))*. 🟢 **A studio that owns those three can take the
same permissive platform into all four regions and differentiate only the policy data and the
locale catalogue.** 🔴 **The market size is a `19`% band on the current year and should never be
quoted as a point.**

🆕 🔴 **And the market figure moved the wrong way: the range for a SINGLE year WIDENED.** Two

## Opportunities by region

🔵 **Supply placements come from reading TREES; demand and regulation come from prose search. Where
a region has demand evidence and no supply evidence, this section says so rather than leaving the
asymmetry to be inferred.**

### North America

🟢 **Demand: the strongest policy-driven procurement trigger of any region, and it is live now.**
`134` bills on `AI` in education were introduced across `31` states in `2026`. **Ohio** is the first
state to require every K-12 district to adopt a formal `AI` use policy *(deadline `1` July
`2026` — already passed)*. **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** bar `AI` from high-stakes decisions about
students, requiring human oversight. **New York City** runs a one-year moratorium on student-facing
`AI` through `8`th grade; **Katy ISD (Texas)** bans generative chatbots for K-6 and widens access in
later grades. Federally, the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 (H.R. `8747`)** advanced in
House committee markup in July. `30`+ states already publish guidance. Reported adoption: `60`% of
US K-12 teachers used `AI` tools in the `2024`-`25` school year; North America is put at `36`% of the
global market *(aggregator figure, treat loosely)*.

🔴 **Supply: `0` ROUTE-grade permissive rows.** The one North-American row read this pass —
[`brownplt/LTLTutor`](https://github.com/brownplt/LTLTutor), Brown PLT — is **`GPL-3.0`**.

🟢 **The opportunity, specifically:** every Ohio district needs a *policy* and the *evidence* that
its tools comply, and Oklahoma/Maryland need a demonstrable **human-in-the-loop** record. 🟢 **That
is exactly [`zhangl1001/civil-ai`](https://github.com/zhangl1001/civil-ai)'s
`assessment_policy_versions` + `agent_tool_receipts` shape (`MIT`), or
[`ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp)'s `audit_events` +
`assessment_adjudications` (`MIT`).** 🔵 **Sell the audit trail, not the tutor. The mandate is for a
policy with proof, and the permissive supply for proof exists — just not from this region.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Demand + the clearest regulatory calendar.** The `AI` Act is the binding instrument and
education sits squarely in Annex III — admissions, learning-outcome evaluation, exam monitoring —
now at **`2` December `2027`**, while **Article `50` transparency applies already**. Enforcement by
the `AI` Office and national authorities began `2` August `2026`. One vendor estimate puts the
European `AI`-in-education market at `$2.64` B in `2026` growing to `$8.0` B by `2030` *(`31.9`%
CAGR)*, naming **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** as K-12 leaders. The **UK** put ~`£4` M into
`AI` lesson-planning and marking tools. The **OECD** `2026` Digital Education Outlook argues for
purpose-built educational `AI` over general-purpose tools; **UNESCO**'s generative-`AI` guidance
remains the international reference.

🟢 **Supply: this is where the permissive supply actually lives — `5` of the `6` ROUTE rows.**
`OpenOLAT` *(Apache-2.0, Swiss-led consortium)*, `Artemis` *(MIT, TU München)*, `mentingo` *(MIT,
Selleo, Poland)*, `tutor-mcp` *(MIT, French-language design docs)*, `feedbacksystem` *(Apache-2.0,
TH Mittelhessen)*, plus `dolos` *(MIT, Ghent)*.

🟡 **Middle East and Africa, inside this region's vocabulary:** the UAE's mandated nationwide K-12
`AI` curriculum *(~`1` M pupils, `7` strands)*, Saudi and Qatari national strategies, Alef Education
and Classera as named platforms, and national frameworks in Nigeria *(`2024`)* and Kenya *(`2025`)*
all stand from p180. 🆕 🟡 **New this pass: the first shelf row with first-class Arabic + RTL
(`lumen` — `rtl` in `37` files, a complete `ar.ts` catalogue, an i18n-parity test).** 🔴 **It is
`GPL-3.0`, and its holder's country is NOT established.** 🔴 **`0` permissive MEA-origin repositories
still (`gap 124`, supply half).**

🟢 **The opportunity, specifically:** `OpenOLAT` *(Apache-2.0, `LTI 1.3` + `QTI` + `SCORM` +
`OpenBadges`, own `Shibboleth`/`LDAP`)* is the only permissive full LMS anywhere, and it is
EMEA-held — so an EMEA engagement can be built on a permissive system of record that already speaks
the institutional standards. 🔴 **Budget the `385`-table discovery spike explicitly.** For the Gulf
curriculum mandates, the Arabic/RTL layer is the scarce asset and `lumen`'s copyleft licence means
it is a reference implementation, not a base.

### APAC

🟢 **Demand + the most binding near-term obligations outside the EU.** **South Korea**'s `AI` Basic
Act took effect January `2026` with education inside *"high-impact"*, and foreign providers must
appoint a local representative. **Vietnam**'s Law `134/2025/QH15` took effect `1` March `2026`, names
education as high-risk with automated assessment and behavioural monitoring as the examples, reaches
foreign companies, and sets compliance for `1` March `2027`. **Japan** is light-touch and voluntary;
**Singapore** and Japan work through governance toolkits; **China** has binding content rules;
**Australia/New Zealand** are light-touch with a safety emphasis. Market reports name Google,
Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's as regional players, put China, India and Japan in the lead, and
project a `28.1`% CAGR for `2026`-`2033` *(vendor figures)*. UNESCO notes readiness varies sharply:
China and Singapore have education-`AI` policies while others are still meeting basic needs. The
Ipsos Education Monitor `2026` finds LOWER support for banning `AI` in schools across Asian markets
than in Australia and New Zealand.

🟢 **Supply: `4` rows, all individual- or lab-held** — `DeepTutor` *(Apache-2.0, HKUDS)*, `OpenTutor`
*(MIT)*, `civil-ai` *(MIT)*, `gen-mentor` *(`CC0`)*, plus `Bloom`, `Study-Mate` and
`universal-examprep-skill` at COMPONENT/APPLIANCE grade.

🟢 **The opportunity, specifically:** Vietnam's `1` March `2027` deadline is the one date a studio
can sell against right now, and what it requires for automated assessment is traceability.
🟢 **`civil-ai` is `MIT`, APAC-origin, and already models `assessment_policy_versions`,
`ai_invocations` and `agent_tool_receipts`.** 🔵 **Korea's local-representative requirement is a
delivery-model question, not a build question — flag it in the engagement's commercial terms, not
its architecture.**

### LATAM

🟢 **Demand: the richest adoption evidence of ANY region this pass — and it is institutional, not
anecdotal.** UNESCO IESALC surveyed `200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries:
**`87`% use `AI` in at least one area, `74`% in teaching and learning — and only `26`% have a formal
`AI` strategy.** Uptake is highest at private non-profit universities *(`84`%)*. TALIS `2024` puts
secondary teachers' `AI` use well above the OECD average of `36`%: **Brazil `56`%, Chile `55`%,
Colombia `53`%, Costa Rica `52`%**. UNESCO launched an **Observatory on `AI` in Education for Latin
America and the Caribbean** in April `2026`, at ECLAC headquarters in Santiago. The Digital Education
Council's `2026` LATAM survey covered `30 000`+ respondents at `29` institutions. Generative `AI`
*(ChatGPT, Copilot, Gemini)* is the default entry point, driven by free or low-cost access.

🟡 **Regulation is moving but not landed.** Brazil's **PL `2.338/2023`** passed the Senate in
December `2024` and sits in the Chamber of Deputies, so the text can still change. Chile's
government bill is in its first constitutional stage. Colombia relies on national policy
**CONPES `4144`** with a budget through `2030` rather than binding statute. UNESCO ran `AI`
regulation and ethics capacity courses for officials in Ecuador and Chile.

🔴 **Supply: `0` rows. On every pass this KB has run.** 🔵 **And the asymmetry is now the single
most actionable fact in this file: `87`% adoption against `26`% strategy, and no regional
open-source supply to build on.**

🟢 **The opportunity, specifically — and it is the clearest in this document:** `61` percentage
points of LATAM institutions are using `AI` without a formal strategy. 🟢 **That gap is a
governance-and-evidence engagement, not a model engagement**, and it can be delivered on permissive
components that already exist elsewhere: `civil-ai`'s versioned assessment policy *(`MIT`)*,
`tutor-mcp`'s `audit_events` and multi-tenancy for a ministry or university-system deployment
*(`MIT`)*, `dolos` for integrity at the calibrated `0.85` threshold *(`MIT`)*. 🔴 **What a studio
must supply itself: Spanish and Portuguese localisation.** 🔵 **No row on this shelf has a
Spanish or Portuguese message catalogue — the one localisation depth this KB has measured is Arabic,
in a `GPL-3.0` row. For a region with the highest measured adoption, that is the supply gap worth
naming to a client.**

### Global

🟢 **Cross-cutting and region-neutral:** `Article 50`-style synthetic-content marking is the
obligation most likely to apply wherever a studio delivers, and it is live in the EU now. The
permissive integrity component *(`dolos`, `MIT`)* is calibrated in this KB with numbers rather than
defaults — threshold `0.85`, an honest-work band topping out at `0.6720`, and a measured rule for
when template exclusion helps *(see [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md))*.
🔵 **`T308`: the cheapest globally reusable asset in education `AI` is not a model or a tutor — it
is the evidence layer: versioned policy, per-call receipts, an audit trail, and a defensible
integrity threshold. Three permissive rows now supply pieces of it, from three different regions,
and none of them is famous.**

## Pass 180 — 2026-10-13

🆕 🔴 **`ACTION LF` is DISCHARGED WITH LIMB `1` FIRED, and it corrects the single most
client-consequential date in this KB: the *"digital omnibus"* was ADOPTED.** 🟢 **Regulation (EU)
`2026/1744` — signed `8` July `2026`, published in the Official Journal `24` July `2026`, in force
`27` July `2026`.** 🔵 **p179 carried the omnibus as *"a live amendment … flagged rather than
relied on"*. It is law, and it has been law for `11` weeks.**

### 🔴 The regulatory calendar, corrected

| in force | jurisdiction | instrument | what it reaches in education |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Jan `2026`** | 🟢 South Korea | `AI` Basic Act | *"high-impact"* `AI`; education named explicitly beside healthcare, finance and employment |
| **`1` Mar `2026`** | 🟢 Vietnam | Law No. `134/2025/QH15` | education named for HIGH-RISK, with automated assessment and behavioural monitoring as the given examples |
| **`1` Jul `2026`** | 🟢 Ohio, USA | state mandate | first US state requiring EVERY district to adopt a formal `AI` use policy |
| **`2` Aug `2026`** | 🟢 European Union | `AI` Act — Article `50` transparency duties | 🆕 🟢 **UNCHANGED BY THE OMNIBUS AND ALREADY APPLYING** |
| **`2` Dec `2026`** | 🆕 🟡 European Union | `AI` Act — two NEW Article `5` prohibitions added by the omnibus | 🟡 phasing in; read before deploying behavioural or emotion-inferring classroom tooling |
| **`2` Dec `2027`** | 🆕 🔴 European Union | Annex III high-risk obligations — **DEFERRED from `2` Aug `2026`** | 🔴 education where a system determines access, evaluates learning outcomes, screens applicants or monitors candidates during examinations |
| **`2` Aug `2028`** | 🆕 🔴 European Union | Annex I embedded high-risk — **DEFERRED from `2` Aug `2027`** | high-risk `AI` inside regulated products |

🆕 🟢 **The distinction that decides what a studio builds this quarter, and it is the opposite of
the headline:** 🔴 **the high-risk duties moved; the TRANSPARENCY duties did not.** 🟢 **Article
`50` has applied since `2` August `2026` and the omnibus left it alone.** 🔵 **`T295`: *"the AI Act
was delayed"* is false as stated to an education client. The obligation that bites a
content-generating tutor TODAY — marking synthetic output — was never deferred, while the one that
bites an assessment engine moved to `2` December `2027`.**

🟡 **What remains UNRESOLVED, and is recorded rather than guessed:** 🟡 **whether the adopted text
kept the Commission's original CONDITION — Annex III duties contingent on harmonised standards
being available, *"at the latest"* `2` December `2027`.** 🔴 **One secondary source still describes
the deferral as conditional; one `20`-day-old source still describes the whole omnibus as *"in
negotiation"* and is simply stale.** 🔴 **This could not be settled on a primary channel:
`eur-lex.europa.eu` answered `403` to `curl` *(proxy `connect_rejected`)* and `ENOTFOUND` on the
second channel, as did `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`.** 🔵 **`gap 110` now holds on THREE
channels across three passes — the EU primary corpus is unreachable from this environment, and
every EU date in this KB is secondary-sourced by necessity, not by laziness.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **Regulation is state-level, fast-moving and the volume is now the story: `134` AI-in-education
bills across `31` states** in the `2026` session, concentrated on student-data privacy, classroom
use limits and curriculum.

- 🟢 **Named instruments:** California `AB 1159` *(would restrict training models on student
  data)*, Idaho `SB 1227` *(privacy protections for school AI tools)*, Oklahoma and Maryland
  *(human oversight required; AI barred from high-stakes decisions about students)*, Ohio
  *(district AI policy mandated, in force `1` July `2026`)*.
- 🟢 **Federal:** the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act *(`H.R. 8747`)* cleared House committee
  markup on `21` July, largely along party lines; it would let schools spend federal funds on AI
  curriculum.
- 🟡 **A counter-current worth naming to a client:** New York City runs a one-year moratorium on
  student-facing AI through `8`th grade, and UChicago Law pilots a device ban in core first-year
  classes from autumn `2026`. 🔵 **Procurement in this region must survive a reversal, not just an
  approval.**
- 🟢 **Opportunity:** 🟢 **the `31`-state patchwork is itself the product.** A deployment that can
  prove, per district, that a model was not trained on student data and that a human made every
  high-stakes call is sellable in every one of those states. 🔵 **`OpenOLAT`'s `Apache-2.0` grant
  plus a self-hosted `ollama` path (`13` files upstream) is the architecture that answers both
  without a licence conversation.**
- 🔴 **Supply gap:** 🔴 **North America contributes `0` permissive routes to this KB's shelf.** Its
  two major platforms — [`canvas-lms`](https://github.com/instructure/canvas-lms) *(`AGPL-3.0`)*
  and [`sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai) *(`ECL-2.0`, education-scoped patent
  grant)* — are both integrate-only.
- 🟡 Market share: one vendor estimate puts North America at `36%` of the global AI-in-education
  market. 🔵 Treat as directional.

### EMEA

🟢 **The binding framework is the `AI` Act as amended by Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`, and the
engagement-relevant split is above: Article `50` now, Annex III at `2` December `2027`.**

- 🟢 **EMEA is where this KB's permissive SUPPLY actually lives, and it is institution-held:**
  [`OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) *(`Apache-2.0`, Swiss-led consortium,
  `440` entities)*, [`Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis) *(`MIT`, TU München)*,
  [`dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) *(`MIT`, KU Leuven / Ghent)*,
  [`mentingo`](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) *(`MIT`, Poland)*,
  [`rosariosis`](https://github.com/francoisjacquet/rosariosis) *(`GPL`, France)*.
  🔵 **`4` of the `5` are permissive and `3` are university- or consortium-held.**
- 🟡 **Adoption is shallow where it is measured:** roughly `10%` of `450`+ surveyed schools and
  universities report formal AI-use guidelines, and the European AI-in-education market is put at
  about `$2.64` B for `2026` *(single low-authority source — directional only)*. Finland, Estonia
  and the Netherlands are named as K-12 integration leaders.
- 🟢 **The OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook argues for purpose-built educational AI over
  general-purpose tools, on durable-learning-gain grounds.** 🔵 **That is a procurement argument a
  studio can quote: it favours a composed, standards-speaking stack over a chatbot.**
- 🟢 **Opportunity:** 🟢 **an `Apache-2.0` route plus a `2` December `2027` Annex III clock is the
  cleanest engagement window in this KB.** Build the assessment path on
  `OpenOLAT`+`Artemis`+`dolos` now, ship Article `50` provenance marking from day one *(it is
  already due)*, and have the high-risk conformity documentation ready well inside the deferral.
- 🆕 🟢 **MEA — no longer a measured absence, on POLICY.** `gap 124` has stood for several passes
  as *"MEA returns nothing"*. 🟢 **It returns a great deal this pass:** the UAE made a nationwide
  K-12 AI curriculum MANDATORY by Cabinet decision, reaching close to a million pupils in
  government schools across seven curriculum strands with `1 000`+ specifically trained teachers
  *(private schools not yet in scope)*; Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Qatar all run advanced national
  AI-in-education strategies; regional platforms named are **Alef Education** and **Classera**;
  Nigeria has a National AI Strategy *(`2024`)* alongside its `NDPA`; Kenya has an AI Strategy
  *(`2025`)* and is in talks with OpenAI over an East African OpenAI Academy in Nairobi
  *(talks, not a commitment)*. A UNESCO-ICHEI report covers African higher education across
  `12` countries.
  🔴 **What MEA still returns is `0` open-source education repositories.** 🔵 **So `gap 124`
  SPLITS: closed on policy and platforms, open on supply.**
- 🔴 **The capacity asymmetry a client will not raise but should:** Kenya runs up to `68` students
  per teacher against the UAE's `2`. 🔵 **The same product cannot serve both; the UAE buys
  enrichment, Kenya buys teacher leverage.**

### APAC

🟢 **APAC holds both the earliest binding obligations and the most individual-held permissive
supply — and those are different countries.**

- 🟢 **Regulation:** South Korea's `AI` Basic Act *(January `2026`, education named as
  high-impact)*; Vietnam's Law No. `134/2025/QH15` *(`1` March `2026`, education high-risk with
  automated assessment and behavioural monitoring as examples)*. Japan runs a deliberately
  light-touch voluntary model; India's MeitY supervises under IT rules, also light-touch. China and
  Singapore have established AI-in-education policies.
- 🟡 **Sentiment is NOT uniform and it cuts against deployment in the Anglophone south:** the Ipsos
  Education Monitor `2026` finds support for BANNING AI in schools is markedly higher in Australia
  and New Zealand than in the Asian markets surveyed. 🔵 **An APAC engagement is at least two
  distinct sales motions.**
- 🟢 **Supply, measured from trees rather than from search:**
  [`OpenTutor`](https://github.com/zijinz456/OpenTutor) *(`MIT`, `34` tables, own auth — now
  ROUTE-grade)*, [`Bloom`](https://github.com/Li-Evan/Bloom) *(`MIT`, appliance)*,
  [`Study-Mate`](https://github.com/Miaotofu01/Study-Mate) *(`MIT`, multi-host adapter)*,
  [`universal-examprep-skill`](https://github.com/ZeKaiNie/universal-examprep-skill) *(`MIT`)*,
  [`openeducat_erp`](https://github.com/openeducat/openeducat_erp) *(`LGPL-3.0`, India)*,
  [`GibbonEdu/core`](https://github.com/GibbonEdu/core) *(`AGPL-3.0`, Hong Kong)*.
  🔵 **`4` permissive APAC rows, all individual-held, all Chinese-language-first.**
- 🟢 **Opportunity:** 🟢 **the region ships the intelligence and lacks the institution.**
  `OpenTutor` is the exception that shows the move: an individual-held `MIT` tutor that already has
  `users`, `auth_sessions`, `JWT` refresh and `alembic` migrations. 🔵 **Productising an APAC
  permissive row for an institutional buyer — SSO, tenancy, audit, a conformity file against Korea's
  and Vietnam's high-impact rules — is a defined, repeatable studio engagement.**
- 🟡 Vendors named in market reports: Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's, Squirrel AI; Baidu's
  ERNIE has entered Indonesian and Thai education through partnerships. One report puts APAC CAGR
  at `28.1%` *(`2026`–`2033`)*. 🔵 Vendor-sourced.

### LATAM

🟢 **The region's defining number is a GAP between use and governance, and it is the best-evidenced
regional statistic in this pass.**

- 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, across `200` institutions in `19` countries: `87%` use AI
  somewhere, and only `26%` have a formal AI strategy.** Adoption is driven by individual academics
  and students rather than by institutions.
- 🟢 **The Digital Education Council's LATAM faculty survey: `79%` of faculty use AI in teaching,
  but `88%` only at a minimal-to-moderate level**, from `7 319` faculty across `29` institutions.
  🔵 **Separate pages of the same source cite a total above `30 000` responses, so always state
  which sample a figure comes from.**
- 🟢 **Regulation:** Uruguay was the first Latin American state to sign the Council of Europe's AI
  framework convention *(`2025`)*; Peru has a risk-based, use-based approach; Colombia adopted
  national AI policy `CONPES 4144` *(February `2025`)*; Mexico has a federal AI bill *(`2024`)*
  proposing an oversight authority. 🔴 **None of these is education-SPECIFIC in the material
  available this pass.**
- 🟢 **Institutional players to engage through:** UNESCO's Regional Observatory on AI in Education
  *(launched April `2026`)* with CAF, CENIA *(Chile)*, CETIC.br *(Brazil)*, ECLAC, Fundación
  Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal *(Uruguay)*; UNESCO
  capacity-building for regulators in Ecuador and Chile; a pilot with Mexico's CONALEP and DGETI.
  ILIA `2025` names Ecuador, Costa Rica, the Dominican Republic and Guatemala as catching up
  fastest.
- 🟢 **Opportunity:** 🟢 **`87%` use against `26%` strategy is a governance engagement, not a
  tooling one.** The sellable artefact is an institutional AI strategy with the technical
  substrate under it — an `Apache-2.0` platform the institution owns outright, an auditable
  provenance trail, and a Spanish/Portuguese-first tutor layer. 🔵 **The Observatory's membership
  is the route to multi-institution scope rather than single-campus pilots.**
- 🔴 **Supply gap, stated plainly: LATAM contributes `0` rows to this KB's agent shelf and `0` to
  its foundation set.** 🔵 **Not one of `4` regional searches this pass named a LATAM-originated
  open-source education repository (`gap 141`).**

### Global

- 🔴 **The market-size range is still a factor of two for the same year and every source is
  vendor-published. No point estimate should be quoted to a client.** IMARC: `$6.4` B *(`2025`)*
  → `$79.6` B *(`2034`)*. A Grand-View-style estimate: `$8.3` B *(`2025`)* → `$57.2` B *(`2033`)*.
  The Business Research Company: `$7.52` B *(`2025`)* → `$10.6` B *(`2026`)*. 🔵 **Three sources,
  three `2025` figures, and one `2026` figure that is below another source's `2025` figure.**
- 🟡 Institutional maturity is rated `35`/`100` by one analysis — *"most institutions are still in
  pilot mode"*. 🟡 Student-usage claims *(`66%` `2024` → `92%` `2025`)* are vendor-compiled.
- 🟢 **The durable global trend across all four regions: adoption has outrun governance.** 🔵 **NA
  is legislating it (`134` bills), EMEA is regulating it (Annex III, `2` December `2027`), APAC is
  split by jurisdiction and by sentiment, LATAM has quantified the gap exactly (`87%`/`26%`), and
  MEA is mandating curriculum ahead of regulation. 🟢 **The product that sells into all five is the
  evidence layer, not the model.**

## Pass 179 — 2026-10-13

🆕 🟢 **The regulatory map is the finding this pass, and it is now a CALENDAR rather than a
sentiment: four jurisdictions have education-AI obligations that are already in force in 2026, and
they are in force on four different dates.** 🔵 **p178 established that APAC is the most productive
region for permissive education AI. p179 establishes that APAC is also where the binding
obligations landed FIRST — and the two facts are about different countries.**

| in force | jurisdiction | instrument | what it reaches in education |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Jan `2026`** | 🟢 South Korea | `AI` Basic Act | *"high-impact"* `AI`, education named explicitly alongside healthcare, finance and employment |
| **`1` Mar `2026`** | 🟢 Vietnam | Law No. `134/2025/QH15` on Artificial Intelligence | education named for HIGH-RISK systems, with automated assessment and behavioural monitoring given as examples |
| **`1` Jul `2026`** | 🟢 Ohio, USA | state mandate | 🔵 **first US state to require EVERY district to adopt a formal `AI` use policy** |
| **`2` Aug `2026`** | 🟢 European Union | `AI` Act — enforcement began; `AI` Office and national authorities now enforcing | education is Annex III HIGH-RISK where a system determines access, assesses outcomes or steers an educational path |

🟡 **The EU figure this KB has carried as permanently secondary since `ACTION KV` is unchanged and
is NOT re-litigated here:** `2` December `2027` for Annex III education obligations, corroborated
only on secondary channels because `6` of `6` EU primary hosts are unreachable from this
environment on two channels (`gap 110`). 🔵 **What p179 adds is that the enforcement MACHINERY
started on `2` August `2026` — a different fact from the Annex III date, and one that secondary
sources agree on.**

🟡 **A live amendment that would change the above, flagged rather than relied on:** the November
`2025` *"digital omnibus"* proposes making high-risk obligations CONDITIONAL on the availability of
harmonised technical standards. 🔴 **Status unverified from primary sources here. If adopted it
moves the EU education deadline from a date to a dependency, which is a materially different
engagement risk — and it is the single most important thing for a later pass to resolve
(`ACTION LF`).**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The market is in operational deployment, and the opportunity is COMPLIANCE INFRASTRUCTURE
rather than tutoring.**

- 🟢 **`134` bills related to `AI` in education were introduced across `31` states in `2026`**,
  concentrated on student-data privacy, classroom-use restrictions and curriculum.
- 🟢 **Named instruments:** California `AB 1159` would bar using student data to train `AI`
  models; Idaho `SB 1227` would require privacy protections for `AI` tools in schools; Oklahoma
  and Maryland require human oversight and bar `AI` from high-stakes decisions about students;
  Ohio requires a district policy by `1` July `2026`.
- 🔴 **And the counter-movement is real: New York City imposed a one-year moratorium on
  student-facing `AI` through eighth grade.** 🔵 **A K-8 student-facing pilot is not sellable in
  the largest US district this school year; a teacher-facing one is.**
- 🟡 **Federal:** the `K-12 AI` Literacy and Readiness Act of `2026` (`H.R. 8747`) was advanced by
  the House Education and Workforce Committee on `21` July `2026`, largely along party lines —
  it would let schools spend federal funds on `AI` literacy. 🔴 **Advanced from committee is not
  enacted.**
- 🟢 **The platform players are moving on procurement, not product:** Microsoft announced free
  Copilot and Teams for Education statewide for Washington classrooms from `2026`; North Carolina
  created an `AI` Leadership Council and an `AI` Accelerator with the state education department
  as a partner.
- 🟢 **Globant-shaped opportunity:** 🔵 **the oversight layer the statutes now REQUIRE and no
  vendor ships** — human-in-the-loop adjudication, decision logging, and a district-policy
  conformance report. `Artemis`'s `528` write endpoints are where such a log would attach, and
  `dolos`'s measured `0.3066` separation is the kind of number a district policy can actually
  cite.
- 🔴 **Declared gap:** `ACTION KK` (p176) established that `0` of `6` *"North America"* artefacts
  in this KB name a COUNTRY. 🔴 **That is unchanged: every item above is United States. Canada and
  Mexico are unmeasured in this file, and "North America" here should be read as "US" until a
  pass fixes it (`gap 119`).**

### EMEA

🟢 **The opportunity is the conformance artefact, and the clock is running.**

- 🟢 **Enforcement began `2` August `2026`:** the `AI` Office and national authorities are now
  enforcing the `AI` Act, with education in Annex III high-risk where a system determines access,
  assesses outcomes or steers a path. 🔵 **Automated essay grading, adaptive placement, proctoring
  and dropout prediction are the named shapes — and all four are products Globant clients already
  run.**
- 🟡 **Beyond the EU:** the Council of Europe's Framework Convention on `AI` covers design,
  development and use; UNESCO and the OECD supply the non-binding guidance that national
  ministries actually cite.
- 🟡 **Adoption, vendor-sourced and low-confidence:** a European `AI`-in-education market of
  `$2.64` B in `2026` growing at `31.9`% CAGR to `$8.0` B by `2030`, with Finland, Estonia and the
  Netherlands leading K-12 integration; around `70`% of institutions in Europe and North America
  have or are developing `AI` guidance. 🔴 **Single vendor-style sources. Treat as direction, not
  size.**
- 🟢 **Public money is in teacher workflow:** the UK government invested £`4` M in `AI` tools for
  lesson planning and homework marking. 🔵 **Teacher-facing, not student-facing — the same shape
  the NYC moratorium forces in North America, arrived at for a different reason.**
- 🟢 **Globant-shaped opportunity, and it is the strongest regional fit in this KB:**
  🔵 **an Annex III conformance package built on institution-held code.** `ls1intum/Artemis` is
  `MIT`, held by the Technical University of Munich, speaks `LTI 1.3` with grade passback, and
  ADJUDICATES by design — so the high-risk classification applies to it and the evidence trail
  can be built INSIDE it. `ls1intum/Pyris` (`MIT`, `1 089` B) is the sovereignty limb, with
  `ollama` and `weaviate` topics. 🔴 **And the risk line this KB will keep repeating: `Pyris` is a
  `12`★ microservice.**

### APAC

🟢 **The region has both the earliest binding law and the most permissive supply, and they are not
in the same countries.**

- 🟢 **Korea's `AI` Basic Act (Jan `2026`) and Vietnam's Law `134/2025/QH15` (`1` Mar `2026`) both
  name education as high-impact / high-risk** — Vietnam explicitly citing automated assessment and
  behavioural monitoring. 🔵 **Two national statutes already in force, both ahead of the EU's
  Annex III date.**
- 🟡 **Singapore leads on governance** with adopted generative-`AI` regulation, a recognised
  governance research centre and a dedicated `AI` policy body. 🟡 **Japan took the opposite route:
  light-touch, voluntary.** 🔵 **So a regional rollout cannot carry one compliance posture — the
  jurisdictional spread inside APAC is wider than the gap between the EU and the US.**
- 🟡 **UNESCO records uneven readiness:** China and Singapore have established `AI`-in-education
  policies while others are still meeting basic educational needs. Squirrel AI is named as a
  significant China-based player; Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's are named as the
  commercial set *(market-research sourced, low confidence)*.
- 🟢 **Sentiment is permissive:** the Ipsos Education Monitor `2026` finds LOWER support for
  banning `AI` in schools across the Asian markets examined, even where support for restricting
  children's social media is high. 🔵 **The regulatory risk here is compliance cost, not political
  veto — the inverse of New York City.**
- 🟢 **Supply, and this is p178's measurement standing unchanged:** `4` of the `5` new `MIT` rows
  above `170`★ were Chinese-language projects. 🟢 **p179 read the two largest and they are both
  individually held** — `Study-Mate` by *"Cattofu"*, `Bloom` by *"Li Zhengping"*.
  🔵 **The shape thesis holds on the deepest evidence yet: the permissive education supply here is
  excellent and has no institution behind it.**
- 🟢 **Globant-shaped opportunity:** 🔵 **put an institution behind APAC-originated permissive
  code.** `Study-Mate` is a genuinely sophisticated multi-host adapter with `0` databases and `0`
  auth; `Bloom` is a working appliance with no user. 🟢 **Both are one layer away from a cohort
  product, and that layer — tenancy, identity, enrolment, audit — is exactly what a studio
  supplies.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The region's defining number is the gap between USE and GOVERNANCE, and that gap is the
offer.**

- 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, `200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries:
  `87`% use `AI` in at least one area and only `26`% have a formal `AI` strategy.**
  🔵 **A `61`-point gap, and adoption is driven by individual academics, researchers and students
  rather than institutional plans.**
- 🟢 **Digital Education Council `2026` LATAM survey: `79`% of faculty use `AI` in teaching —
  `18` points above the global `2025` figure — while `88`% report only *"minimal"* to
  *"moderate"* engagement.** 🔵 **Broad, shallow, ungoverned: three conditions that each imply the
  same first engagement.**
- 🟡 **Regulation is policy-first rather than statute-first:** Uruguay was the first Latin
  American country to sign the Council of Europe's Framework Convention (`2025`); Mexico's bills
  are converging on an unacceptable / high / lower risk taxonomy with a proposed federal oversight
  authority; Colombia took the policy route via `CONPES 4144` (February `2025`).
  🔴 **No LATAM country in these sources has an education-specific `AI` statute in force, unlike
  Korea and Vietnam.**
- 🟢 **The institutional scaffolding is NEW and it is a channel:** UNESCO launched the Observatory
  on `AI` in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean on `14` April `2026` in Santiago, with
  CAF, CENIA (Chile), CETIC.br, ECLAC, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo
  and Fundación Ceibal as partners; Tec de Monterrey signed its own `AI`-in-education agreement
  with UNESCO in March `2026`.
- 🔴 **School-level `AI` education remains the exception in the region**, per the Latin American
  `AI` Index — the adoption above is higher education, and K-12 should not be inferred from it.
- 🟢 **Globant-shaped opportunity, and it is the most natural fit in this file:**
  🔵 **institutional `AI` strategy as a product, delivered through the Observatory's partner
  network.** The `87`/`26` split means the buyer is not purchasing a tutor — they are purchasing
  the governance, the academic-integrity position and the evaluation framework that makes the use
  already happening defensible. 🟢 **`dolos` is the concrete anchor: `MIT`, `Apache-2.0`-adjacent
  tooling, now MEASURED at `0.3066` separation with a usable threshold near `0.85`, and it
  refuses to adjudicate — which is exactly the posture a region with no statute and heavy faculty
  discretion needs.**

### Global

- 🟡 **Market size is contested and every figure is vendor-sourced:** `$10.6` B in `2026` → `$42.48` B
  by `2030` (Research and Markets); `$12.3` B by `2026` (HolonIQ, via a vendor blog); ~`$6` B in
  `2024` → ~`$32` B by `2030` (K-12 Academics); North America at `36`% of global share.
  🔴 **These disagree by a factor of two on the same year. This KB records them as a RANGE and
  should not quote a point estimate to a client.**
- 🟢 **The qualitative convergence is strong and consistent across independent sources:** the shift
  away from general-purpose chatbots toward education-specific platforms; agentic multi-step work
  (plan a teaching sequence, draft the assessment, leave the teacher to approve); governance and
  evaluation structures being built before tools are chosen (1EdTech); skills-based credentialing;
  and teacher-first deployment, with TIME SAVED PER WEEK emerging as the predictor of whether a
  school keeps using a tool.
- 🔵 **That last metric is the one to carry into a proposal**, because it is the one a client can
  measure in the first month and it is the one the sources agree on.

- 🔴 **MEA remains a MEASURED ABSENCE and was not re-probed:** `ACTION JY` (p175) ran `16`
  formulations on `2` channels for MEA-originated education repositories and found `0` qualifying
  (`gap 124`). 🔵 **It is not re-registered each pass by design; it is recorded as measured.**
- 🔴 **The supply-side regional evidence in this pass is ZERO.** Four regional searches returned
  policy, adoption surveys and market sizing — 🔴 **not one named an open-source education `AI`
  repository originating in its region.** 🔵 **Regional placement of SUPPLY remains reachable only
  through the tag census (p178's APAC finding), and prose search does not produce it. That is an
  informed gap, not coverage.**
- 🔴 **"North America" in this file means the United States** (`gap 119`, carried).

🆕 🟢 **The regional claim this KB has been making by impression is now a measurement: APAC is the

institution-held platform verified this pass is a German university's
([`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)), which is why an engagement that needs an

🔴 **SOURCE GRADE, stated before the numbers: every market and policy figure below is search-layer
SECONDARY.** `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, `ec.europa.eu`,

### 🆕 🔴 The one global number this pass will not average

🔴 **Two market sizings reached this pass disagree by more than a factor of two for overlapping

organisations report using generative AI while most have no policy; `92`% of students report use;
and in the US and Canada faculty INTENT to use AI fell `9` points, `76`% → `67`%.
🔵 **The gap between `86`% usage and near-absent policy is the engagement, in every region.**

## Opportunities by region

### 🟢 North America

🔵 **The market here is made of STATUTES, not platforms, and the statutes are at district level.**
🟢 **United States, `2026` session, secondary but consistent across two independent trackers:**
**`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states.** The three themes are student-data privacy,
classroom restriction, and curriculum.
- 🔴 **California `AB 1159` bars using student data to TRAIN models** — a procurement clause, not a
  guideline. Any tutoring or grading engagement in California needs a no-training contractual term
  and a technical control that evidences it.
- 🔴 **Idaho `SB 1227`** requires privacy protections for school AI tools.
- 🟢 **Maryland, Oklahoma and Virginia set HUMAN-OVERSIGHT or policy requirements**; Maryland is one
  of four states requiring BOTH state guidance and mandatory district-level policy adoption.
  🔵 **This is where `dolos`'s *"returns no verdict"* property is worth money: a non-adjudicating
  tool satisfies an oversight statute by construction** *(and `Artemis`, which adjudicates, is the
  row that needs the human-review workflow built around it)*.
- 🟡 **Ohio has mandated written district AI policies by a July `2026` deadline** — a date that has
  passed, so the market is remediation, not preparation.
- 🟡 **Scale precedents exist:** Utah announced statewide K-12 deployment of Google Gemini tools
  *(`3` June `2026`)*; Illinois published a `409`-page K-12 AI guidance document in July.
  🔵 **A `409`-page guidance document is a compliance-mapping engagement on its own.**

🟢 **Canada, and the shape is different enough to price differently:** a national AI strategy
*(`4` June `2026`)* that relies on targeted laws rather than one framework; **no federal AI
statute** — the `2025` AIDA bill died and as of `27` September `2026` the responsible minister had
named neither bill nor timeline; **provinces moving first** — Manitoba's public-sector AI governance
act received Royal Assent `1` June `2026` and **covers universities and school divisions**, with no
regulations made as of September. 🔴 **The AI-literacy limb is reported as equipping `3 000` of
roughly `420 000` public educators** — a `0.7`% reach. 🔵 **Teacher-capability building is an open
market in Canada because the public programme is, on these numbers, a pilot.**
🔴 **DECLARED GAP: `0` Canada-originated open-source education repositories surfaced in either
census.** Canada appears this pass as policy only. 🔵 **So *"North America"* in the SUPPLY tables
remains United States evidence under a continental heading — `canvasapi` (UCF) and `Anubis` (an
individual, NYU-adjacent) — and that is a second consecutive pass of the same honest limitation.**

### 🟢 EMEA

🔵 **EMEA is the only region where this KB can route an institution-held platform, and the only one
where the regulation is a SEQUENCE with dates attached.**
🟢 **The supply, all verified or licence-read in this KB:**
[`ls1intum/Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis) *(TUM, Germany — `MIT`, verified past its
licence this pass, `818`★)*, [`ls1intum/Pyris`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Pyris) *(the same
org's LLM microservice, `12`★)*, [`dodona-edu/dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos)
*(Ghent University, Belgium — `MIT`, verified p177)*,
[`Selleo/mentingo`](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) *(Poland — `MIT`, the only row declaring
`scorm` and `xapi`)*. 🔵 **Three countries, three institutions, one commercial vendor. That is a
referenceable regional stack, and p178 is the first pass that can say so from code reads.**
🔴 **The regulatory sequence, secondary and corroborated across independent channels this pass:**
Article `50` transparency **in force** *(`2` August `2026`)*; Article `50(2)` marking of synthetic
content at **`2` December `2026`** — `50` days out; **Annex III stand-alone high-risk, which is where
education actually sits, moved to `2` December `2027`** by the Digital Omnibus, reported as `+16`
months for schools, universities and EdTech vendors; Annex I embedded at `2` August `2028`.
🔴 **A conflict recorded UNRESOLVED and no longer re-derivable: `50(2)` is given as both `2`
December `2026` and `2` February `2027`, and the Omnibus's operative status is reported both as in
force and as awaiting adoption.** 🟢 **`ACTION KV` settles the method rather than the date: no
primary channel is reachable, so this figure is PERMANENTLY SECONDARY in this KB. Sequence the work,
do not bet a delivery date on the day.**
🟢 **What the classification actually names as high-risk, consistent across sources:** admissions
screening, automated grading, placement, and exam proctoring. 🟡 **A tutoring chatbot that does not
grade is likely NOT high-risk but still carries transparency duties.** 🔵 **Plagiarism detection is
reported as *"debatable"* — and this KB has the measurement that settles it for one product:
`dolos` returns `falsePositive 0`, `confidence 0`, and no pass/fail anywhere in `513` files.**
🔴 **And the prohibition that is NOT a future duty: emotion recognition of students and staff is
banned under Article `5` — present tense.** 🟢 **Measured consequence, from this pass's read:
`Artemis` has `proctor` `0`, `webcam` `0`, `emotion` `0` and `face` `2`; what it ships instead is
`ExamSession` and `SuspiciousExamSessions`, i.e. session-level anomaly detection. It clears the
Article `5` bar architecturally, which is a harder bar than Annex III and cannot be retrofitted.**
🔴 **DECLARED GAP, second consecutive pass: `0` named EMEA EdTech VENDORS were recoverable.** The
sweep returned compliance consultancies and policy explainers. 🔵 **The region's recoverable
*"vendors"* in this KB are universities. Treat that as the finding it is: in EMEA the buyer and the
builder are often the same institution, which changes who the engagement is sold to.**

### 🟢 APAC

🔵 **APAC is where the supply is, and the regulation is a patchwork that can be sequenced per
jurisdiction rather than per region.**
🟢 **Supply, measured this pass:** `4` of the `5` new `MIT` rows above `170`★ are Chinese-language
— `Study-Mate` `925`★ *(created `2026-09-17`; `26` days to `925`★)*,
`universal-examprep-skill` `304`★ *(declares `anti-hallucination` and page-citation provenance)*,
`kaogong-skill` `173`★ *(civil-service exam prep)* — plus `taiwan-exam` `18`★ and
`fakao-ai-exam-engine` `10`★ already held, `Echo-Loop` *(`AGPL-3.0`)*, `showlab/Code2Video`, and
`uccser/cs-field-guide` *(University of Canterbury, New Zealand — the region's one holder-grade
institutional row)*.
🟢 **Policy and procurement signals, secondary:**
- 🟢 **India — the largest curriculum mandate in the sweep: AI and computational thinking become
  MANDATORY from Class `3` across government and private schools from `2026`–`27`**, alongside a
  `₹500` crore centre of excellence and the `₹10 372` crore IndiaAI Mission. 🔵 **A Class-`3`
  mandate is a teacher-training and content-localisation programme at national scale, and it has a
  date.**
- 🟢 **Singapore — teacher AI training offered at ALL levels by `2026`**; IMDA issued an **agentic
  AI framework on `22` January `2026`** on top of its Model AI Governance Framework; no binding AI
  statute, so data-protection law is what binds. Reported first in the region for AI diffusion among
  working-age adults at `60.9`%. 🔵 **Voluntary frameworks plus a sophisticated buyer: the market is
  implementation quality, not compliance.**
- 🔴 **China — the most binding rules in the region** *(algorithm filing, deep synthesis,
  generative-AI registration and content controls)*, and reported as leading on SCALE of AI
  education. 🔵 **Registration obligations make a self-hosted, auditable stack the default
  architecture, not a preference.**
- 🟡 **Japan — the AI Promotion Act is innovation-first; the Cabinet adopted a new AI Basic Plan on
  `14` July `2026`.** 🔴 **The sweep returned little on Japan's school-level AI policy
  specifically — a declared gap, not an absence of activity.**
- 🟢 **Vietnam's high-risk list names education outright** *(carried from p177)* — still the only
  statute outside the EU in this KB that classes education AI as high-risk by name.
- 🟡 **Sovereign models to compose against rather than assume OpenAI:** Sarvam AI *(India)*,
  SEA-LION *(Singapore)*, NTT Sarashina *(Japan)*.

### 🟢 LATAM

🔵 **The asymmetry here is the thesis, and both halves of it are now measured: the DEMAND evidence
is the best-sourced in this KB and the SUPPLY evidence is empty on two independent instruments.**
🟢 **Demand, from UNESCO IESALC's higher-education study — `200` institutions across `19`
countries:** **`87`% use AI in at least one area**, **`74`% in teaching and learning**, and
**only `26`% have a formal AI strategy.** Uptake splits by institution type: private non-profit
`84`%, public `68`%, private for-profit `52`%.
🟢 **Schools, from OECD TALIS:** secondary teachers in **Brazil, Chile, Colombia and Costa Rica**
all above the OECD average of `36`%; **Uruguay reports `75`% of public-school teachers** using these
tools *(Ceibal, `2026`)*.
🔵 **`87`% adoption against `26`% strategy is a governance engagement with a number attached, and it
is the single most quotable statistic this KB holds for any region.**
🟢 **Regulation — a policy route, not a statute route:**
- 🟡 **Brazil `PL 2.338/2023`** passed the Senate in December `2024` and sits in the Chamber of
  Deputies, so the text can still change.
- 🟡 **Chile** has a government-sponsored risk-based bill in its first constitutional stage.
- 🟢 **Colombia took the policy path first: `CONPES 4144`** *(February `2025`)*, government-wide
  actions with a budget through `2030`. 🔵 **A funded national policy is a procurement calendar.**
- 🟢 **Institutional anchor: UNESCO launched an Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education
  for Latin America and the Caribbean on `14` April `2026`, at ECLAC headquarters in Santiago.**
  🔵 **A named convening body with a launch date is the partner a studio can actually approach.**
- 🟢 **Named actors:** UNESCO IESALC, ECLAC, **Ceibal** *(Uruguay — the region's best-instrumented
  public programme)*, FLACSO Ecuador.
🔴 **Supply: EMPTY, twelfth consecutive pass, and now measured in Spanish.** A language-native query
returns `128` repositories whose head is link vaults, reading lists and personal profiles; the only
education-AI-shaped rows are `2`★ and `1`★
*(details in [`agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md))*.
🔵 **The engagement consequence has not changed and is now better evidenced: in LATAM a studio does
not integrate local open source, it LOCALISES someone else's.** 🟢 **`Kolibri` (`MIT`, offline-first)
remains the best-fitting route for low-connectivity deployments, and `Artemis` + `Moodle` over
`LTI 1.3` is the first institutional topology this KB can offer a LATAM university with every link
code-verified.**

### 🟢 Global

🟢 **Cross-region, and the one trend that is the same everywhere: buyers are moving from generic
assistants to education-specific products.** 🔴 **Only `29`% of students believe their instructors
are equipped to guide them on AI use** — while `92`% of students already use it.
🔵 **Capability building is the largest undifferentiated opportunity in every region, and unlike
platform work it needs no licence review.**
🆕 🔴 **And the structural finding this pass adds to the market map: the permissive supply's head is
AGENT SKILL PACKS, not products.** At least `6` of the top `12` on `topic:ai-tutor license:mit`
declare `agent-skills`, `claude-code`, `codex` or equivalent. 🔵 **A skill pack cannot be bought as a
platform — it is a prompt asset that composes with an agent runtime the client already owns.
Two exceptions carry a deployment surface and are worth reading next: `Bloom` *(`fastapi` + `react`,
self-hostable)* and `OpenTutor` *(local-first, `10+` providers)* — both pre-registered as
`ACTION KX`.**

## Opportunities by region

### Global

🟢 **The permissive supply is exactly known and it is not growing:**
`topic:ai-education license:mit pushed:>=2026-04-16` = **`135`**, the **fourth consecutive** exact
`135`; the wide frame `topic:education license:mit pushed:>=2026-04-16` = **`8 199`**, `+1` in a day
for the second day running. 🔵 **The tag's head is UNCHANGED row for row from p176, so the vertical's
visible supply is stable rather than merely re-sampled.**
🟡 **Market sizing stays the weakest evidence in this file and p177 does not improve it.** Sources
on AI-in-education for `2026` span **`$6.4`B**, **`$8.3`B** and **`$10.6`B** with `2033`–`2034`
forecasts between **`$57.2`B** and **`$79.6`B** — vendor and aggregator blogs, methodology
undisclosed. 🔵 **Quote the spread or quote nothing; a single figure from this set is a guess
wearing a decimal point.**
🟢 **The opportunity that does not depend on a market figure:** `2` of `22` routing-table rows have
been read past their licence. 🔵 **Verification, not discovery, is the scarce good — and it is
billable.**

### North America

🟢 **The best-evidenced regulatory picture of any region this pass, and it is overwhelmingly
state-level rather than federal.**
- 🟢 **`134` bills related to AI in education across `31` states** this legislative session; more
  than `30` states already publish AI guidance documents.
- 🟢 **Ohio is the sharpest single datum: every K-12 district was required to adopt a formal AI use
  policy by `1` July `2026`.** 🔵 **A mandate to HAVE a policy is a procurement event — districts
  that must publish one need help writing and operationalising it.**
- 🟢 **Oklahoma and Maryland require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about
  students.** 🔵 **This is the single most commercially useful line in the region, because it names
  the shape of tool that is purchasable: `dolos` returns no verdict and therefore clears it, while
  any *"AI decides"* integrity product does not** ([`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)).
- 🟢 **California AB `1159` bars using student data to train AI models**; Idaho SB `1227` is
  named alongside it on student-data privacy.
- 🟡 **Federal: the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of `2026` (H.R. `8747`) advanced out of the
  House Education and Workforce Committee on `21` July `2026`**, largely along party lines. 🔴 **Not
  law. Do not plan funding against it.**
- 🔴 **Restriction is a real counter-current, not noise: New York City has a one-year moratorium on
  student-facing AI through eighth grade**, and UChicago Law pilots device bans in core first-year
  classes from autumn `2026`.
- 🟡 **Adoption: `60%` of US K-12 teachers used AI tools in the `2024-2025` school year, `32%` at
  least weekly**; the region holds roughly **`36%`–`37.5%`** of the global market. 🟡 **Aggregator
  figures — directionally safe, not citable as research.**
- 🔴 **DECLARED GAP: `0` Canada-specific results returned.** 🔵 **This region's evidence is United
  States evidence under a continental heading, which is precisely what `ACTION KK` exists to refuse.
  A Canadian engagement is NOT served by this block.**
- 🟢 **Repository placement:** `canvasapi` (University of Central Florida) routes here, at
  subnational grade; `Anubis` is description-grade only.

### EMEA

- 🟢 **The EU AI Act is the binding frame and education is explicitly inside it:** AI used in
  educational and vocational training is **high-risk** where it affects access, progression or
  assessment — admission decisions, student evaluation, exam scoring — which triggers a conformity
  assessment before the system may be placed on the market.
- 🆕 🟢 **But the clock moved, and this is the finding a client will care about most:** Annex III
  stand-alone high-risk duties now apply from **`2` December `2027`**, Annex I from **`2` August
  `2028`**. 🔵 **Education's high-risk obligations are `14` months further out than the headlines
  imply, while the transparency duty is `50` days out. Sequence the work accordingly: marking and
  disclosure now, conformity assessment on a `2027` roadmap.**
- 🔴 **`10%` of `450`+ surveyed schools and universities have established formal AI-use
  guidelines.** 🔵 **The mirror image of Ohio: no mandate, and almost no policy. The governance gap
  is the opening, and it is larger here than in North America.**
- 🟡 **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands lead K-12 AI integration**; the UK is named as a public
  investor in AI education tooling. European AI-in-education market put at **`$2.64`B for `2026`**
  🟡 *(single aggregator — weak)*.
- 🟢 **The OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook recommends moving beyond general-purpose AI
  tools toward purpose-built educational AI.** 🔵 **That is an institutional buyer being told to stop
  buying chat wrappers — which is the brief for a studio that can compose permissive components.**
- 🔴 **DECLARED GAP: no EMEA vendor or competitive landscape was recoverable.** The sweep returned
  policy bodies — UNESCO, the OECD, national governments, the EU AI Office as enforcer — and `0`
  named EdTech vendors. 🔵 **Written down rather than left blank.**
- 🟢 **Repository placement is this region's genuine strength: `3` holder-grade rows** —
  `mentingo` (Selleo), `Artemis` (TUM Applied Education Technologies) and 🆕 **`dolos` (Ghent
  University), now the only code-verified assessment-integrity route in this KB.**

### APAC

🆕 🟢 **The gap p176 declared is CLOSED. `6` jurisdictions with instruments and dates:**

| jurisdiction | instrument | date | education-specific? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **South Korea** | AI Basic Act | 🟢 **in force `22` January `2026`**; MSIT signals `2026` a pilot year with a `1`-year grace period on penalties | 🟡 not education-specific |
| 🔴 **Vietnam** | high-risk list *(reported as the Digital Technology Industry Law)* | 🟡 **effective `1` March `2026`** *(names and dates vary across sources)* | 🔴 **YES — education named among `6` sectors: automated assessment and behavioural monitoring** |
| 🟡 **Taiwan** | AI Basic Act | 🟡 passed December `2025` | 🟡 not education-specific |
| 🔴 **China** | mandatory registration + content labelling | 🟢 labelling live `1` September `2025`, broader than the EU's (`ACTION KG`, p175) | 🟡 general, but it reaches generated teaching material |

🟢 **Named vendors, which this KB had none of for the region:** Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and
Byju's. 🟡 **From a single market report — treat as a market map, not a share table.**
🟡 **China, India and Japan are described as dominating regional AI-in-education demand; India is
the fastest-growing APAC AI market at a `38.9%` CAGR.**
🟢 **Repository placement is strong and getting stronger — this is where the permissive supply
actually originates:** `cs-field-guide` (University of Canterbury, New Zealand, holder-grade),

`taiwan-exam` and `fakao-ai-exam-engine`, `Code2Video` (description-grade).
🔵 **The regulatory and the repository evidence now point the same way for the first time: APAC is
both the strictest education-AI regime outside the EU and the largest source of permissive
education repositories in this KB.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The best-sourced DEMAND evidence in this entire KB, and it is institutional rather than
vendor — UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, across `200` institutions in `19` countries:**
- 🟢 **`87%` use AI in at least one area; `74%` in teaching and learning.**
- 🔴 **Only `26%` have a formal AI strategy.** 🔵 **The adoption-governance scissors, measured: this
  is the clearest procurement opening in any region in this file.**
- 🟡 **Uptake is uneven by sector:** private non-profit universities `84%`, public `68%`, private
  for-profit `52%`.
- 🟢 **K-12, from TALIS `2024`: `56%` of Brazilian secondary teachers used AI in the prior year
  against an OECD average of `36%`.** 🟢 **Uruguay's Ceibal reports `75%` of public-school teachers
  using AI tools** — 🔵 **a national programme with a measured number, which is the strongest
  single operator datum this region has.**
- 🟢 **UNESCO launched a regional Observatory on AI in Education at ECLAC in Santiago, April
  `2026`**, and runs AI ethics and regulation training for officials in Ecuador and Chile.
- 🟡 **Regulation is in progress, not in force:** Brazil's PL `2.338/2023` passed the Senate in
  December `2024` and sits in the Chamber of Deputies — 🔴 **its text can still change**; Chile has
  a government-sponsored risk-based bill; Colombia adopted national AI policy CONPES `4144` in
  February `2025`, running to `2030`.
- 🔴 **ELEVENTH consecutive pass with `0` LATAM-originated open-source education repository.**
  `0` of the `135`-row tag's top `30` place here, on top of p175's `0` of `100`. 🔵 **The asymmetry
  is now this KB's most durable finding about the region: the demand evidence is the best it holds
  and the permissive supply evidence is empty. A LATAM engagement imports its components and the
  value added is integration, Spanish/Portuguese localisation and governance — not local
  open-source leverage.**
- 🟢 **The component that does travel: `Kolibri` (`MIT`, offline-first) remains the correct spine
  for low-connectivity deployments, and `26%`-strategy institutions need the governance layer more

## Pass 176 — 2026-10-13

🆕 🟢 **The two population figures are re-counted on the one-request channel, same window, and the
narrow one reproduces for the THIRD time:** `topic:education license:mit pushed:>=2026-04-16` =
**`8 198`** *(p175: `8 197`, one day earlier — `+1`)*; `topic:ai-education`, same filters =
**`135`**, identical at p173, p175 and p176. 🔵 **A figure reproduced three times on two different
channels is this KB's only exactly-known market size, and it is a repository count, not a revenue.**
🆕 🔴 **`gap 119` is SETTLED after four carries, and not in the direction the action expected.**
`ACTION KK` asked the artefact of every *"North America"* row which COUNTRY it names.
🔴 **`0` of `6` name a country. `2` name a subnational place that fixes it (Florida, Sacramento).
`4` name only an organisation whose address is nowhere in the artefact.** 🔵 **The continental
heading is not a geography error — it is the strongest precision the evidence supports for two
thirds of the rows (`T274`).**
🆕 🟡 **One datum cuts the other way and is worth more than the heading argument:** `canvasapi`'s
README names the **University of British Columbia** as a documented operator of the library.
🔵 **That is the first Canadian evidence in this KB — at OPERATOR grade, not holder grade. The
holder is still Florida.** 🟢 **So *"North America"* finally has a non-US datum, and it is about who
RUNS the software rather than who wrote it.**
🔴 **`T267` applies in full and p176 re-declares it rather than rediscovering it: every regulatory
and market figure below is `search-summary` tier.** `eur-lex.europa.eu` and every other legal-text
host are NXDOMAIN in this container; `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` are the only hosts
that resolve. 🔵 **Nothing below is a primary-source read, and no deck should imply it is.**

## Opportunities by region

### Global

🟢 **State the size with the tag attached or do not state it. `8 198` repositories tag themselves
`education`, carry an `MIT` licence and were pushed inside `180` days** — exact, one request,
reproduced against p175's `8 197` the day before. 🔴 **`135` tag themselves `ai-education` on the
same filters, a ratio of `60.7×`, and that narrower tag places `0` rows in North America
(`T266`).**
🟢 **The commercially useful consequence for any engagement, in one line:** the permissive education
layer that exists is LMS, interop, credentials and now a Canvas rostering client; the permissive
layer that does not exist is a system of record. 🔴 **Fourteenth consecutive pass with no
`MIT`/`Apache` education ERP or institutional-scale permissive SIS.**
🆕 🔴 **And a channel precision measurement a buyer should know about: a keyword search for
education agents does not return education.** `tutor OR tutoring OR grading agent
license:apache-2.0 pushed:>=2026-08-01 stars:>100` returns `1 684` repositories, and **`0` of its
top `25` by stars are education-vertical** — they are coding agents, RAG engines and agent
runtimes. 🔵 **The tag is the only usable sampling frame; keyword reach is noise at this star level.**

### North America

🟢 **Market, `search-summary` tier and the figures do not reconcile with each other:** one
geography report puts the region at **`~US$951M` (2024) rising to `~US$2.3B` by 2029 at `15.9%`
CAGR**; a separate roundup claims **`36%` of global share** and cites no primary source for it.
🔵 **Carry the first; do not quote the second.**
🟢 **Demand signals with dates and named parties, which is what a brief can use:** **OpenAI** ran a
country-level education programme with **`8` national partners** in Q1 `2026`; **Carnegie Mellon**
and the **Gates Foundation** put **`US$55M`** into AI courseware aimed at gateway college courses.
🔴 **Institutional readiness is the gap, and it is the sales opening:** reporting puts **`10%` of
institutions with formal AI guidelines** and **`71%` of US teachers without AI training**, against
student use reported at **`92%`**. 🔵 **A governance-and-training engagement is better supported by
this evidence than a tooling engagement.**
🔴 **Regulation is fragmented by design:** no education-specific federal instrument, with
state-level requirements appearing piecemeal — **Colorado** and **Texas** are the two named.
🔴 **NOT ESTABLISHED: p176 did not reach a single primary policy document** — no federal guidance,
no state statute text, no district rule. The regulatory sentences above are secondary coverage
(`T267`).
🟢 **Repository layer, from `ACTION KK`:** `5` rows place here — `ucfopen/canvasapi` (University of
Central Florida, subnational grade), `opensacorg/learning-blocks` (Code for Sacramento, subnational
grade), `AnubisLMS/Anubis` (*"NYU"*, no country in the artefact), `learningequality/kolibri`
(*"Learning Equality"*, no country), and Microsoft's two courseware repositories (*"Microsoft
Corporation"*, no country). 🟡 **Canada appears once, at operator grade, in `canvasapi`'s README.
Mexico appears `0` times anywhere.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Market, `search-summary` tier, two reports that define the market differently and therefore do
not reconcile:** Europe AI-in-education **`~US$512.6M` (2024) → `~US$1.33B` by 2029**; Europe
AI-for-kids **`~US$289M` (2024) → `~US$649M` by 2034**. 🔵 **Use one or the other, never the sum.**
🟢 **Leading adopters named: the UK, Germany, France and Scandinavia.** Vendors named in the
AI-for-kids segment: **LEGO Education**, **Oxford University Press** and **Seppo** (Finland), all
described as carrying **GDPR-K**-compliant portfolios — which is a procurement fact, not a
marketing one.
🟢 **Policy is the densest of any region and it is dated:** the **EU AI Act** is in force, with the
**AI Continent Action Plan** adopted and the **Apply AI Strategy** underway; the **Commission
updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education in June**; the **Council of Europe** runs a
standing AI-and-education expert group and held a second working conference on the **regulatory
dimensions of AI in education in October**. 🔴 **The UK diverges from the EU in posture:** its first
**AI Adoption Summit** committed **`£200M+`**, delivered through government funding, **Cisco, IBM
and BT**, with **Skills England** setting the curriculum framework — and *few traditional education
providers named in the delivery model*. 🔵 **The EU is buying institutional capacity; the UK is
buying deployment speed. A studio proposal should be shaped differently in each.**
🟢 **Public demand is measurable: `64%` of Europeans agree that by `2030` everyone will need to be
AI-literate**, with country-level variance in whether generative AI use in formal education is
encouraged or still taboo.
🔴 **Article `50`'s transparency duty remains the hard commercial deadline here, and it is `50` days
out.** 🆕 🔴 **p176 adds something concrete to it rather than restating it:** the one permissive
shelf row with provenance tooling can be brought into Article `50(2)` compliance by **one line** —
and the measured consequence is that its export gate then **refuses every real asset** until a
signing certificate is procured (`gap 136`). 🔵 **That is the honest EMEA conversation: the code is
a line, the blocker is procurement, and today's green gate is green because the field is discarded.**
🟢 **Repository layer: `2` rows, both HOLDER-GRADE** — `ls1intum/Artemis` (TUM) and
`dodona-edu/dolos` (Ghent). 🔵 **Both are university-owned, which is the strongest placement
evidence `P800` admits, and neither has been read past its licence yet.**

### APAC

🟢 **Market, `search-summary` tier:** **`~US$591.6M` (2024) → `~US$1 848.1M` by 2029**, with the
region repeatedly described as the fastest-growing, driven by internet penetration and
government-led digital education reform.
🟢 **Adoption evidence is broad rather than education-specific:** `56%` of APAC businesses have
deployed chatbots, copilots or AI assistants; `57%` of organisations in Asia have incorporated AI
into at least one area of operations.
🔴 **Regulation is fragmented, with three distinct postures named:** **Singapore** promoting
responsible AI with mature guidelines; **China** legislating against algorithmic misconduct; the
**ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and Ethics** still early-stage. 🟢 **China's labelling duty, recorded
at p175 from `ACTION KG` and carried: Art. `4` explicit / Art. `5` implicit marking, live since
`1 Sep 2025`, and BROADER than the EU's** — the implicit duty applies to all synthetic content
regardless of confusion potential.
🔴 **WRITTEN DOWN AS A GAP, because silence looks exactly like coverage: the APAC sweep returned
`0` education-specific AI vendors and `0` education-specific regulators.** No ministry programme,
no student-data rule, no classroom-use guidance, no named edtech company. 🔵 **The region with the
strongest compulsory-curriculum mandate in this KB is the region whose vendor map is emptiest, and
that is a finding about this KB's channels, not about APAC.**
🟢 **Repository layer is the opposite: APAC is the best-served region at the repository level.**
`5` of `topic:education`'s top `50` and `6` of `topic:ai-education`'s `7` placeable rows sit here.
🆕 🟢 **p176 adds three `MIT` rows at licence-body grade** — `wwlwgo/cn-primary-math-knowledge-graph`
(`1 117` B, *"Contributors to the China Primary Mathematics Knowledge Graph"*, `HEAD`
`3b6f38f0e4fb`), `Scorpioyyy/verichalk` (`1 066` B, `HEAD` `9f963ab192a1`, `4 198` files) and the two
national-examination engines already routed. 🔵 **Both new rows are grounded in a national
curriculum, which is what `P800` accepts as placement.**

### LATAM

🔴 **TENTH consecutive pass with no LATAM-ORIGINATED open-source education repository, and the
statement is a measurement rather than a silence:** `0` of `100` rows across two exactly-counted
tags at p175, placement rule applied row by row; p176 re-counted both tags and added no LATAM row.
🔵 **The nearest miss is still `kelvins/algorithms-and-data-structures` (`1 088`★), maintained from
Brazil with nothing in the artefact saying so — which is exactly what `P800` exists to refuse.**
🟢 **But the DEMAND evidence is now the strongest of any region, and it is institutional:** a
**UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS** working paper maps AI use across teaching, research, administration and
governance in Latin American and Caribbean universities, from a survey of **`200` institutions in
`19` countries, fielded August–October `2025`**. 🔵 **That is a named, dated, multi-country
instrument — better sourcing than any market figure in this file.**
🟢 **Uptake, from a `2026` regional survey: student use `92%` (from `86%`), teacher use `79%` (from
`61%`), and `65%` of students fear AI will make their learning superficial.** 🔵 **Teacher adoption
rising `18` points in a year is the sharpest adoption delta in this file, and the `65%` is a
pedagogical-design opening, not an objection.**
🔴 **Market sizing here is the weakest in the file and should be used with the caveat attached:** the
*"Rest of Latin America"* segment is put at **`~US$18.2M` (2024) → `~US$36M` by 2029** — a residual
category that **excludes the region's largest markets**, so it measures the tail, not LATAM.
🔴 **WRITTEN DOWN AS A GAP: the LATAM sweep returned `0` education-specific `2026` regulations.**
CEPAL's **ILIA `2025`** index carries a regulation sub-dimension, and p176 reached only its table of
contents. Commentary describes *a patchwork of pilot policies vulnerable to being discarded with
each administration*, with **Chile** named as the counterexample — early digital infrastructure and
an AI roadmap that survived a change of government.
🟢 **The commercial map, carried: `Luca`, `BeConfident` (`US$15.8M`, Prosus Ventures) and `Teachy`
(`US$7M`, Goodwater + Reach).** 🔵 **The vendor column fills while the repository column stays
empty, and after ten passes that pattern is itself the finding: LATAM edtech is being built as
product, not as commons.** 🟢 **For a studio, that is an opportunity statement rather than a
complaint — the permissive layer a LATAM engagement needs will have to be brought, not found.**
