## Opportunities by region

### Global

🟢 **State the size with the tag attached or do not state it.** **`8 197`** repositories tag
themselves `education`, carry an `MIT` licence and were pushed inside `180` days — exact, one
request. **`135`** tag themselves `ai-education` under the same filters — also exact, reproduced for
a third pass. 🔴 **The ratio is `60.7×`, and the two populations describe different worlds: the
narrow one is single-maintainer tutor skills, the wide one is institutional software.**
🟢 **Market frame, directional and source-conflicted, which is how it should be quoted.** Published
2026 global AI-in-education figures cluster between **`US$8.7B`** and **`US$10.4B`**, with
ten-year projections between `US$100B` and `US$137B` at CAGRs of `34–41%`. 🔴 **The spread is a
SCOPE artefact — some include corporate training, hardware and services — so a deck should name the
publisher and the scope or give a range, never a single figure.**
🟢 **The one compliance instrument that is live in two markets at once, with article numbers:**

| market | duty | article | in force |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **EU** | transparency / machine-readable marking of synthetic content | **Art. `50`**; `50(2)` backstop for systems already on the market | 🟢 Art. `50` since **`2 Aug 2026`**; backstop 🔴 **`2 Dec 2026`** |
| 🔴 **China** | EXPLICIT label on text, image, audio, video, virtual scenes — retained on download and export | **Art. `4`** | 🔴 **`1 Sep 2025`** |
| 🔴 **China** | IMPLICIT label in file METADATA — content attribute, provider name or code, content reference number | **Art. `5`** | 🔴 **`1 Sep 2025`** |

🔴 **The Chinese implicit duty is BROADER than the EU's: it applies to all AI-generated synthetic
content regardless of whether anyone could be confused, where Art. `50` is aimed at deception.**
🟢 **And the EU high-risk regime moved while Art. `50` did not** — the Digital Omnibus put Annex III
(which carries education) at `2 Dec 2027` and Annex I at `2 Aug 2028`. 🔵 **So a client's
transparency obligation is live NOW and their high-risk obligation has `26` months. Those are two
different programmes and a deck that merges them will over-sell urgency on one and under-sell it on
the other.**
🔴 **Said rather than implied: not one of those dates was read in primary form.** `9` of `11` hosts
are NXDOMAIN, `eur-lex` among them.

### North America

🟢 **This is the region p175 corrects most, and the correction is favourable.** The repository layer
is not thin — it was invisible to the query. **`5` of the `12` placeable rows** in the wide tag's
top `50` are North American, and two are holder-grade:
[`ucfopen/canvasapi`](https://github.com/ucfopen/canvasapi) (`MIT`, `1 129` B, *"University of
Central Florida — Center for Distributed Learning"*) and
[`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) (`MIT`, Learning Equality).
🟡 **Plus [`AnubisLMS/Anubis`](https://github.com/AnubisLMS/Anubis) — a distributed LMS on
Kubernetes, *"From NYU"*, `MIT`** — description-grade, because its copyright holder is an individual.
🟢 **The opportunity: the administrative layer finally has a permissive client under it.**
`canvasapi` is a Canvas LMS client, and Canvas is the dominant North American institutional LMS.
🔵 **An engagement that previously had to choose between writing a Canvas integration from scratch
and buying one now has a university-maintained `MIT` option to extend.**
🟢 **Market frame:** North America is the largest regional market on every published split —
`36–38%` of global spend, with regional 2025 estimates around `US$3.1B` growing at `24%` CAGR on
the conservative scope and `41.7%` on the aggressive one. Teacher adoption in the US is reported
around `60%`.
🔴 **`gap 119`, fourth carry, and p175 makes it sharper rather than quieter: all `5` of those
placements are UNITED STATES institutions under a heading that says *"North America"*.** 🔵 **No
Canadian or Mexican institution has ever placed a row in this KB.** `ACTION KK` settles it next pass
— either the heading gets the country split or it gets renamed.

### EMEA

🟢 **Two holder-grade institutional placements in one sweep, both university-OWNED teaching
software released permissively, and this is the region's distinctive asset.**
[`ls1intum/Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis) is the Technical University of Munich's
own exercise and exam platform — programming, modelling and text exercises, autograding, exam mode —
`MIT`, `818`★. [`dodona-edu/dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) is Ghent University's
source-code plagiarism detector, `MIT`, `350`★.
🟢 **The opportunity, and it is the most concrete one in this file: `Dolos` fills a need this KB has
never had a permissive row for.** Assessment integrity under AI has been answered here with design
patterns — oral defence, process evidence, detection-scepticism — because no tool existed to name.
🔵 **Now one does, and it comes from a university rather than a vendor, which is the provenance a
European institutional buyer trusts most.**
🟢 **Regulatory position, and it is an advantage rather than only a cost.** Art. `50` transparency
is live since `2 Aug 2026`; Annex III high-risk — which covers admissions screening, automated
grading, proctoring and student monitoring — moved to `2 Dec 2027`. 🔵 **Deployer duties were NOT
postponed:** human oversight, monitoring, logging and informing affected people under Art. `26`,
and a Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment under Art. `27` before a high-risk system goes into use.
🟢 **So the sellable EMEA engagement is a compliance-shaped one: `26` months to build the Annex III
file, and an Art. `50` marking obligation that is already due.** The marking recipe is Pattern `1`
in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).
🔴 **A tutoring chatbot that does not grade is probably NOT high-risk** — a distinction worth making
early, because it decides whether an engagement carries an Annex III programme at all.

### APAC

🟢 **The most placeable region in both populations, and the only one that was ever visible through
the narrow tag** — `6` of `7` there, `5` of `12` in the wide one. 🔵 **That asymmetry is itself the
finding: APAC's education-AI output is tutor-and-exam shaped and tags itself accordingly.**
🟢 **Holder-grade this pass: [`uccser/cs-field-guide`](https://github.com/uccser/cs-field-guide)** —
*"University of Canterbury Computer Science Education Research Group"*, `MIT` at `LICENCE`, an
interactive high-school CS textbook from New Zealand.
🟡 **Description-grade: [`showlab/Code2Video`](https://github.com/showlab/Code2Video)** (`MIT`,
`2 123`★, ICML 2026) — educational video generation via CODE rather than pixels, which is a
genuinely different architecture from the shelf's video-authoring route and worth a technical look.
🟢 **Curriculum-anchored rows are the region's signature, and they are an asset for engagement
work:** `taiwan-exam` (學測 / 大考), `fakao-ai-exam-engine` (法考, NPC statute database),
`cn-primary-math-knowledge-graph` (中国小学数学), `sparks-ai-math-tutor` (**NCERT**, India),
`genki-study-resources` (a named Japanese textbook), `Auto_courseGrabber` (正方教务系统, tested at
universities across several provinces).
🟢 **Policy, which is moving faster here than anywhere:** China made AI a compulsory subject from age
six by September 2025, with Beijing mandating at least eight hours a year, and already enforces
algorithm filings, synthetic-content labelling and security review for public models. South Korea's
**AI Basic Act took effect `22 Jan 2026`** and classes education as *"high-impact"*, requiring human
oversight and documentation. Japan's AI Promotion Act carries no penalties and works through
guidance. 🔴 **India has no AI statute yet, though it signalled in July 2026 it may pursue one.**
🔴 **A cautionary datum for anyone selling an AI-textbook programme: South Korea's mandatory AI
textbooks were stripped of official status after adoption stayed under `30%` and teacher unions
objected that the pace had outrun preparation.** 🔵 **Korea redirected to a `US$960M` plan for AI
talent centres and vocational schools through 2030. The lesson sells better than the product did:
teacher readiness is the binding constraint, not content.**

### LATAM

🔴 **Ninth consecutive pass with no LATAM-originated open-source education repository, and the claim
is finally a MEASUREMENT rather than a search that came back quiet: `0` of `100` rows across two
exactly-counted tags, with `P800` applied row by row.**
🔵 **The nearest miss is instructive:
[`kelvins/algorithms-and-data-structures`](https://github.com/kelvins/algorithms-and-data-structures)
(`1 088`★, `MIT`) is maintained from Brazil and its artefact names no institution, curriculum or
standard.** Placing it would be guessing from a person's name — the one inference `P800` forbids.
🟢 **So the region's story in this KB is COMMERCIAL, and it is a strong one.** `BeConfident`
(`US$15.8M`, Prosus Ventures), `Teachy` (`US$7M`, Goodwater + Reach) and `Luca`, with Brazil
carrying close to half of the 2026 regional edtech cohort and the clear majority of deal activity.
🟢 **Adoption is the region's real asset and it is above the OECD average, not below it:** teacher
AI use at `56%` in Brazil, `55%` in Chile, `53%` in Colombia and `52%` in Costa Rica against an
OECD average of `36%`, and nine in ten 15-year-olds in the region's PISA 2025 countries reporting
chatbot use for schoolwork. 🔴 **Governance lags that adoption — UNESCO IESALC finds widespread
higher-education adoption with institutional governance behind it.**
🟢 **Regulation, with the status flagged because the sources are dated:** Brazil's **PL 2338/2023**
is the most advanced bill and classes education as high-risk, bringing mandatory impact assessments
and human oversight — though a 2024 analysis describes a legislative setback after amendments.
Colombia has issued mostly non-binding ethical frameworks. Mexico's bill mirrors the EU risk model
without an explicit ban on unacceptable-risk systems.
🟢 **The engagement shape this implies, stated plainly so a LATAM pitch is not weaker than an EMEA
one: start from a global permissive base — `Kolibri` for offline reach, `mentingo` or `Artemis` for
the platform, `Dolos` for integrity — and pair with a local commercial partner.** 🔵 **The one thing
a LATAM deck should not do is imply there is a regional repository to fork. There is not, and this
KB has now measured that twice on exact denominators.**

## Opportunities by region

### Global

🟢 **State the size with the tag attached or do not state it.** `8 191` repositories tag themselves
`education`, declare `MIT` and were pushed inside `180` days. `135` tag themselves `ai-education`
under the same filters — a usable **shortlist** and a useless denominator (`T255`). 🔵 **Both
numbers are now exact; the difference between them is a labelling convention, not a market.**
🔴 **The marking cliff is `50` days out: Article `50(2)` of `Regulation (EU) 2024/1689` requires
machine-readable marking of synthetic content from `2 December 2026`.** 🆕 🟢 **What changed is the
answer, not the deadline.** On the shelf's most provenance-literate row the remedy is four edits —
reject the synthetic default, stop the gate exempting it, sign between delivery-verification and the
content-addressed insert, carry the signer's record — plus **a certificate, which is procurement.**
🆕 🔴 **The compliance trap worth repeating to any client with a C2PA story: that project's
`c2pa_required` flag can be switched ON and nothing changes**, because the gate accepts the
*"not applicable"* status that generated assets are born with, and no test in the repository sets
the flag (`T258`). 🔵 **A compliance switch nothing exercises is a label, not a control.**
🟢 **Liveness, with its last caveat removed:** `25.2%` of the *"active"* population is unpushed for
`90` days, and `pushed:` matches the last COMMIT to within a day on `19` of `20` sampled rows
(`ACTION KB`), so a `pushed:`-filtered shortlist needs no asterisk — but still filter on it.

### North America

🆕 🟢 **The adoption curve, dated:** district teacher AI training nearly **doubled, `24%` → `48%`**,
and student use of chatbots for schoolwork doubled, **`13%` → `26%`**; `60%` of US K-12 teachers
used AI tools in `2024/25` and `32%` weekly. 🔴 **Governance has not followed: only `40%` of
districts had an AI policy by spring `2025`, `~10%` of institutions have formal AI guidelines and
`71%` of teachers report no AI training.** 🟢 **That asymmetry — use doubling, policy flat — is the
engagement: policy, assessment integrity and teacher enablement, not tooling.**
🟢 **Scale precedent for a deck:** Miami-Dade moved from banning chatbots to deploying Gemini to
**`100 000`** high-schoolers. 🟢 **Vendor posture:** Anthropic's Claude for Education with Learning
Mode, Google's free Gemini for students through `2026`, OpenAI's `US$50M` university research
commitment — the model vendors are buying the channel, so differentiation is integration and
governance.
🆕 🟢 **And North America's repository column is no longer empty of institutional code:**
[`opensacorg/learning-blocks`](https://github.com/opensacorg/learning-blocks) is an `MIT` SIS whose
grant names **Code for Sacramento**, a Code for America brigade — an institution, so the row is
PLACED under `P800` rather than guessed.
🔴 **`gap 119` carried and not addressed: every other *"North America"* figure here is a United
States fact under a continental heading.** Canada and Mexico were not re-asked this pass.

## Opportunities by region

### Global

🟢 **The honest framing is that the market numbers disagree and the disagreement is large.**
Research and Markets puts AI-in-education at **~`$10.6B` in 2026 → `$42.48B` by 2030**; a K-12
Academics report traces **~`$6B` (2024) → ~`$32B` (2030)**. 🔴 **Do not present a single figure as
consensus — present the range and the two sources, because a client who checks will find both.**
🟢 **Where the demand signal is converging, across `1EdTech`, HolonIQ and the vendor-neutral
coverage: (`1`) purpose-built platforms displacing generic chatbots, (`2`) **institutional
governance** — AI-use policies, approval processes, oversight structures — and (`3`) **skills-based
credentials** driven by employer demand.**
🟢 **The Globant-shaped opportunity: `6` of this KB's `16` routing needs have a verified permissive
route, `1` more has a permissive route with a fixable dependency, `5` are copyleft-only, and `1`
— a permissive SIS — has no option at all after eleven passes of looking. That asymmetry is the
offer: the gap is never the LMS, it is the administrative and assessment layers around it.**
🔴 **And `T235`: across the `12` agent rows this KB holds, `0` are rostering- or
analytics-primary. The administrative layer has `7` platforms and not one agent.**

### North America

🟢 **Largest regional share: **`36%` of the market, `$3.68B` in 2026**. Teacher adoption is
already mass-market — **`60%` of US K-12 teachers** used AI tools in the 2024–25 school year,
**`32%` at least weekly**.**
🔴 **The regulatory surface is STATE-level and it is crowded: **`134` AI-in-education bills across
`31` states in 2026**, and **more than `30` states** now have AI guidance documents. The themes
that matter commercially:**
- 🔴 **Student-data training restrictions** — California's **AB 1159** would bar training models on student data.
- 🔴 **Mandatory human oversight** — **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** require it and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students.
- 🟢 **Policy mandates with a date** — **Ohio** required every K-12 district to adopt a formal AI use policy by **1 July 2026**. That is a procurement trigger, not a principle.
- 🟡 **Federal**: the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act (H.R. 8747)** would let schools spend federal funds on AI curriculum and literacy; advanced in committee **21 July 2026**, largely on party lines.
- 🔴 **Counter-current**: **New York City** has a one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through eighth grade and is urging other districts to copy it.

🟢 **Opportunity: the oversight and policy-compliance layer, not the tutor.** A district that must
show human oversight and a written AI policy needs audit trails, decision logs and
teacher-in-the-loop gates. 🟢 **`mentingo`'s `licenses.config.yaml` gate and the
`compose/code/grading-draft-gate` pattern are the two assets this KB already holds for it.**
🔴 **Measured silence: every NA source that returned was about the UNITED STATES. Canada and Mexico
returned nothing on adoption, regulation or players. Written down because an empty bucket and an
unexamined bucket look identical.**

### EMEA

🔴 **Regulation is the market driver here and the near date is `2 December 2026` (Article `50(2)`
marking), not the Annex III date most decks lead with.**
🟢 **`ACTION JQ`'s measurement is the sales argument: of the `13` shelf rows that put synthetic
content in front of a learner, **`0` can mark it** — `0` marking artefacts and `0` marking
dependencies across `18 360` files. `4` rows track INPUT provenance, which is a different
obligation. 🔴 **If this shelf is representative, essentially no open-source edtech is Article
`50(2)`-ready `50` days out.**
🟢 **Adoption is programme-led rather than product-led. **JA Europe's AI-ENTR4YOUTH**, backed by
**Intel** and the **European Commission**, has secondary students building applied AI projects in
**`10` countries** — piloted in Italy, Portugal and Spain, now also Albania, Bulgaria, Czechia,
France, Greece, Romania and Ukraine.**
🔴 **Institutional caution is real and quotable: the **European Schools** network reports that **no
generative AI tool has been officially approved for system-wide use**, and its approval process is
still being built.**
🟢 **Opportunity: the marking-and-conformity layer.** `50` days of runway, a measured `0%` readiness
on the open-source shelf, and this KB already ships the tooling —
`compose/code/aiact-50-2-marking`, `-spans`, `-pack` and `-exposure`.
🟢 **EMEA-placed assets this KB now holds, where it previously claimed none:
[`Selleo/mentingo`](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) (Poland, `MIT`, corporate L&D) and
[`TovTechOrg/Tov-learn`](https://github.com/TovTechOrg/Tov-learn) (`MIT`, non-profit).**
🔴 **Measured silence, and it is the biggest one on this page: *"EMEA"* returned EUROPE only. Middle
East and Africa produced nothing on adoption, regulation or players in any search this pass. A
region named for three continents was answered about one.**

### APAC

🟢 **The region's drivers in 2026 are two ENACTED laws, which is more than any other region can
say:**
- 🔴 **South Korea — the `AI Basic Act`, effective **January 2026**, built around *"high-impact AI"*, and **education is a named high-impact domain** alongside healthcare, finance and employment.
- 🔴 **Vietnam — AI law in force **`1 March 2026`**, with an extra **six-month grace period** for education, healthcare and finance systems already running before `15 August 2026`.
- 🟡 **China** already enforces algorithm filings, **synthetic-content labelling** and security review for public models — the marking obligation EMEA is `50` days from, operating now.
- 🟢 **Japan** is light-touch and voluntary; **Singapore** has the deepest governance investment in ASEAN; the **Philippines** intends to propose a regional AI framework during its **2026 ASEAN chairmanship**.

🟢 **Market: Grand View projects APAC to **`$18.21B` by 2033** at **`28.1%` CAGR (2026–2033)**, from
`$2.28B` regional revenue in 2025. Named regional players: Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's;
AWS and Alphabet globally.**
🟡 **Public opinion diverges sharply INSIDE the region — Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 finds lower
support for banning AI in schools across Asian markets, while **Australia and New Zealand record
HIGHER support for banning it**. ANZ is not APAC for this purpose and a single regional message
will fail in one half of it.**
🟢 **UNESCO: China and Singapore have established AI-in-education policies while other countries in
the region are still meeting basic educational needs. The spread is the point.**
🟢 **Opportunity: **synthetic-content labelling is already a live compliance requirement in China**,
so the Article `50(2)` tooling built for EMEA ships to APAC with a changed label and no new
engineering. 🟢 **APAC-placed assets this KB now holds:
[`echo-loop/Echo-Loop`](https://github.com/echo-loop/Echo-Loop) (中央民族大学, `AGPL-3.0`),
[`SenmuuuuW/universal-diagnostic-tutor-skill`](https://github.com/SenmuuuuW/universal-diagnostic-tutor-skill)
(`MIT`), [`niansia/taiwan-exam`](https://github.com/niansia/taiwan-exam) (`MIT`, `AGPL` dependency)
and [`jeliael/fakao-ai-exam-engine`](https://github.com/jeliael/fakao-ai-exam-engine) (`MIT`) — `4`
rows, the most of any region, and `2` of the `4` were mis-filed as `Global` until this pass.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The region's defining number is a GAP, and it is measured: UNESCO IESALC surveyed **`200`
institutions across `19` countries** and found **`87%` using AI in at least one area** and **`74%`
in teaching and learning** — while **only `26%` have a formal AI strategy**.**
🔴 **Adoption `87%`, governance `26%`. That `61`-point spread is the single most sellable fact on
this page, and it is a governance engagement, not a tooling one.**
🟢 **Institution type predicts it: private non-profit universities **`84%`**, public **`68%`**,
private for-profit **`52%`**.**
🟢 **Schools match higher education. OECD TALIS puts teachers in **Brazil, Chile, Colombia and
Costa Rica above the OECD average of `36%`**, and **`75%` of Uruguayan public-school teachers**
report using these tools (Ceibal, 2026).**
🟡 **Regulation is pending, not absent, and takes two shapes:**
- 🟡 **Brazil — `PL 2.338/2023`, risk-based, now in the Chamber of Deputies. The text can still change.**
- 🟡 **Chile — a government-sponsored risk-based bill in its first constitutional stage (Commission on Future, Science and Technology).**
- 🟢 **Colombia — policy first: national AI policy via **`CONPES 4144`, February 2025**, rather than a statute.**

🟢 **UNESCO is the most active regional actor and the natural partner channel: it launched the
**Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean on
`14 April 2026`**, and runs AI regulation and ethics training for officials in Ecuador and Chile.**
🟢 **Opportunity: close the `87/26` gap.** AI-use policy, oversight structure and evidence of
learning benefit — the three things HolonIQ and `1EdTech` say buyers are now demanding globally,
needed here by `74%` of institutions that already deployed. 🟢 **`Kolibri` (`MIT`, offline-first)
remains the right technical substrate for the low-connectivity half of the region.**
🔴 **Measured silence: no LATAM **edtech vendor or funder** was named in any source this pass, and
no country-specific 2026 EDUCATION rule (as distinct from general AI bills) surfaced. The searches
returned institutional and multilateral actors only — UNESCO, IESALC, ministries, Ceibal. The
commercial map of LATAM edtech is a hole in this KB and this is the fourth pass to say so.**

## Pass 171 — 2026-10-13

🆕 🟢 **`T217` is SETTLED and the answer is better than either side of it. `Regulation (EU)
2026/1744` is REAL — it is the *"Digital Omnibus on AI"*, signed `8 July 2026`, and it AMENDS
`Regulation (EU) 2024/1689`, the AI Act. This KB was neither fabricating nor mis-citing; it was
naming the amendment and omitting the Act.**
🆕 🔴 **And the consequence this KB had BACKWARDS: it has been watching the Annex III high-risk
date. That date moved to `2 December 2027`. The date that did NOT move is Article `50`
transparency — **`2 December 2026`, `50` days from this pass** — and it is the nearest binding
obligation on every row of this shelf.**
🆕 🟢 **`gap 114` CLOSED: the `2026-08` vs `2027-12-02` disagreement was two sources being right
about two different instruments.**

### 🆕 🟢 The instrument stack, named correctly, for any EMEA client deck

| | instrument | what it does | tier |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **BASE — name this FIRST** | 🟢 **`Regulation (EU) 2024/1689`** | 🟢 **the AI Act. Annex III point `3` puts education in scope: access and admission, evaluating learning outcomes *(including steering the learning process)*, assessing the appropriate level of education, and monitoring and detecting prohibited behaviour during tests** | 🟡 `search-summary` |
| 🟢 **AMENDMENT** | 🟢 **`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`** | 🟢 **Digital Omnibus on AI. Defers stand-alone Annex III high-risk duties to `2 December 2027` and Annex I embedded systems to `2 August 2028`. Adds two Article `5(1)` prohibitions (non-consensual intimate imagery, CSAM)** | 🟡 `search-summary`, confirmed on `3` independent non-vendor channels |
| 🔴 **UNMOVED** | 🔴 **Article `50` of the base Act** | 🔴 **transparency and machine-readable marking of synthetic content. `2 December 2026`. NOT deferred by the omnibus** | 🟡 `search-summary`, consistent across `~8` sources |

🔵 **Read the dates in the order a client will feel them: marking in `50` days, high-risk in `~14`
months, embedded systems in `~22` months. A deck that leads with Annex III leads with the furthest
deadline.**
🟡 **Honest limit, unchanged: `eur-lex.europa.eu` returns `403` at the CONNECT layer from this
container — the egress proxy DENIES it by organisation policy, not a site outage, so no retry and
no alternate transport closes it. `artificialintelligenceact.eu` does not resolve. The Official

## Opportunities by region

🟢 **All five values from the closed vocabulary appear below, each as its own `###` subsection.**

### Global

🟢 **The permissive supply is now sized rather than estimated. A sweep of the whole `ai-education`
GitHub topic — `485` repositories, `357` active within `180` days — yields `149` that are permissive
and active (`135` `MIT`, `14` `Apache-2.0`, `0` `BSD`, `0` `ISC`). In this vertical the permissive
choice is `MIT` or nothing.**
🔴 **But at `≥20` ★ the permissive active population is `6` repositories, `4` of which this shelf
already carries. The vertical's significant tail is genuinely thin; its long tail is large and
mostly single-author.**
🟢 **Opportunity: the `217` third-party grant documents inside `Sakai` and `Moodle` are now
classified, `0` of them hostile. Licence review of the two leading institutional LMSes is a
PROCEDURAL cost a studio can quote with confidence rather than a risk it must discover.**
🔴 **Counter-opportunity, and it is the honest one: `10` passes have now failed to find a SIS or an
education ERP under `MIT` or `Apache-2.0`. Anyone needing one is choosing between copyleft
(`openeducat_erp` `LGPL-3.0` on `odoo/odoo` `LGPL-3.0`, `frappe/erpnext` `GPL-3.0`) and a build.**

### North America

🟢 **Policy is the demand driver and it is fragmented by design. Federal: the April `2025` executive
order on AI education for youth sets priorities, and the Department of Education finalised an
AI grant-priority rule on `13 April 2026` favouring proposals that build AI literacy into teaching.**
🟡 **States: sources disagree on the count — `37` states plus Puerto Rico with official guidance by
June `2026` on one tracker, `33`–`35` on another. Legislation is moving faster than guidance: `68`
bills across `27` states this session, `10` enacted in `2026`. Ohio required every public, community
and STEM school to adopt an AI framework by `1 July 2026`; Tennessee's `2024` law already required
district AI policies.**
🔴 **A procurement constraint worth naming: New York City's March `2026` guidance BARS using AI to
assign grades or make disciplinary decisions. Any assessment-automation pitch into a US district
needs a human-decision boundary in the architecture, not in the appendix.**
🟢 **Opportunity: `37` states' worth of guidance documents and `0` interoperability standard between
them. Policy-to-configuration tooling — mapping a district's adopted framework onto an LMS's actual
settings — is a repeatable engagement, and `Selleo/mentingo`'s `licenses.config.yaml` is the shape of
the artefact.**
🔴 **Informed gap: this pass found little on HIGHER-ED adoption in North America beyond campus-wide
vendor deals and some institutions turning AI detection off. Not searched to exhaustion; recorded
so the next pass does not read silence as coverage.**

### EMEA

🟢 **The regulatory calendar above IS the EMEA opportunity, and the `50`-day Article `50(2)` marking
deadline is the entry point. This KB already carries offline tooling for it
(`compose/code/aiact-50-2-marking`, `-pack`, `-exposure`, with an `XSD`), which is a rare case of the
shelf being ahead of the deadline rather than behind it.**
🟢 **Institutional demand is documented and unmet: the DEC Leadership Summit EMEA (Paris, May `2026`,
`27` institutions across `15` countries) found `51%` of employers already expect AI proficiency as a
baseline graduate competency while only `3%` believe higher education is keeping pace. The EUA's
February `2026` report frames adoption around institutional VALUES, not tooling.**
🟢 **Sovereignty is a live procurement axis with named actors: a French university consortium
including Paris-Saclay is deploying with Mistral AI and EdTech France explicitly on sovereignty
grounds; Estonia runs a national ChatGPT Edu integration, which cuts the other way. Both are
reference shapes a studio can be asked to replicate.**
🟢 **Opportunity: `OpenOLAT` (`Apache-2.0`, §`1`–`9` verbatim, Swiss/German institutional lineage) is
the only permissive institutional LMS this KB has verified, and it is the natural core of a
sovereign EMEA stack. Pair with self-hosted inference; the licence carries a patent grant.**
🔴 **Informed gap: no EMEA-originated permissive AI-education AGENT was found this pass at any star
level. The EMEA strength is platforms and policy, not agents.**

### APAC

🟢 **Binding, education-naming regulation exists here and nowhere else. Korea's Framework Act on AI
took effect `22 January 2026` with extraterritorial reach and places education inside
*"high-impact"*. Vietnam's Law on AI took effect `1 March 2026` — the first comprehensive
standalone AI statute in Southeast Asia — and names EDUCATION high-risk, citing automated
assessment and behavioural monitoring. China regulates in a targeted way; Japan, Singapore and
Australia remain voluntary and principles-based. India has no dedicated AI statute.**
🆕 🟢 **And for the first time this KB can name APAC-ORIGINATED education code: `niansia/taiwan-exam`
(`MIT`, `593` files, Taiwan university-entrance mock exams with worked solutions and official
layout) and `jeliael/fakao-ai-exam-engine` (`MIT`, Chinese bar exam, `8` subjects, statute
cross-checking). Both licence bodies READ, both `HEAD`-verified.**
🔴 **Both are single-purpose exam engines, not platforms, so the standing gap — no APAC-originated
open-source education PLATFORM — survives a sixth pass. What changed is that the silence broke.**
🟢 **Opportunity: Vietnam and Korea both name automated ASSESSMENT specifically, and `4` of this
pass's `5` new finds are assessment tools. The regulatory requirement and the open-source supply
point at the same place — assessment with an auditable decision trail — which is the clearest
regulation-to-build match on this shelf.**

### LATAM

🔴 **Adoption is the highest in the world and governance the thinnest, and that gap IS the
engagement. A Digital Education Council study across `29` institutions including UNAM measures
`91.5%` of students and `75%` of faculty using AI tools; student use rose from `86%` in `2024` to
`92%` in `2026`. Only `30%` of students and staff think their university integrates AI effectively
or resources it adequately. UNESCO IESALC's September `2026` study reports the same split:
widespread adoption, lagging governance.**
🔴 **`gap 104` re-confirmed a SIXTH time: there is no education-specific AI regulation anywhere in
LATAM. Chile leads with a `2021` national AI policy and a bill in its first constitutional stage;
Brazil's `PL 2.338/2023` sits in the Chamber of Deputies; Colombia's `CONPES 4144` is a policy
instrument, not a statute. What LATAM has instead is institutional.**
🟢 **Opportunity, and it is the one this KB keeps arriving at: institutional AI governance as a
deliverable — acceptable-use policy, assessment-integrity boundary, data-protection posture and the
licence gate — because no regulator will supply it. `65%` of students fear AI makes their learning
shallower and over half worry about data privacy, so the governance artefact has internal demand,
not just compliance demand.**
🟢 **`learningequality/kolibri` (`MIT`) remains the offline-first row for low-connectivity
deployments, and `47` of this topic's permissive repositories are `MIT`-licensed single-purpose
tools that can be composed without a procurement cycle.**
🔴 **Informed gap, stated plainly for a sixth pass: a KB built for a company with largely LATAM
delivery cannot name ONE education platform originating in the region, and p171's `485`-repository
topic sweep returned no LATAM-originated permissive education repository at any star level. This is
a measured absence on a named channel, not an unsearched one.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The region with the most statutory pull, and the pull has not moved this pass: `~134` bills across
`31` states, Ohio first to require a district AI policy (deadline `2026-07-01`), Tennessee alongside
it.** 🟢 **Sell the policy-and-controls engagement, not the tutor — a per-state compliance matrix
instantiated as district templates, at the volume of thousands of districts.**
🔴 **Two hard architectural constraints, both statutory: California `AB 1159` forbids training on
student data, and Oklahoma and Maryland require human oversight with AI barred from high-stakes
decisions. A product that grades autonomously or trains on tenant data is unsellable in those states.**
🔴 **Lead with the objection, because a client will raise it: New York City and LAUSD have both
suspended or restricted student-facing generative AI. The sellable shape is TEACHER-facing with the
teacher in the loop.**
🟢 **The funded framing is AI LITERACY — `H.R. 8747` advanced in committee `2026-07-21`, and the ED
grant-priority rule points the same way.** 🟢 **Adoption is real, not aspirational: `~60%` of K-12
teachers used AI tools in `2024-25`, `32%` weekly, and North America holds `~36%` of the market.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Sell CONFORMITY work: this is the region where the rules are mapped and uptake is not.**
🟢 **Name `Regulation (EU) 2024/1689` in the deck (`T217`), and treat the `2026-08` vs `2027-12`
Annex III conflict as a budgeting question — budget to the earlier date, build to the later.**
🔴 **One feature class is removed rather than documented: emotion inference is PROHIBITED in
educational contexts. Engagement detection is not a compliance task, it is a deletion.**
🟢 **Schools bind as DEPLOYERS, which is the commercially useful fact — the work attaches to the
institution's use, where the budget and the anxiety both sit.**
🆕 🟢 **The architecture that fits just improved: `OpenOLAT` (`Apache-2.0`, §`1`–`9` verbatim, HEAD
`1` day old, `QTI` and `SCORM` built in, Swiss/German institutional heritage) is the permissive
institutional LMS this page should have been routing EMEA engagements to for `32` passes (`T216`).**
🟢 **`Open edX` behind an API boundary stays the `AGPL-3.0` route; `Sakai` stays available with
`ECL-2.0` decided in writing first (`T208`, `P169-C`).**

### APAC

🆕 🟢 **The timing argument is now a CURRICULUM argument, which is stronger than a compliance one.**
🟢 **India is the single largest dated opportunity on this page: AI and computational thinking
compulsory from Class `3` starting academic year `2026-27`. That is courseware, teacher training and
assessment at national scale with a deadline inside twelve months, and `P168-C`'s `MIT` courseware
tier — `53` verified locales — is the asset to take into it.**
🔴 **China is the largest market and the highest barrier: compulsory AI from age `6`, six binding
instruments, and algorithm registration that requires a local entity. Treat as partner-led.**
🟢 **Singapore is the cheapest credential: no statute, but AI Verify conformance functions as a de
facto procurement requirement, and the `2026-01` agentic governance framework means an agent product
can be certified against something that exists.**
🟡 **Korea's pilot period (`2026`, limited enforcement) remains the lowest-risk window to deploy and
document, carried from p169.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The concrete entry point is UNESCO's LAC observatory, launched `2026-04-14`, with a Mexico pilot
running through CONALEP and DGETI — a named programme with an institutional counterpart, which is
rarer and more actionable than a market figure.**
🟢 **Brazil is the convergence play: `PL 2338/2023` is risk-based and the EU–Brazil digital
partnership (`2026-06`) signals alignment with the EU shape, so EMEA conformity work PORTS here
rather than being rebuilt.**
🔴 **The honest constraint, fifth confirmation: there is NO education-specific AI regulation anywhere
in LATAM (`gap 104`). Peru's `Ley 31814` is general-purpose. So compliance cannot be the pitch —
institutional governance, teacher capability and curriculum can.**
🟢 **The measured need: `13` of `19` LAC countries do not teach early AI (CENIA). That is the gap to
sell into, and `Latam-GPT` is the regional-sovereignty narrative to sell alongside it.**
🔴 **Stated plainly because it is this KB's own blind spot: after five passes, no open-source
education platform originating in LATAM has been named by any query. Delivery capability here is not
matched by regional open-source supply.**

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional thesis this pass supports: procurement is shifting from *"does it work"* to
*"can you evidence it"*, and that is a documentation-and-controls market before it is a model market.**
🟢 **Three independent signals agree — EMEA conformity duties, US state statutes requiring human
oversight, and APAC frameworks (AI Verify, Korea's high-impact classification) functioning as
procurement gates.**
🟢 **What Globant can build on today, licence-verified this pass: `OpenOLAT` (`Apache-2.0`,
institutional LMS, `1` d), `Kolibri` (`MIT`), `scorm-again` (`MIT`), `Selleo/mentingo` (`MIT`, and it
ships a working dependency-licence gate), `anki-mcp-server` (`MIT`), `ai-shifu` (`Apache-2.0`, `0` d).**
🔴 **What is still missing globally, and is a real hole rather than a search failure: no permissive
SIS (ninth pass), no education-vertical permissive agent added in several passes (`gap 113`), and no
APAC or LATAM originated platform at all.**
🔴 **The global licence caution that now has numbers behind it: the licence NAME on a repository
describes its outer grant only. `Sakai` carries `129` grant bodies, `Moodle` `193`. Budget the inner
audit, and run it with a content test rather than a filename match (`T213`, `P170-A`).**

### 🆕 🟢 `T210` — `gap 108` CLOSES, and it closes by being dissolved

🟢 **p168 recorded two readings *"`15` months apart on a duty that is live either way"*. p169's sweep
returned `9` independent sources and they do not disagree:**

| claim | value | agreement |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Article 50(1), (2) and (5) applicable from** | 🟢 **`2 August 2026`** | 🟢 **multiple sources, consistent** |
| 🟢 **Commission guidelines on Article 50 adopted** | 🟢 **`20 July 2026`** | 🟡 single source |
| 🔴 **transitional for the `50(2)` MACHINE-READABLE MARKING limb** | 🔴 **`2 December 2026` — and ONLY for generative systems placed on the market BEFORE `2 August 2026`** | 🟢 **two sources, consistent in scope** |
| 🔴 **penalty exposure** | 🔴 **up to `EUR 15 000 000` or `3%` of worldwide annual turnover, whichever is higher** | 🟡 single source |
| 🔴 **Article 113's own text** | 🔴 **NOT QUOTED BY ANY SOURCE** | 🔴 **`0` of `9`** |

🔵 **The error was this KB's, and naming it is the point: a duty's APPLICATION DATE and a grandfathering
window for one limb of it are different objects. Put side by side without their scopes they read as a
contradiction. `gap 108` was manufactured, not discovered.**
🔴 **What stays open, narrower — `gap 110`: Article 113 is the provision that SETS application dates and
not one source quotes it. `2 August 2026` rests on nine secondary sources agreeing, which is not the
same as reading the article.** 🟢 **`eur-lex.europa.eu` is `ENOTFOUND` on `WebFetch` and `000` on `curl`
— no DNS and no route, NOT a policy denial. One fetch from a container with egress closes it.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The engagement with the most statutory pull on this page.** 🟢 **`Ohio HB 96` and `Tennessee
SB 1711` push policy authorship down to every district, and `Maryland`'s Act dates it — `24` districts,
`120` days.** 🟢 **Sell the policy-and-controls engagement, not the tutor: a per-state compliance matrix
over `134` bills and `33`–`35` guidance documents, instantiated as district templates.**
🔴 **Two hard constraints to design to, both statutory: California `AB 1159` forbids training on student
data, and Oklahoma and Maryland require human oversight with AI barred from high-stakes decisions.
A product that grades autonomously or trains on tenant data is unsellable in those states.**
🔴 **And the honest objection to lead with: New York City, the largest district in the country, has
suspended student-facing generative AI for elementary and middle school. The sellable shape is
TEACHER-facing with the teacher in the loop — which is also what the first negative efficacy datum this
KB holds (p168) recommends on pedagogical grounds.**
🟢 **The funded framing is AI LITERACY: the ED grant-priority rule (`April 2026`), `H.R. 8747`, and
`Alabama HB 329`'s graduation requirement all point the same way — `P168-C`'s `MIT` courseware tier is
the asset, and it is already translated.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Sell conformity work, because this is the region where the rules are mapped and the uptake is
not.** 🟢 **Annex III makes admissions screening, outcome assessment and learning-path steering
high-risk, with obligations from `2 December 2027` — far enough out to build, close enough to budget.**
🔴 **Article 50 is LIVE NOW (`2 August 2026`), and education sits squarely inside it: every
AI-generated assignment, feedback note and synthetic teaching asset. The `2 December 2026` marking
transitional applies only to systems already on the market before `2 August 2026` (`T210`) — for
anything new it is simply in force.**
🔴 **One feature class must be removed rather than documented: emotion inference is PROHIBITED in
educational contexts. Engagement detection and attention tracking are not a compliance task.**
🟢 **Where the demand is: `4` of `23` member states had AI-in-education policies as of the Council of
Europe's survey, and a binding Council of Europe instrument on AI in education is in progress — so
ministry-level policy and readiness work is unmet, with a second legal layer coming to justify it.**
🟢 **The architecture that fits: `Open edX`'s `AGPL-3.0` RPC carve-out behind an API boundary, with
`Sakai` as the permissive route — and `ECL-2.0` decided in writing before the client's scanner stops on
it (`T208`, `P169-C`).**

### APAC

🟢 **The timing argument is Korea's: the AI Basic Act is in force, education is a *"high-impact"* use,
and MSIT has signalled `2026` as a pilot period with limited enforcement. That is the cheapest window
in the world right now to build a compliant system and be early rather than retrofitted.**
🔴 **Vietnam is the opposite posture and should be priced that way: Law No. `134/2025/QH15` names
education high-risk by statute, including automated assessment, and reaches foreign providers.**
🔴 **China's synthetic-content labelling and algorithm filings are ENFORCED today, and they bear
directly on AI-generated courseware — the operational work is provenance and marking, the same
machinery EU Article 50(2) needs. Build it once, sell it in both regions.**
🟡 **Segment on consent, not geography: ANZ shows higher support for banning AI in schools than the
Asian markets surveyed, so an ANZ engagement should be scoped like an EMEA one.**
🔴 **The standing sourcing gap, stated rather than left silent: `14` consecutive passes have named `0`
APAC-origin open-source education platforms. Any APAC build starts from a global or EMEA codebase, and
a client who wants local provenance cannot be served from this shelf today.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The clearest arbitrage on this page, and it is a governance gap rather than a technology gap:
`87%` of institutions using AI against `26%` with a formal strategy — a `61`-point spread on the same
`200`-institution population.** 🟢 **Sell AI strategy, governance and teacher enablement into demand
that already exists and does not need to be created.**
🟢 **The teacher base is the most AI-active measured anywhere in this KB: Brazil `56%`, Chile `55%`,
Colombia `53%`, Costa Rica `52%` against an OECD average of `36%`, and Uruguay's Ceibal at `75%`.
Teacher-facing tooling has a willing population, which is exactly the shape the negative efficacy
datum and the North American statutes both argue for.**
🟢 **The reference to cite is in-region: PUC Chile's `194` pedagogical agents over `~100` courses and
`4 600+` students. Outcomes are NOT yet measured — so propose the measurement as the differentiator
rather than repeating the claim.**
🟡 **`gap 104`, fourth confirmation, read as an opportunity with a deadline: there is no
education-specific AI regulation anywhere in the region, so an institution's own policy IS the
governing instrument — and Brazil's `PL 2.338/2023` and Chile's bill are both moving. Work done now
becomes the compliance baseline when they land.**
🟡 **Where to anchor institutionally: UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the
Caribbean (launched `14 April 2026`) and its capacity-building programmes in Ecuador and Chile.**
🔴 **What cannot be served: no `2026` LATAM vendor or investment report was returned on any query, so
there is no competitive map for this region and a pitch cannot be positioned against named local
rivals.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T203` — the verification rule this KB publishes cannot be executed in this container

🔴 **The standing quality bar says *"verify every URL before writing it (`curl -sI`)"*. Measured
against `26` hosts and channels, the available surface is:**

| channel | result | what it can verify |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 `git ls-remote` / `git clone` | 🟢 **open** | 🟢 **repo existence, default branch, HEAD sha, tags, and with a clone every file** |
| 🟢 `raw.githubusercontent.com` | 🟢 **`200`** | 🟢 **any file in any public repo, which is how every licence body and source line this pass cites was read** |
| 🟢 `apache.org`, `pypi.org`, `registry.npmjs.org` | 🟢 **`200`** | 🟢 **canonical Apache text; package registries** |
| 🔴 `github.com` HTML *(curl)* · `api.github.com` · `codeload` · `gnu.org` | 🔴 **`403` policy denial** | 🔴 **nothing — and this is the measured cause of the KB's long-standing *"github-api-proxy-blocked"*** |
| 🟡 `github.com` HTML *(WebFetch)* | 🟡 **open, though `403` on `curl`** | 🟡 **directory listings — the two channels DISAGREE, which is itself worth holding** |
| 🔴 `github.com/search` | 🔴 **`0` results on every channel** | 🔴 **repo DISCOVERY is closed, so no row can enter by search this pass** |
| 🔴 every other web host | 🔴 **`000` / `ENOTFOUND`** | 🔴 **nothing. `example.com` and `wikipedia.org` fail too, so this is not about the sources chosen** |

🔵 **Consequence, stated so a reader does not mistake it for absent effort: every regional and
market figure below is tier `search-summary` BY NECESSITY. `T160`'s promotion rule holds — none of
it is promotable until a pass runs where the primary is reachable.**
🟢 **The honest division of this pass: `4` actions, `3` new `T`-numbers and every licence finding
rest on channels that ARE open. Only the market and regulatory prose does not.**

education sits, apply from `2 December 2027`. Annex I embedded high-risk moves to `2 August 2028`.

*"one named instrument, one unreachable primary"* — a verification task a future pass can finish in
one fetch instead of re-litigating.**
🆕 🔴 **A NEW contradiction opens in its place (`gap 108`): on Article 50 transparency, one source
puts the `50(2)` watermarking grace period at `2 December 2026` and another says Article 50 has
applied since `2 August 2026`. Education is squarely inside Article 50 — every AI-generated
assignment, feedback note and synthetic teaching asset — so the two readings are `15` months apart
on a duty that is live either way.**

🟢 **By institution type: private non-profit `84%` implementing, public `68%`, private for-profit

`CONPES 4144` is a policy rather than a statute. None is education-specific.**

### 🔴 `T168` — the market stays incoherent, and p168 can now say WHY in one line

| scope | 2026 | later | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟡 **AI in education, broad** | `$10.6B` | `$42.48B` by `2030`, `41.5%` CAGR | Research and Markets |
| 🆕 🔴 **AI TUTORS only** | `$2.7B` | `$17.7B` by `2033` | Grand View Research |

🔴 **A `3.9×` spread at the same base year.** 🟢 **The cause is now explicit and it is not
disagreement: these are different DENOMINATORS — one counts the whole sector's AI spend, the other
counts tutoring products. `T168`'s incoherence is a scope problem, and the only safe use is to
compare figures within one report and never across two.**
🟢 **The directional claim both agree on, and Technavio names it: the shift from *"experimental
generative AI"* to *"autonomous agentic workflows"* — which is `T182`, unchanged.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **Unchanged and still the best-specified build on this shelf: district rostering on
`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/edfi-oneroster` (`Apache-2.0`, `12` entities, `24` read-only GETs, per-scope
authorisation — all measured at p167), with `openedx/edx-platform`'s RPC carve-out opening the
higher-education MOOC side.**
🆕 🟡 **New this pass, and it is a constraint rather than an opportunity: the regulatory surface is
converging on HUMAN OVERSIGHT and STUDENT-DATA boundaries, not on model quality — California
`AB 1159` would bar student data from training, Idaho `SB 1227` mandates privacy protections,
Oklahoma and Maryland bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students, and New York City has a
one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through `8`th grade.**
🔵 **Read for a build: every one of those is a requirement on ARCHITECTURE — provenance, an audit
trail, a human decision point and data residency — which is exactly what `P167-B`'s read-only
rostering posture already gives. Sell the audit trail, not the model.**
🟢 **Federal movement, first time this KB holds it: the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026
(`H.R. 8747`) cleared House committee markup on `21 July 2026` and would let schools spend federal
funds on AI literacy.** 🆕 🟢 **If it passes, the deliverable it funds is COURSEWARE — and `T199`
found three `MIT`-licensed AI-literacy curricula this pass, `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`
among them, with an `es` locale already shipped.**
🔴 **Unchanged caution: `~134` bills across `31` states and `60%` teacher use are
`search-summary` figures, now measured as UNVERIFIABLE from this container rather than merely
unverified (`T203`).**

### EMEA

🟢 **The instrument is named for the first time: `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, in force
`27 July 2026`, moving Annex III high-risk — where education sits — to `2 December 2027`.**
🔵 **That is a `14`-month build window on a date this KB has argued about for three passes, and the
posture it rewards is unchanged: AI that determines access, assesses learning outcomes or influences
an educational path is high-risk, so the deliverable is the conformity evidence as much as the
feature.** 🟢 **Article 4's AI-literacy duty is already live and has been since `2 February 2025` —
a sellable engagement TODAY that needs no new regulation.**
🔴 **Two hard edges: AI that infers EMOTIONS in educational institutions is a PROHIBITED practice,
which rules out the engagement-detection features clients ask for by name; and `gap 108` opens on
whether Article 50's transparency duty bites now or from `2 December 2026`.**
🆕 🔴 **And the contribution-side finding that lands here hardest: `Sakai` is `ECL-2.0`, whose
patent grant is narrowed for contributors EMPLOYED BY AN INSTITUTION (`T200`). EMEA
higher-education engagements are precisely institutional-employment engagements.**
🟢 **The platform map is otherwise unchanged and strong: `ILIAS` for German-speaking public-sector
procurement, `OpenOLAT` as the `Apache-2.0` route, `Moodle` everywhere, all three with licence
bodies now READ rather than banded.**
🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged: AFRICA and the MIDDLE EAST returned nothing education-specific again.
Every EMEA finding above is EU. This is a measured silence, not coverage.**

### APAC

🟢 **Promoted from a measured silence to a named map this pass.** 🟢 **Two statutes now bound the
work: Korea's AI Basic Act (in force `January 2026`, education inside *"high-impact"*, with `2026`
signalled as a limited-enforcement pilot) and Vietnam's Law No. `134/2025/QH15` (`1 March 2026`),
whose implementing decision names EDUCATION high-risk and cites automated assessment and
behavioural monitoring as the examples.**
🔵 **Those two cites are the opportunity, read together: automated assessment is the single feature
most asked for and the single feature named as high-risk in both jurisdictions. The sellable
deliverable is assessment WITH a human decision point and an evidence trail, which is the same
architecture `North America`'s bills reward — one build, two regions.**
🟢 **The competitive map now has names: Squirrel AI, Byju's, plus Google, Microsoft, IBM and
Pearson.** 🟡 **And the region splits on consent: support for banning AI in schools is lower across
Asian markets but HIGHER in Australia and New Zealand, so ANZ should be scoped with EMEA-style
transparency expectations rather than with Asia.**
🔴 **Still `0` for a thirteenth pass: no APAC open-source education platform, and no Indian national
education-AI instrument — only a vendor name.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The demand side is now the best-evidenced of any region on this shelf: `87%` of `200`
institutions across `19` countries using AI in at least one area, `74%` in teaching and learning —
and `26%` with a formal strategy.** 🔵 **The engagement IS that gap. `61` points between use and
governance, on the same surveyed population, is an AI-governance-and-policy deliverable before it
is a build — strategy, acceptable-use, assessment integrity, data handling — and it is the one
offer that fits `74%` of the market rather than the `26%` that already has a plan.**
🟢 **`PUC Chile`'s `194` pedagogical agents over `~100` courses and `4 600+` students is the
reference to quote, and quoting it honestly INCLUDES that its learning outcomes are not yet
measured — which is itself the second engagement: measurement.**
🟢 **Teacher-level demand is real and above OECD average in four countries (Brazil `56%`, Chile
`55%`, Colombia `53%`, Costa Rica `52%` vs `36%`), and Uruguay's Ceibal reports `75%`.**
🔴 **`gap 104` holds a third time: no education-specific AI regulation anywhere in the region.
Brazil's `PL 2.338/2023` is still in the Chamber, Chile's bill is in its first constitutional stage,
Colombia's `CONPES 4144` is policy. Governance is institutional — UNESCO's regional Observatory,
launched `14 April` — not statutory.**
🔵 **Which cuts both ways and should be said plainly to a client: there is no compliance deadline
forcing the work, so it will be bought on assessment integrity, teacher workload and institutional
risk appetite — not on a regulator's calendar.**
🟢 **Delivery note, verified on the git channel rather than claimed: `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`
(`MIT`) ships `es`, `pt-BR` and `pt-PT` locales, so an AI-literacy programme for a LATAM institution
starts from translated `MIT` courseware instead of a translation budget.**

### Global

🟢 **The cross-region finding of this pass, and it is a build rather than an observation:
AUTOMATED ASSESSMENT is simultaneously the most-requested education-AI feature and the named
high-risk trigger in three independent legal traditions — EU Annex III (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`,
from `2 December 2027`), Vietnam's Law No. `134/2025/QH15` (`1 March 2026`, citing automated
assessment by name), Korea's AI Basic Act (`January 2026`, education inside *"high-impact"*), and
the US state bills barring AI from high-stakes student decisions (Oklahoma, Maryland).**
🔵 **So one architecture satisfies all four: a human decision point, an evidence trail, a
data boundary, and provenance on anything generated. That is `P167-B`'s read-only rostering posture
generalised — and it means a compliance build quoted for one region is reusable in the others
rather than re-scoped.**
🟢 **The permissive foundation for it is unchanged and now fully body-read:
`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/edfi-oneroster` (`Apache-2.0`) for rostering, `Selleo/mentingo` (`MIT`) or
`openolat/OpenOLAT` (`Apache-2.0`) for delivery, `jcputney/scorm-again` (`MIT`) for SCORM runtime
including the `2004` manifests `mentingo` cannot emit (`P168-B`), and the three `MIT` AI-literacy
curricula of `P168-C` for the training layer, already translated into `53` locales.**
🔴 **And the constraint that applies in every region: `0` of the `12` agent-shelf rows
speaks `LTI`, `SCORM`, `xAPI`, `OneRoster`, `Ed-Fi`, `QTI` or `Caliper` — re-confirmed this pass on
`13` terms instead of `5`. Every institutional integration is still a build, everywhere.**

orders of magnitude below the global forecasts in the same sweep, which is itself the clearest

the absence of binding education rules together argue for `P164-C`'s provenance port and

no regulation yet requires, and say so.**
🟡 **`Selleo/mentingo` (`MIT`) and `edfi-oneroster` (`Apache-2.0`) remain the two permissive
starting points with no copyleft obligation, which matters more where public-sector procurement
cannot absorb legal review.**

## Pass 165 — 2026-10-13

🟢 **Eight queries, run globally and once per region.**
🆕 🟢 **North America produced a CORRECTION rather than another statistic: p164's
*"education EXEMPTED in Colorado"* lead is REFUTED, and what actually exists is a
deemed-compliance safe harbour — which is billable work, not an exemption (`T177`).**

attached, or do not quote it.**

### 🔵 A note on what this page can and cannot be, now stated as policy rather than as a complaint

🟢 **This page's regional and regulatory content is `search-summary` and, on the evidence of
many passes, will stay that way: the primary publishers it needs — EU institutions, UNESCO,
standards bodies, law firms — do not answer in this environment, while the git and
package-registry channels answer reliably.**
🟢 **So the division of labour is explicit:**

| class of fact | channel | standard it is held to |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **repository / code** | 🟢 **git, licence bodies, package registries — primary** | 🟢 **MEASURED. Never summarised, and quantified where it matters** |
| 🔴 **market / regulatory** | 🔴 **search summaries** | 🔴 **labelled `search-summary`, NAME the instrument, and tell the client to verify at source with counsel** |

🔵 **Written down because it is the honest shape of the deliverable: naming
`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` and saying *"verify this with your counsel"* is worth more to a client
than a confident citation this shelf cannot stand behind.**

### 🆕 🔴 `T143` — a regional figure can arrive attached to the WRONG REGION, and only a second query catches it

🔴 **This pass's APAC query returned the Digital Education Council's `76% → 67%` faculty-intent
decline attributed to ASIA-PACIFIC, described as *"the lowest future AI adoption intent among
all regions"*.** 🟢 **This shelf holds that figure as US+CANADA.** 🟢 **A second, targeted query
settled it: the decline is US+Canada, *"the lowest of any region DEC tracked"*, and the APAC
number from the same instrument is the `57%` enthusiasm figure, not an intent decline.**
🟢 **The shelf's attribution was CORRECT and is unchanged.**
🔵 **`T143`:** 🔴 **the aggregator layer re-regionalises figures, so a regional claim from a
search summary is not evidence of REGION — only of the figure.** 🟢 **The gate: when a summary
attaches a held figure to a different region, re-query naming the instrument before touching
the shelf.**

p153 established that the four 2026-base figures *"disagree about what counts as a market."*
🟢 **This pass can say something stronger and more useful: the disagreement is LARGER between

| route to a North America 2026-ish number | figure | implied CAGR |
|---|---|---|

global AI-in-education market", it sells into a region. 🟢 **So the number a client will be
quoted at them is precisely the one with the widest disagreement**, and the defensible move is
to quote adoption and governance rates — which multiple independent publishers agree on to
within a few points — and refuse the currency figure. 🔴 **Eight passes of refusing it is now
a position, not a gap.**

### North America

#### p165

🟢 **Colorado is CORRECTED, and the correction is the opposite of what p164's lead suggested.**
🔴 **p164's open-gaps recorded a tracker claim that Colorado had *"repealed its AI Act and carved
out FERPA-covered institutions — i.e. education EXEMPTED"*.** 🔴 **That is wrong in the part that
matters.**

| what p164 carried | what p165's sources say |
|---|---|
| 🔴 *"repealed"* | 🟢 **CORRECT — `SB 26-189`, signed `2026-05-14`, repeals AND REPLACES the 2024 Colorado AI Act; effective `2027-01-01`** |
| 🔴 *"education exempted"* | 🔴 **WRONG. Education remains in scope — the law reaches decisions affecting *educational enrollment or opportunity*, and sources state it imposes NO exemption for public or nonprofit educational institutions** |
| — | 🟢 **What actually exists is a DEEMED-COMPLIANCE SAFE HARBOUR: a FERPA-covered institution giving FERPA-compliant notices is deemed to satisfy the notice requirement, and an institution with a FERPA-compliant correction and human-review process is deemed to satisfy human review** |

🆕 **`T177`:** 🔵 **an exemption and a deemed-compliance safe harbour are not the same thing, and
the difference is the whole engagement.** 🔴 **An exemption means no work. A safe harbour means the
institution must HAVE a FERPA-aligned notice and a documented human-review-and-correction process,
and must be able to show it.** 🟢 **For a studio that is a concrete, scoped build: notice
generation, a correction workflow, and the audit trail that evidences human review —
which is exactly what `P160-B`, `P161-A` and `P163-C` already construct.**
🟡 **Also noted: the replacement is LIGHTER — it reframes around *"automated decision-making
technology"* and drops the impact-assessment and risk-management-framework duties of the 2024 Act.**

🟡 **Federal and state movement, labelled as search-summary:** 🟡 **`134` AI-in-education bills
across `31` states in 2026; California `AB 1159` (limiting student data for model training) and
Idaho `SB 1227` (privacy protections for school AI tools) named as the data-privacy archetypes;
Oklahoma and Maryland reported to require human oversight and to bar AI from high-stakes decisions
about students; the `K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026` (`H.R. 8747`) advanced from
committee on `2026-07-21`.** 🟡 **District practice is diverging sharply — Katy ISD bans generative
AI for K-6 with scaffolded access to grade 12; New York City is reported to hold a one-year
moratorium on student-facing AI through grade 8.**

🟢 **Opportunity, stated as work rather than as a market:** 🟢 **the regulatory shape in North
America is converging on three artefacts — a notice, a human-review-and-correction process, and a
provable audit trail. All three are buildable, none requires a model, and `SB 26-189`'s
`2027-01-01` effective date gives a real deadline to sell against.**

### EMEA

#### p165

🟢 **The Annex III date now has a NAMED INSTRUMENT, which is a real advance on p164 — and it is
still not verifiable from this session, for the reason this page has already documented across
several passes: the primary publishers it needs do not answer here.**

| field | p164 | p165 |
|---|---|---|
| 🟡 the claim | 🟡 *"the Digital Omnibus delays Annex III high-risk obligations to `2027-12-02`"* | 🟡 **unchanged, and now corroborated across several independent law-firm and compliance sources** |
| 🔴 the instrument | 🔴 **unnamed** | 🟢 **NAMED: reported as `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, published `2026-07-24`, in force `2026-07-27`** |
| 🔴 verification | 🔴 *"all three EU hosts refused at the proxy"* | 🔴 **STILL UNVERIFIED — and the channel closure is already documented and is not EU-specific** |
| 🟡 internal consistency | 🔴 **sources disagreed on a neighbouring date** | 🟡 **one source still describes the Omnibus as a PROPOSAL in negotiation; the others as adopted law. Treat as a strong lead, not a finding** |

🟡 **What the sources agree on, all labelled search-summary:** 🟡 **Annex III standalone high-risk
obligations move from `2026-08-02` to `2026-12-02`→`2027-12-02`; education sits in Annex III
point 3 (admission and access, assignment to institutions, evaluation of learning outcomes where
those outcomes steer the learning, level assessment, and monitoring for prohibited behaviour
during tests); Article 50 transparency is UNCHANGED on its original schedule; the machine-readable
marking grace period for existing generative systems is reported as `2026-12-02`.**

🟢 **The two things that do NOT move, and they are what keeps this shelf's EMEA work valid either
way:** 🟢 **(1) the emotion-recognition ban in education has been in force since `2025-02-02` and
is reported unaffected — it is live TODAY; (2) every code finding on this shelf is a fact about a
repository. `T161`, `T170`, `T171`, `T172` and the OpenOLAT provenance schema do not depend on a
deadline.**

🔵 **The engagement line, unchanged and now better founded: a delay is a budget for doing it
properly, not a reason to stop.** 🟢 **And the honest client sentence is *"this date must be
verified with your counsel against the published regulation"* — which this shelf can now say
while naming the instrument to check.**

🟢 **Where EMEA is the strongest market for what this shelf has actually built:** 🟢 **OpenOLAT is
Swiss-originated, `Apache-2.0`, and now measured to carry LTI 1.3 and SCORM in-tree plus
per-correction AI-grading provenance (`T165`, p164).** 🟢 **That combination — permissive licence,
standards interop, and an audit schema that already ships — is precisely the Annex III evidence

### APAC

#### p165

🟡 **APAC gains two dated statutes and keeps its defining property: it is the region where
*"compliance"* has no single meaning, and that is itself the commercial opportunity.**

`2026-01`, after a one-year preparation period, and names education among its *"high-impact AI"*
domains alongside healthcare, finance and employment.** 🟡 **Vietnam is reported to have enacted an
AI law effective `2026-03-01`, with systems already operating before `2026-08-15` in healthcare,
education and finance receiving an additional six-month grace period — though one source instead
describes it as a *Digital Technology Industry Law*, so the name and status need checking.**
🟡 **Japan maintains a light-touch voluntary model; Japan and Taiwan are drafting laws while
standing up testing and assurance institutes; China already enforces algorithm filings and
synthetic-content labelling; Indonesia and Thailand are moving toward binding frameworks that
name education among covered sectors.**

🟡 **Adoption and sentiment:** 🟡 **UNESCO reports readiness varying widely — China and Singapore
have established AI-in-education policies while other countries are still meeting basic
educational needs.** 🟡 **The Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 finds LOWER support for banning AI in
schools across the Asian markets surveyed, while Australia and New Zealand record HIGHER support
for a ban** — 🔵 **a split worth carrying, because it means an ANZ engagement and a Southeast Asian
engagement face opposite public-opinion headwinds.**
🟡 **Named commercial players: Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's. Grand View Research
projects `28.1%` CAGR `2026`–`2033` off a `2025` base of `$2 282.9M`** — 🔴 **subject to `T168`'s
scope warning, below.**

🟢 **The opportunity, and APAC's is structurally different from every other region:** 🟢 **a
multi-jurisdiction institution here must satisfy Korea's high-impact regime, Vietnam's grace
period, China's labelling rules and Japan's voluntary posture AT ONCE.** 🟢 **That is an
argument for policy-as-configuration rather than policy-as-code: one audited pipeline whose
gates are data, with per-jurisdiction rule sets.** 🟢 **`P158-A` already makes the rubric data
rather than a prompt, and `P163-A` already handles non-Latin writing systems — the two
APAC-critical pieces exist.**

#### p160

🆕 🔴 **`T137` — the channel boundary is now understood, not just observed.** 🟢 **`rate_limit`
answers `200` with the full quota unspent, while `search/repositories` and even
`repos/{owner}/{repo}` answer `403` with *"sessions are bound to their configured
repositories."*** 🔵 **So the refusal is a repository-SCOPE policy: it cannot be waited out, and
the only response is to change instrument.**
🟢 **What replaced it this pass, and it produced every finding on this page's sibling files:**
🟢 **`git ls-remote` for existence and default branch, `--depth=1` clones for tree AND content,
`raw.githubusercontent.com` for licence bodies, `git log -1` for recency.** 🔴 **What is gone:
stars, topics, forks, `created_at` — so attention-ranked discovery is closed until a pass has API
access.**

### 🔴 Why the market-size spread is a STRUCTURAL fact and not a research failure

🔵 **The shelf has refused to quote a figure for seven passes and the refusal is starting to
look like timidity. It is not, and this pass can finally say why.** The four 2026-base
figures do not disagree about the market; **they disagree about what counts as one**:

| figure | base year | publisher class | what it appears to count |
|---|---|---|---|
| **$10.6B → $42.48B by 2030** (CAGR **41.5%**) | 2026 | market-research house | AI-in-education software and services, global |
| **$12.3B** | 2026 | vendor blog, *attributed* to an analyst | 🔴 **attribution UNVERIFIABLE against the named analyst's own page** |
| **~$6B (2024) → $32B by 2030** | 2024 | sector research report | a narrower K-12-weighted base |
| **$3.68B — 36% of global** | 2026 | statistics roundup | 🔴 **implies a $10.2B global, close to the first row — the only internal consistency found** |

🟢 **The one consistency worth keeping**: a **36% North American share** of a **~$10.2B**
2026 global is arithmetically compatible with the **$10.6B** row. 🔴 **Two secondary
sources agreeing by arithmetic is not a primary**, so nothing is adopted — but the pair is
recorded, because it is the first time any two figures on this page have been reconcilable
at all.

🔴 **And the figure a studio actually needs does not exist in any of them: the open-source
share.** 🟢 Seven passes, zero named open-source vendors — which is the gap this shelf
fills by measuring repositories instead of quoting markets.

### 🔴 `T88` — how five passes missed a date change, and the mechanism is reusable

🔴 **The omnibus never says "education".** It amends an annex; education sits *inside* the
annex. 🔴 **Every query this shelf has run named education** — so the instrument that moved
the date was invisible to all of them, and the shelf kept re-confirming a superseded figure
from education-sector sources that had not caught up either.

🔴 **The exposure was worse than a stale figure.** This shelf used `2026-08-02` to CORRECT
the market, writing that a wrong date appearing *"in five passes means the wrong date is the
market's MAJORITY reading"* and that *"the client's installed advisor probably gave them the
wrong deadline."* 🔴 **The shelf was handing out a superseded date with the confidence of a
correction.**

🟢 **The rule that comes out of it, and it is cheap:** 🔵 **for any dated obligation this
shelf publishes, query the INSTRUMENT (the regulation, the act, the decree) and not only the
SECTOR.** 🟢 One query naming *"Digital Omnibus"* found it; five passes of
*"AI education EMEA regulation"* did not.

🔴 **Provenance, stated plainly and not dressed up.** No primary text was read and **no
secondary source could be fetched**: `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `gibsondunn.com`, `orrick.com`,
`lewissilkin.com`, `uniwise.eu`, `legal500.com`, `secureprivacy.ai` and
`artificialintelligenceact.eu` all refuse — 🟢 **the same `ENOTFOUND` / `CONNECT 403` event
p151 established as one thing**, and the hosts are NOT re-registered — not retried host by host (the **categorical-egress rule** — the denial is an allowlist, not a per-host fault — defined on `intel/open-gaps.md`).
🟢 **What carries the finding: nine independent publishers agreeing on the regulation
NUMBER, both annex dates, the OJ date and the in-force date.** 🔵 **That convergence is
stronger than anything this page has on a market size** — 🔴 **and it is still secondary.**

  🔵 No Africa query was run this pass; 🟢 the omission is budget, and it is named.
- 🔴 **The market-size spread is untouched** — `$6.4B` / `$7.52B` / `$8.3B` for a completed
  2025 — 🟢 and no figure is carried.
- 🟡 **The Art 50(2) watermarking date (`2026-12-02` for systems on market at `2026-08-02`)

### 🟢 `ACTION FV` — the reconciliation, in full

> 🔴 **`ACTION FV`** — reconcile Vietnam's three compliance dates; find a second source for
> the **September 2027** high-risk education deadline or withdraw it by name.
> 🔴 *Refuted if no second source exists* — which would put the figure in `P148-K`'s class.

🔴 **The falsifier did NOT fire.** Four independent publishers carry the regime, and one of
them quotes the decision's own operative sentence:

| element | value | where |
|---|---|---|
| instrument | **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg** (Prime Minister) | 🔴 primary host **403 at CONNECT**; read through the publisher's quoted extract |
| takes effect | 🆕 **2026-08-15** | 🔴 **this shelf had `2026-03-01` — WITHDRAWN** |
| scope | **46** high-risk AI systems across **six** sectors | education; ethnicity and religion; healthcare; banking; litigation; transportation |
| deadline, systems ALREADY RUNNING at effect | 🟢 **2027-09-01** — healthcare, **education**, finance | *"providers and deployers shall be responsible for completing compliance obligations … Before September 01, 2027, for artificial intelligence systems in the fields of healthcare, education, and finance"* |
| deadline, systems started WITHIN SIX MONTHS of effect | 🟢 **2027-03-01** | and the same date for the other listed fields |
| during the transition | existing systems may keep running **unless** a competent state agency finds serious risk and orders suspension or termination | |

🟢 **So the "three readings" were three RUNGS.** An education system live before
2026-08-15 has until **2027-09-01**; one launched between 2026-08-15 and 2027-02-15 has
until **2027-03-01** — 🔴 **the later-launched system has the EARLIER deadline**, which is
counter-intuitive and is exactly why three dates looked like a contradiction. 🔵 **What it
changes in a room**: an engagement that *replaces* a legacy Vietnamese education system
moves the client from the September rung to the March rung. Say so before the proposal,
not after.

🔴 **One thing still unresolved and not to be quoted: the number of conformity pathways.**
One publisher describes two under the AI Law; another lists three, with third-party
certification mandatory for the highest-risk systems. 🔵 **Two sources, two counts, no
primary text — so no count is carried.**

### 🟢 `ACTION FI` — the verdict, and why "not refuted" is the honest word

🔴 **Falsifier:** *"refuted if all three return policy documents only"* — which would have
established Africa's education-AI evidence base as **policy-only by nature at this date** and
made the figure gap permanent rather than outstanding.

🟢 **It did not fire. Kenya returns three figures** — a peer-reviewed teacher survey (**308**
respondents, **37 of 47** counties), a **~35%** school-connectivity baseline, and a **23.3%**
learner completion rate from the OIAI trial. 🔴 **Nigeria and South Africa return policy and a
vendor training target only.** 🔵 **So the correct verdict is partial: the action's claim is
true of one of the three countries, and the other two behave exactly as the consequence clause
described.**

🔴 **Two honest qualifications, because they bear on whether these are quotable:**

- 🔴 **The Kenyan teacher survey predates this page's window** — roughly 2024, not 2026. 🟢 **It
  is the most rigorous Africa figure on this shelf and it is not current.**
- 🔴 **The one continental study declines to be representative.** UNESCO **IICBA**/**ICHEI**,
  *Digitalization and the Use of Artificial Intelligence in Higher Education in Africa: An
  Exploratory Study*, **January 2026** — its authors caution the sample is small,
  online-recruited and **not representative**. 🔵 **A named, bounded limitation is far more
  useful to a client than a null, and it is what Africa's evidence base actually is.**

🔴 **The primary PDF was not read.** `www.ichei.org` and three more UNESCO hosts answer **403
at CONNECT** — confirmed this pass from the proxy's own refusal ledger (`connect_rejected`),
which also settles that `WebFetch`'s `ENOTFOUND` is the same event. 🟢 **The UNESCO hosts join
the standing environment requests and are NOT re-registered.**

- 🔴 **No Africa adoption rate, spend figure or deployment count.** Connectivity and a

  figure" and stays open.**
- 🔴 **Every North America adoption number this pass is aggregator-sourced** and none traced

- 🔴 **Vietnam's compliance date now has three readings and the primary text is 403.** 🟢 **No
  published figure moved.** 🔵 **`ACTION FV`.**

### 🟢 `P149-B` — the three Vietnamese education entries, NAMED

🟡 **`ACTION EW` is NOT discharged**: 🔴 **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg itself remains unread** and

🔵 **Entry 2 is Korea's whole education scope** (Art. 2(4)(j), student evaluation) and
🔵 **entry 3 is affect-adjacent inference**, which EU education prohibits outright rather than
merely classifying. 🟢 **So the three jurisdictions are not three strictnesses of one rule**:
Vietnam is broadest in *kind*, the EU strictest in *consequence*, Korea narrowest in *scope*.

🔴 **The qualifier `ACTION EW` was written to test is still not settled.** The action asked
whether a *"sole basis for a decision, no human in the loop"* condition applies to the
education entries. 🔴 **It is not shown to**, and 🟡 **a narrowing of some kind does exist**:
Allen & Gledhill state that *"not all AI systems used in these sectors will automatically be
classified as high-risk."* 🔵 **So `T71` keeps a caveat rather than losing one** — the
narrowing is confirmed to exist and its text is unread, which is a sharper exposure than
p148 recorded, not a softer one.

Decision 33 says **"finance"** in an article and **"banking"** in the appendix, and does not
reconcile them. 🔵 **Recorded because it bears on how confidently any secondary reading of
this decision should be quoted**, education included.

**2026-08-15**, 46 systems across six sectors, education **three**, compliance

for *every* host including reachable ones, and a bare `curl` reports what looks like a DNS
error. 🟢 **The discriminating signal is the proxy's own relay log**, which names each refusal
`connect_rejected — gateway answered 403 to CONNECT (policy denial or upstream failure)`.
🔵 **`aibasicact.kr ENOTFOUND`, recorded by p148, is very likely the same 403** and should not
be cited as evidence the host is dead.
