`codeload.github.com`, `registry.npmjs.org`. 🔵 **So the five-pass `curl -sI github.com` 403

that does not. 🟢 **`git clone` 50 of 50 and `npm pack` 8 of 8 this pass.**

government legal text should be registered again** until the allowlist changes. 🟢 **Law-firm

only, and everyone else is guidance. 🔴 **That story is now wrong in the commercially
relevant direction.**

|---|---|---|---|
| **EU** | AI Act Annex III, as amended by **Reg. (EU) 2026/1744** | **four** use cases, **every level** | **2027-12-02** |

| **China** | curriculum mandate | AI compulsory **from age 6** | reported from **2025-09** |
| **Taiwan** | AI Basic Act (**2025-12**) | 🟡 **unread** | 🟡 unread |

🔵 **Three of the four APAC dates land before the EU's.** 🔴 **p148 said the nearest was
"twenty-one months earlier than Annex III" and that figure is WITHDRAWN** — see
`P149-A` below. 🟢 **Corrected: the earliest APAC date is Korea's ~2027-01, eleven months
before Annex III; Vietnam's general 2027-03-01 is nine months before, and Vietnam's
education-specific deadline of 2027-09-01 is three.** 🟢 **An APAC engagement is still the
one with the nearest compliance deadline, not the one with the loosest rules**, and
Vietnam's framing is the EU's (a listed high-risk sector with conformity assessment) rather
than Korea's (one named use case).

### 🟡 `ACTION EL` — `T58` stands, and a citation in this KB is one of two translations

🟢 **`T58`'s scope claim is confirmed by independent sources.** Korea's AI Basic Act
**Article 2(4)(j)** names **student evaluation in early childhood, elementary and secondary
education**, cross-referencing **Article 9(1)** of the education framework statute.
🟢 **Higher education is not named, so `T58`'s "Korean higher education sits outside the
category while EU higher education is inside Annex III" holds.**

🆕 🟡 **The statute's NAME differs between translations.** p146 wrote *"Framework Act on
Education"*, following two commentaries. 🔴 **The Georgetown CSET translation — which p146
also cited as one of its four sources — calls the same cross-referenced statute the
"Basic Education Act."** 🔵 **Same article, same paragraph, two English names.** 🟢 **The
substance is unaffected; the citation is not settled, and this page now says so rather than
picking one silently.**

🔴 **The primary Korean text was not reached.** `aibasicact.kr` answers `ENOTFOUND` on the
WebFetch channel, as `capitol.texas.gov` does. 🔵 **Both are environment allowlist
requests, not findings** — `ACTION CW`'s shape, now with a second instance.

🟢 **The obligations side is corroborated**: operators must self-assess high-impact status
and may request confirmation from the Minister of Science and ICT (**Article 33**), with
duties on explanation, human oversight, user protection and record-keeping. 🟡 **One
commentary reads a human-in-the-loop requirement into assessment specifically; recorded as
a secondary interpretation, not adopted.**

### 🟢 The survey evidence firms up — a second publisher on LATAM, and a split on NA

🟢 **LATAM higher education now has two independent surveys agreeing on the SHAPE of the
gap**, which is the part a studio sells against:

| | UNESCO IESALC | Digital Education Council |
|---|---|---|
| population | **200 institutions, 19 countries**, Aug–Oct 2025 | LATAM higher education, **2026** |
| adoption | 🟢 **87% use AI in at least one area** | 🟢 **92% of students, 79% of faculty** |
| governance | 🔴 **26% have a formal framework** | 🔴 **19% of faculty use AI for feedback vs 50% of students who want it** |
| leading use | teaching & learning **73.5%**, research **57.0%**, administration **34.1%**, community **20.0%** | — |
| sector split | 🟡 **private non-profit 84% vs public 68%** | — |

🔵 **Two publishers, two instruments, one conclusion: adoption is near-universal and
governance is not.** 🟢 **That 87%-vs-26% gap is the single most quotable LATAM figure on
this page**, and the DEC survey's 50%-vs-19% feedback gap is the same shape at the
individual level. 🟢 **Named participants: Tecnológico de Monterrey (co-ran the DEC
survey), UNAM, UPC.**

🔴 **North America's equivalent pair disagrees, and the disagreement is the finding**:
student use reported at **92% in 2025** (up from 66% in 2024) against **only ~10% of
institutions holding formal AI guidelines** and **71% of US teachers reporting no AI
training**. 🔵 **Same gap as LATAM, measured on different units — one counts students, the
other counts institutions — so it is a parallel, not a comparison.**

### 🔴 Market-size figures: the spread widened and nothing is adopted

intelligence technology"*, barring districts from using AI to instruct students or to
replace or supplement a teacher's role. 🔴 **Its last recorded action is a 2025-03-14
referral to House Public Education**, and a 2026-09 Texas report describes it in the past
tense. 🔴 **One tracker labels it "Became Law" while its own last-action field contradicts
that.** 🆕 `T57`.

single most common pattern on this shelf. 🟢 **It does not exist** — but a page that quotes
trackers by their status label would have published it.

### 🆕 🟢 Why the four regional figures are worth more together than apart

🟢 **They come from one publisher, with one methodology, across four regions** — which is the

numbers comparable**: not their provenance quality, but the fact that one instrument produced
all of them. So the *ratio* carries information the levels do not:

| region (2024, publisher A) | figure | share of the four |
|---|---|---|
| North America | $951.0M | **46%** |
| Asia Pacific | $591.6M | **29%** |
| Europe | $512.6M | **25%** |

🟢 **Direction, adopted as direction only:** North America leads, APAC is second and growing
fastest of the three majors (20.9% vs 15.9%). 🔵 **That ordering is consistent with this
page's existing rows, which is corroboration of direction and not of magnitude.**

### 🔴 And the scope trap in the fourth row, stated before anyone quotes it

🔴 **"Rest of Latin America" is a RESIDUAL category, not LATAM.** Publishers using this label
break out the large national markets — typically Brazil and Mexico — as their own lines, and
"Rest of" is what remains. 🔴 **Quoting $18.2M as "the LATAM AI-in-education market" would
understate it by whatever Brazil and Mexico are**, and this page does not hold those two lines.

🔵 **This is `T30`'s rule in its original and still-correct form**: a figure's denominator is
part of the figure. 🟢 **The 0.9% above is therefore a floor on LATAM's share and not an
estimate of it** — and it is published as a floor.

## Market size — the spread was mostly a base-year error

🔴 **Correction, `P132-D`. This page has said: *"estimates for the 2026 market span ~$6.4B to
~$11.4B — a 1.8× spread for the current year, so quote the range and the disagreement."* The
~$6.4B row is not a 2026 estimate. It is IMARC's 2025 base**, and IMARC's own earlier edition reads
**$4.8B for 2024 → $75.1B by 2033**. One misfiled row manufactured most of the disagreement this
page was built around.

🟢 **Restricted to figures genuinely for 2026, the firms nearly agree — a 1.19× spread.** The
**base year is not where these sources disagree. The horizon is.**

| year | estimate | forecast | implied CAGR | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2026** | **$10.6B** | → $42.48B by 2030 | 40.9–41.5% | The Business Research Company (its own 2025 base: **$7.52B**) |
| **2026** | **$10.4B** | → $32.27B by 2030 | 31.2% | market-research firm |
| **2026** | **~$11B** | — | — | market-research blog |
| **2026** | **~$9.58B** | → ~$136.79B by 2035 | 🔴 **34.52% and 36.02% on the same page** | Precedence Research (its own 2025 base: **$7.05B**) |
| **2026** | $4.09B (**higher education only**) | → $13.46B by 2030 | 34.7% | market-research firm — *a segment, not the market* |
| 🔴 **2025** | ~~~$6.4B~~ | → $79.6B by 2034 | 31.35% (2026–34) | **IMARC — misfiled as a 2026 figure by earlier passes of this KB** |
| 2025 *(context)* | $7.05B · $7.52B · $8.3B · $8.51B · $9.8B · $5.50B | — | — | Precedence · TBRC · Grand View · various |

🆕 **Added by p135 — the row that reopens the current-year spread, and the CAGR table that
matters more:**

| year | estimate | forecast | implied CAGR | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2026** | 🔴 **$6.02B** | → $12.30B by 2032 | 🔴 **12.55%** | report aggregator (GII; underlying publisher not named on the page) — **the lowest 2026 figure on record here and the lowest CAGR by a factor of 3.6** |
| **2026** | **$11.4B** | → $57.2B by 2033 | 25.9% | Grand View Research (its own 2025 base: **$8.3B**; **North America 37.5%** share in 2025) |
| 2025 | $6.4B | → $79.6B by 2034 | 31.35% | IMARC — 🟢 **re-confirms p132's correction**: this is a 2025 base, exactly as `P132-D` said |
| 2025–30 | *expansion of* **+$3.37B** | — | 🔴 **45%** | Technavio — 🔵 **an expansion figure, not a market size.** Do not place it in a size column |

🔴 **The CAGR spread is the real finding, and it is wider than the size spread by any measure:**
**12.55%** · 25.9% · 31.35% · 40.9% · **45%**. 🟢 **Use this instead of the size range.** Two firms
pricing the same market today within ~20% of each other, while disagreeing 3.6-fold on how fast it
grows, is precisely why the 2035 figures span ~17×. The disagreement compounds; it does not start
large.

🔵 **A unit-discipline note, because this page has now been caught twice.** `P132-D` was a **base-year**
error. The Technavio row above is an adjacent trap — a *market expansion* quoted in the same units as
a *market size* — and the higher-education row is a third (**a segment, not the market**).
🟢 **Rule: before using a figure, check its base year, its unit, and its scope.** Two of three have
already produced a published error on this page.

**The 2035 end of the same sources, which is where the real disagreement lives:**
**~$70.55B** (SNS Insider) · **~$113.2B** (Research Nester) · **~$136.79B** (Precedence) ·
🔴 **~$1 169.44B** (Market Research Future). That is a **~17× spread** on the horizon against
**1.19×** on the current year.

**How to use this, revised:** 🟢 **Say that the firms agree within about 20% on what this market is
worth today (~$9.6–11B) and diverge seventeen-fold by 2035.** That is both true and far more useful
than "nobody agrees" — it tells a client the *current* opportunity is well-bounded and that any
ten-year business case built on a single forecast is arbitrary. Anchor commercial conversations on
the segment figure (higher ed, ~$4.09B in 2026), and 🔴 **always check a quoted figure's base year
before using it** — this page got that wrong for several passes. Note also that one source
publishes two different CAGRs for its own forecast (34.52% and 36.02%), so internal consistency
cannot be assumed either.

### 🆕 p136 — the spread is a SCOPE disagreement, and one widely-quoted figure is not a market size

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** This pass re-ran the market probe and the 2026 column now spans
**$1.94B to $11.4B — a 5.88× spread**, which looks like a collapse of agreement against p135's
~1.89×. 🟢 **It is not. Read the scope column and most of it dissolves:**

| 2026 figure | 2035/2030 figure | implied CAGR | **what is actually being counted** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **$11.4B** (2025: $8.3B) | → $57.2B by 2033 | 25.9% | Grand View — the broad market |
| **$10.6B** (2025: $7.52B) | → $42.48B by 2030 | 41.5% | Research and Markets — broad, published Feb 2026 |
| **$9.58B** (2025: $7.05B) | → $136.79B by 2035 | 34.52% | Precedence — broad, longest horizon |
| **$4.09B** | → $13.46B by 2030 | 34.7% | 🔵 **higher education only** — a segment |
| **$1.94B** | → $48.36B by 2035 | 42.95% | 🔴 **the lowest 2026 figure with the second-highest 2035 figure** — a scope this pass could not establish |
| **$3.37B** | *increase* 2026→2030 | 45% | 🔴 **NOT A MARKET SIZE.** Technavio reports **incremental growth**, and the market it describes is *"fragmented"* |

🔴 **The finding is a measurement-class one, and it has bitten this page before** (`P132-D`, a 2025
base year misfiled as a 2026 estimate). **Here the error waiting to happen is a units error**: a
$3.37B "45% CAGR" row is an *increment*, and quoting it beside a $10.6B *level* produces a spread
that exists only in the reader's head. 🟢 **The rule to carry: before a figure enters this page it
needs a base year (p132), a geography (the "Rest of Latin America" lesson below) and now a
QUANTITY — level, increment or segment.**

🟢 **What survives unchanged and is the honest thing to quote:** the broad-market 2026 level sits
around **$9.6–11.4B** across three independent firms (a **1.19×** spread, exactly as p135 had it),
and the **real** disagreement is still the growth rate — **25.9% to 45%**, which is why 2033–2035
figures span from $42B to $137B. The disagreement **compounds; it does not start large.**

Regional share, as reported: **North America ~36%** (largest), **Asia-Pacific fastest-growing at
~35.3% CAGR**. 🟡 One tracker puts *"Rest of Latin America"* at **$18.2M in 2024 → $36M by 2029** —
three orders of magnitude below this page's held LATAM figures, because its scope excludes the
region's largest markets. **A LATAM number without its country list is unusable.**

## The structural fact of this market

Adoption by individuals has outrun institutional governance, and the gap is where the work is:

- **88%** of students worldwide report using generative AI; **95%** of UK undergraduates.
- **60%** of US K-12 teachers used AI tools in the 2024-25 school year; **32%** weekly.
- **18%** of US teachers have a written policy telling them how.
- An IDB review of **193** AI-in-education initiatives in Latin America found **59%** used
  generative AI.

Students and teachers are already using these tools; institutions have neither policy, procurement,
nor audit trail. **The sellable work is governance, integration and evidence — not another chatbot.**

## Key players

- **Platforms (the installed base):** Instructure/Canvas (dominant in North American higher ed),
  Moodle (largest global deployment, HQ in Perth), Open edX / Axim Collaborative, Blackboard and
  D2L (proprietary, significant higher-ed share, almost no open-source connectors), Google
  Classroom, Microsoft (Education AI Toolkit updated April 2026 with agentic features for
  institutions).
- **Open-source agent supply:** concentrated and thin — `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (41k★, Apache-2.0) is
  the only education-specific agent with real scale; everything else on the shelf is under 1k stars.
- **Institutional/multilateral actors who shape procurement:** UNESCO (regional AI-in-education
  observatory for LAC; OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook argues for purpose-built educational
  AI over general-purpose tools), IDB, Google.org (AI-literacy funding across nine LATAM
  countries), teachers' unions (NEA, AFT, CTA have all issued classroom AI guidance).

### North America

#### 🆕 🟢 p153 — advisory VOLUME, now sized: 134 bills across 31 states

🟢 **The sellable unit is ADVISORY VOLUME, not a platform build, and this pass can size
it.** **134 bills across 31 states** in 2026 means a district procurement officer faces a
moving target with no in-house expertise. The concrete asks visible in the statutes:

- 🔴 **Student-data-for-training bans.** California **`AB 1159`** would bar using student
  data to train models unless it directly benefits the school; Idaho **`SB 1227`** would
  require privacy protections for school AI tools. 🟢 **Engagement: a data-flow audit that
  proves no student record leaves for training** — and `zavora-ai/mcp-student-records`'
  FERPA access logging is the only row on this shelf that already does it.
- 🔴 **Human-oversight and no-high-stakes mandates.** Oklahoma and Maryland require human
  oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students; Nevada **`AB 406`** bars
  AI from counsellor functions (p152). 🟢 **Engagement: an approval-gate layer** — the
  `P39`/`P35` pattern, which this shelf has an instrumented reference for.
- 🟡 **AI literacy funding.** `H.R. 8747` would let schools spend federal funds on AI
  literacy programmes; it cleared committee `2026-07-21` **largely along party lines**, so
  🔴 **treat it as a pipeline signal, not a budget.**
- 🔴 **A moratorium is also a market.** New York City has a **one-year moratorium on
  student-facing AI through eighth grade.** 🟢 **The buyable work there is
  teacher-facing**: preparation, assessment drafting, administrative automation — none of
  which touches a student.

🔴 **The guidance-count denominator is STILL disputed** (28+DC vs 35+, third pass).
🔴 **And Canada and Mexico returned nothing again, FOURTH pass** — the continental query
is US-only in practice, and the gap is named rather than papered over.


#### 🆕 🟢 p152 — the state layer turns BINDING, and the first hard deadline has already passed

🟢 **p151 recorded the federal layer turning against the states. p152 records the state layer
becoming enforceable anyway, with dates a client can be held to.**

| instrument | obligation | date |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Ohio** (law, via `kjk.com` + GovTech) | **every district must adopt an AI policy**; 🔵 content left to the district, so the state model policy satisfies it | 🔴 **`2026-07-01` — ALREADY PASSED** |
| 🟢 **Oklahoma SB 1734** | written district AI policies | **`2027-28` school year** |
| 🟢 **Tennessee** (2024 law) | district AI policy, K-12 | in force |
| 🟢 **Nevada AB 406** | 🔴 **bars public schools from using AI to perform mental-health functions** of counselors, psychologists and social workers | in force |
| 🟢 **US Dept of Education** final rule | **grant priority** for projects advancing AI understanding or ethical use | **`2026-04-13`** |
| 🟡 **Iowa, Illinois** (bills) | AI coursework in high-school requirements | 🔴 **not enacted — carried as bills** |

🔴 **A denominator this page cannot close, and both numbers are given.** States with K-12 AI
guidance: **28 + DC** (Opus, April 2026) against **35+** (Working Educators, June 2026).
🔵 **Two publishers, two counts, two months apart** — 🟢 **so the honest statement is "most
states, and the count is disputed"**, which is `P149-A`'s class.

🟢 **The common obligations across the new laws, which is the sellable shape:** educator
supervision · limits on high-stakes automated decisions · parent disclosure · data
protection.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed.** 🟢 **The Ohio deadline has already fallen and the content is the
district's own** — so the buyer is a district with a policy it had to write without
expertise, not a state procuring a system. 🔴 **That is a small-ticket, high-volume,
assessment-and-disclosure advisory shape**, not a platform build. 🟢 **Nevada's bar is the
sharper constraint**: any wellbeing or retention-alert agent pitched into a US district must
be able to show it is not performing a counselor's function.

🔴 **Canada and Mexico returned NOTHING again.** 🟢 The query was continental; the results
were US-only. 🔵 **An informed gap, third pass.**

state-by-state map this page has built over six passes is now *contested*, so a compliance
pitch keyed to one state's statute should name the federal challenge as a risk rather than
assume the statute is stable.

tutor pitched K-8 can be unsellable in a district on policy grounds alone**, while the
teacher-facing and administrative surfaces stay open. 🔵 **That is `P3`'s premise, sharpened.**

no final status reachable. 🟢 **Not re-probed and not re-registered.**

AI independently determining grades or discipline**, and a student right to decline AI use.
🔵 **Not law, and it is the clearest statement of what this market will ask vendors to
prove.** 🟢 **It maps directly onto `mcp-student-records`' approval gates** — 23 of 46 writes

a human in front of.

~10% of institutions with guidelines and 71% of teachers untrained.** 🟢 **Sell the
governance layer, not the model** — and in this region, sell it against named statutes.

#### 🆕 🔴 p141 — an Open edX core component sends security mail to a domain its steward does not own, and the line was edited four months ago

🔴 **No new North American market instrument; the channel returned only held rows.** What is new
is measured and it is a procurement finding.

`ACTION DB` resolved the three-way contact split p140 found across Open edX's layers. Both
addresses were rewritten **in 2026**, three months apart, by two contributors **at the same
company**, under the same `chore: modernize` banner
([`p141-contact-migration`](../compose/code/p141-contact-migration/README.md)):

| repository | at `HEAD` | commit | date |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 `openedx/XBlock` | 🔴 **`{name = "edX", email = "oscm@edx.org"}`** | `94a759c` | **2026-06-05** |
| 🟢 `openedx/codejail` | 🟢 `{name = "Open edX Project", email = "oscm@openedx.org"}` | `0b38ff8` | **2026-09-14** |

🔵 **Both migrated the legacy `setup.py` field into PEP 621 `authors = [...]`. September corrected
the organisation and the domain. 🔴 June retyped the stale organisation name and the stale domain
into the new syntax** — it had the line open and carried it forward. 🔴 **So this is a live,
in-flight migration that reached one repository and not the other, not neglect** — and p140's
falsifier is refuted in the direction that makes the finding stronger.

🔴 **The consequence is live today.** `edx.org` is a domain p140 established **2U** operates,
while the steward is **Axim Collaborative** (`dave@axim.org`). 🔴 **`openedx/XBlock` — the
component every XBlock-based course component builds against — directs maintainer and security
correspondence to a domain controlled by a different company than its steward.**
`openedx/codejail`, the sandbox that executes untrusted learner-submitted code, now points
correctly. 🟢 **The remedy is one line in `pyproject.toml` and the template is already committed
in the September change.**

🔴 **And a second US-stewarded artefact is silent where it matters.** `ACTION DA` measured
`openedx-core` **1.9.1**: 🔴 **0 licence files, 0 SPDX headers and 0 copyright lines across 237
Python files in six packages**, under a distribution declaring **AGPL-3.0-only** with 35 139
bytes of text. 🔵 **A subtree lifted out of that wheel — the normal way a studio uses it —
carries the most reciprocal obligation on these shelves and announces nothing about itself.**

🔴 **One booking this pass refused to make here.** `canvas-mcp-server`'s repository commits are
**13 of 16 at `-0600`**, and `-0600` is US Central **and** Mexico City, Guatemala, Costa Rica,
Honduras, El Salvador, Nicaragua and Belize. 🔵 **Left `UNMAPPED` rather than booked North
America**, because an offset LATAM shares cannot place a row in a region whose emptiness this
page reports honestly.


#### 🆕 p140 — the Open edX steward is a US non-profit, and its own repositories disagree about where to send a notice

🔴 **No new North American instrument this pass — the channel returned only rows this page
carries.** What is new is measured, and it lands on the supply side rather than the policy side.

🆕 **The Open edX learning core's live artefact is stewarded from the United States, and this KB
was tracking the wrong package.** [`openedx-core`](https://pypi.org/project/openedx-core/)
**1.9.1** (uploaded **2026-10-09**) names `David Ormsbee <dave@axim.org>` — **Axim
Collaborative**, the US non-profit that is edX Inc. *renamed*, holding the ~$700–800M in net
proceeds Harvard and MIT received when the edX brand was sold to **2U**, and obligated to apply
them to public educational purposes. 🔵 **For a North American engagement this is the counterparty
that matters**: the platform's steward is a US charitable entity with a public-benefit mandate,
not a vendor.

🔴 **And its own repositories give three different answers to "who do we notify?"**

| layer | artefact | holder | contact |
|---|---|---|---|
| repo | `openedx/XBlock` | `edX` | 🔴 `oscm@edx.org` |
| repo | `openedx/codejail` | `edX` / 🆕 `Open edX Project` | 🆕 `oscm@openedx.org` |
| registry | `openedx-core` 1.9.1 | 🆕 `David Ormsbee` | 🆕 **`dave@axim.org`** |

🟢 **The holder name is defensible** — `edX` is the pre-rename identity of the current steward.
🔴 **The contact is not**: `edx.org` is operated by **2U**, a different company, so one of two
sibling repositories in one GitHub organisation routes its notice obligation to the wrong
organisation. 🔵 **This is a procurement finding, not a pedantic one**: a US institution's counsel
asked to discharge an AGPL-3.0 notice duty on an Open edX customisation has three addresses and no
way to choose between them from the repositories alone.

🟡 **Two smaller North American placements, both measured:**

- 🆕 `Crosstalk-Solutions/project-nomad` is the only one of five `ROOT_ONLY` rows read this pass
  whose Apache-2.0 appendix is **filled in** — `Copyright 2024-2026 Crosstalk Solutions LLC`, a US
  entity, corroborated in `package.json`. 🟢 **One row in five doing licence hygiene correctly, and
  it is a North American commercial one.**
- 🔴 `edu.ksu.ome.lti:lti-launch` 1.4.7 (**Kansas State University**) remains the worst Maven row
  on this shelf and the correction made it starker: of **5 of 5** artefacts that declare a licence,
  **4 (80%)** ship no text, and this one declares the strongest obligation of them all (LGPL-3.0).
  🔵 **It is still the LTI→OAuth-subject mapping pattern `P13` depends on.**

🔴 **`ACTION CW` — Texas HB 149 (TRAIGA, in force 2026-01-01) — is still unread**, and this pass
established that the cause is a network allowlist rather than a per-host accident. Not
re-registered.

#### 🆕 p139 — the state-board thesis has a federal counterparty now, and a measurable ownership defect

🔵 **Two additions, one of which cuts against three passes of this page's own argument.**

🔴 **🆕 A federal preemption proposal aims at exactly the instrument layer this page has been
tracking.** The White House's **National Policy Framework for AI (March 2026)** recommends that
**Congress preempt state-level AI laws**. 🔴 **If enacted, the system board and the state statute —
p137's buying unit and p134's buyer — stop being the operative instruments**, and a US education
engagement's compliance surface collapses from ~31 state regimes to one federal one.
🟡 **It is a recommendation, not a bill, and no vote is recorded in these sources.** But it is the
first thing found that would *invalidate* rather than extend this page's North America model, and
it is booked for that reason. 🔴 **Secondary-sourced; `digital-strategy`-class primary channels are
403 here (see `intel/trends.md` → `T24`).**

🟡 **🆕 Higher education's own rule-making is under formal review in Virginia.** The **Joint
Legislative Audit and Review Commission (JLARC)** is studying how public colleges and universities
set AI rules — academic integrity, data privacy, equity, transparency — with a report due
**early 2028**. 🔵 **A legislative audit of institutional AI policy is a different instrument class
again**: not a mandate and not a procurement route, but a published baseline that other states copy.
🔴 **The 2028 date means it constrains nothing in this engagement cycle.**

🔵 **Confirmed, not new** (already on this page): **134 bills across 31 states** in the 2026 session
(single-source, approximate); **Ohio**'s district policy deadline of **2026-07-01**, now **passed**;
**North Carolina**'s staged K-12 governance deadlines through **2028**; amended **COPPA** general
compliance **2026-04-22**, which belongs with p138's age-gate class.

🟢 **A deployment datum worth carrying for scale arguments**: **OpenAI + California State
University** — `ChatGPT Edu` for **460 000+ students** and **63 000** faculty and staff. 🔵 **The
largest single-system deployment this page has recorded**, and the comparison point for any
"pilot first" proposal.

🔴 **And one measured, North-America-owned defect this pass.**
`edu.ksu.ome.lti:lti-launch` **1.4.7** — the Kansas State LGPL-3.0 library this KB's pattern `P13`
depends on — **declares LGPL-3.0 in its POM and ships no licence text in its jar** (43 paths,
sha1-verified). 🔵 **The interoperability library a North American engagement is most likely to
inherit is itself the licence defect this KB has spent two passes measuring elsewhere.** Detail:
[`p139-pypi-maven-payload`](../compose/code/p139-pypi-maven-payload/README.md).


#### 🆕 p138 — the age gate is the new instrument class, and it is the one that constrains product

🔵 **p137 found the buying unit (the system board). This pass found the clause that decides whether
there is a product to sell into it at all.** p136 and p137 tracked mandate *deadlines*; the instrument
appearing now restricts the **audience**, which is a different kind of constraint and a harder one to
engineer around.

| instrument | what it does | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🆕 **New York City — student-facing AI moratorium through eighth grade** | 🔴 **a one-year moratorium on student-facing AI** for grades up to and including 8 — not a governance requirement but a **prohibition on the deployment surface** | reported in force; the largest school district in the United States |
| 🆕 **California AB 1159** | would **bar the use of student data to train AI models** | bill, 2026 session |
| 🆕 **Idaho SB 1227** | would require privacy protections for school AI tools | bill, 2026 session |
| **Ohio** | first state to require **every** K-12 district to adopt a formal AI use policy, by **2026-07-01** | 🟢 deadline **passed** — already recorded by p136 |
| **H.R. 8747** (K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act) | federal funds usable for AI curriculum | 🟡 ordered reported **2026-07-21**, **18–15**; no floor vote — unchanged from p137 |

🔴 **The moratorium class is the one to brief a client on, and it cuts the opposite way from every
mandate this page has tracked.** A deadline creates demand for compliance tooling. An age gate
removes the end user: a tutor product aimed at K-8 has no deployment surface in that district for the
duration, regardless of how conformant it is. 🔵 **For a studio the consequence is concrete — in the
US K-12 segment, build the teacher-facing and administrative surfaces first, because they are the
ones that remain sellable while student-facing deployment is restricted.** That is the same
conclusion `P9` reaches from the platform side, now reached from the regulatory side.

🟡 **Volume, from a secondary tracker and labelled as such**: **134** AI-in-education bills across
**31** states in the 2026 session, concentrated on data privacy, classroom restrictions and
curriculum, with **more than 30** states already holding AI guidance documents. 🔴 **p136 already
recorded that published bill counts disagree with each other; this figure is one publisher's and is
not reconciled against the others.**

🟡 **Adoption, same caveat**: **60%** of US K-12 teachers reported using AI tools during the
2024–25 school year and **32%** at least weekly; North America is put at **36%** of the global
AI-in-education market and **USD 3.68B** in 2026. 🔴 **All three figures come from one secondary
statistics roundup**, not from the survey instruments themselves, and the market share sits inside
the unresolved global spread recorded below. 🔴 **Canada: still nothing — a fourth consecutive pass.**

#### 🆕 p137 — the higher-education hole was a vocabulary hole, and the buying unit is the system board

🔴 **p136 recorded "higher education, federal action and Canada returned NOTHING" as a measured
absence.** `ACTION CC` re-ran it with instrument-class nouns — *board of regents*, *system office*,
*provost* — and 🔴 **the absence was a probe artefact for two of the three.**

| institution | instrument | date | what a studio can sell against it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **South Dakota Board of Regents** | 🟢 **BOR policy 7.9 adopted** — systemwide governance for AI and emergent technologies | **July 2026** | the only *adopted* systemwide policy found; implementation and evidence work, across six universities |
| **Kansas Board of Regents** | AI framework made the board's **top priority**: student teaching, academic research, administrative oversight, plus data privacy, cybersecurity and academic integrity | **September 2026** | policy-to-implementation, pre-adoption: the moment to shape the control set |
| **University of Alaska Board of Regents** | **draft** AI policy presented; would require an AI plan at **all three campuses** for students, faculty and staff | **September 2026**, decision due this year | three campus plans from one adopted policy |
| **University of Nebraska** | **AI Institute** item to the Board of Regents, presented by the Provost | **2026-06-18** | a system-wide hub is a build, not a policy |
| **University of California** | **AI Steering Committee** (formed March 2026) advising the President, chancellors and the Regents | discussion item **September 2026** | ten campuses behind one committee's recommendations |

🟢 **Federal movement, dated:** **H.R. 8747**, the *K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026*
(introduced 2026-05-12 by Rep. Randy Fine), was **ordered reported by the House Education and
Workforce Committee on 2026-07-21, 18–15**, as an amendment in the nature of a substitute dated
2026-07-15 that amends the Elementary and Secondary Education Act to make AI curriculum and teacher
training permissible uses of federal funds. 🔴 **No floor vote; Senate action not found.** 🔵 **The
commercial read: permissible-use language moves existing formula money, which is faster than new
appropriations.**

🔴 **Canada returned nothing for a second consecutive pass**, with the same vocabulary that worked
for the US system boards (provincial ministry, council of ministers). Measured, not inferred.

- **Market position:** largest share (~36%), reported at ~$3.68B in 2026 by one tracker. Canvas is
  the platform of record in higher ed, and it has the best connector ecosystem — the MCP work is
  largely done, so engagements start further along.
- **The binding constraint is state-by-state policy, and it is now mandatory, not advisory.**
  134 AI-in-education bills were introduced across 31 states in 2026. **Ohio requires every K-12
  district to adopt a formal AI use policy by 1 July 2026** — a hard date with no accompanying
  capacity. California AB 1159 bars using student data to train models. Oklahoma and Maryland
  require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students. More than 30 states
  have guidance documents. Federally, the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act (H.R. 8747) advanced
  from committee on 21 July 2026.
- **Opportunity:** *policy-to-implementation*. A district with a mandated policy and no technical
  means to enforce it needs data-flow inventory, a model-routing layer that can prove student data
  never reached a training endpoint, human-in-the-loop gates on anything high-stakes, and an audit
  log a board can read. Sell the compliance artefact, deliver the platform underneath it. Build on
  the Canvas MCP connectors plus `tutor-mcp` for auditable pedagogical decisions.
- **Second opportunity:** the AI-literacy curriculum funding line that H.R. 8747 would open, paired
  with `GarethManning/education-agent-skills` as a content base (licence review required).
- 🆕 **New from this pass's battery (p132).** Two capacity figures beside the held *18% of teachers
  have a written policy*: **only ~10% of institutions have formal AI guidelines** and **~71% of US
  teachers lack AI training** (2026 statistics roundup — different predicates, so they stack rather
  than conflict). Capital is moving into courseware specifically: **Carnegie Mellon + the Gates
  Foundation, $55M**, aimed at *gateway college courses*; and **OpenAI has launched a country-level
  education programme with eight national partners**. 🔵 **Read the CMU/Gates target carefully —
  "gateway courses" (the high-failure first-year courses that gate a degree) is the one place in
  higher ed where a learning-outcome improvement is directly measurable in retention dollars.**
  That is the natural home for the outcome-evaluation build in `compose/patterns.md` → P6.
- 🆕 **Supply note: Canada is the best-served NA jurisdiction in the new SIS tier**, with three MIT
  connectors (`mcgill-vsb-mcp`, `UofT-MCP`, `uoft-quercus-mcp`) against the US's one
  (`zavora-ai/mcp-student-records`, Apache-2.0). 🟢 **That US row is the single most
  procurement-shaped agent in this KB** — FERPA access logging as a first-class feature, and the
  only organisation-owned row of its tier. It is the right starting point for the
  policy-to-implementation offer above, which needs exactly that audit trail.
- 🔴 **Expect a client to arrive holding a wrong EU date.** NA-facing sources still state that *"the
  EU AI Act classifies education AI as high-risk, with full effect in August 2026."* The
  schools-facing Annex III date is **2027-12-02**. Correcting this politely is a credibility
  opportunity, not a pedantic one.

- 🟡 **One figure on this page needs a definition warning, added p133.** A North-America-specific
  series puts the regional market at **$951M (2024) → $2 303.2M (2029), a 15.9% CAGR** — while this
  page's global figures carry CAGRs of **31%–41.5%**. 🔴 **A region said to hold ~36% of a market
  cannot grow at half the market's rate unless the two series count different things**, and they do:
  the regional series uses a narrower product definition. **Quote the global CAGR or the regional
  level, never one against the other.** Same class of error as `P132-D`'s base-year mix.
- 🔴 **The stale EU AI Act claim resurfaced in a North-America-facing source, for the fourth pass
  running.** A US comparison piece states the Act takes *"full effect in August 2026"* and
  classifies education AI as high-risk. **The Annex III date is 2027-12-02** (`intel/trends.md` →
  `T3`). 🟡 **This is now a documented pattern rather than a one-off: the error travels through
  North-American commentary, so a US or Canadian client is the most likely to arrive holding it.**
  Correcting it is a credibility win and it widens the EMEA delivery window by 16 months.


#### 🟢 p134 — the state legislature became the buyer, and it has dates

🔵 **The named instrument this pass adds is volume**: the **PIE Network** tracked **~100 state bills
in 2026 that directly affect students' use of AI in K-12**, inside **more than 1 500 AI-related
bills** introduced nationwide. 🟢 **The pattern across them is research, transparency and guardrails
rather than mandated classroom adoption** — which is a governance-services market, not a product
market.

🟢 **The rows that matter are the ones with a compliance date, because a date is a budget cycle:**

| jurisdiction | instrument | what it obliges | date |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Oklahoma** | **S.B. 1734** | **every district** must adopt a written AI policy | 🔴 **before the 2027-28 school year** |
| **Maryland** | **S.B. 720** | each local school system designates an **AI coordinator**; AI literacy added to state standards | 🔴 **by June 2027** |
| **Idaho** | **S.B. 1227** | a **statewide generative-AI framework**, with districts adopting aligned policies | framework-led |
| **California** | **A.B. 1159** (🟡 **pending** — passed first chamber) | bars using **student data to train AI models** unless it directly benefits the school | not yet law |
| **Virginia** | **S.B. 394** | directs the state education agency to issue guidance, including **teacher training** | guidance-led |
| **Alabama** | **H.B. 329** | a computer-science course **with AI instruction** as a graduation requirement | curriculum |
| **Utah** | **H.B. 273** | folds AI into state computer-science standards | curriculum |

🟢 **Federal, and still early:** the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** was introduced in
April (Rep. Randy Fine), and the White House released a **National AI Policy Framework**.

🔴 **Three conflicts in the sources, recorded rather than resolved — this is the honest state of the
picture, not a finished map.** (a) Trackers **disagree on how many states have enacted** laws; one
says five while the same result set names more as enacted or pending. (b) **Idaho and Maryland are
reported as enacted by some sources and pending by others.** (c) One source states **Colorado
repealed its AI Act in May 2026** and replaced it with a narrower framework **exempting
FERPA-covered institutions** — 🔴 **this could not be confirmed against any second source and must
not be quoted to a client.** All of these came from trackers and vendor blogs; **verify against the
official state legislature site before it goes in a proposal.**

🟢 **Supply, newly placed in this region by p134** (`agents/top.md` → *Standards-native agents*): the
**Ed-Fi Alliance** (Apache-2.0, pushed 2026-10-09), **Google** (Apache-2.0 OneRoster conformance
tests), the **ADL Initiative / US DoD** (Apache-2.0 xAPI profile), the **Minnesota Department of
Education** (🔴 unlicensed), plus district- and state-level publishers in **Fresno CA**, **Virtual
Arkansas** and the **Idaho Digital Learning Alliance**. 🟢 **North America is the only region where
the open-source supply is owned by the institutions that buy** — which makes the sanctioned-integration
pattern (`compose/patterns.md` → **P11**) land here first.

🔵 **Reading note carried forward from p133, still live:** the NA-specific market series
(**$951M 2024 → $2 303.2M 2029, 15.9% CAGR**) **cannot** be quoted against the global 31–41.5% CAGRs
on this page. Different denominators, same `P132-D` class of error.

#### 🆕 p135 — `ACTION BO` discharged: Colorado's replacement statute names education, and it has dates

🟢 **Confirmed against the Colorado General Assembly's own bill page and the Legislative Council
Staff fiscal note.** p134 recorded the repeal as *unconfirmed* and opened `ACTION BO` to confirm or
strike it. It stands, and it is now the most concretely dated AI obligation this KB holds for
any region:

| fact | value |
|---|---|
| bill | **SB 26-189, "Automated Decision-Making Technology"** |
| what it does | **Repeals and reenacts SB 24-205** (the Colorado AI Act), replacing *"high-risk artificial intelligence systems"* with **"automated decision-making technology" (ADMT)** used in *consequential decisions* |
| signed | **2026-05-14** (House passed **34–1** on 2026-05-12) |
| 🔴 **scope** | Consequential decisions explicitly include **education enrolment and opportunity**, alongside employment, housing, lending, insurance, health care and essential government services |
| developer duties | From **2027-01-01**: technical documentation to deployers, and notice of material updates to covered ADMT |
| deployer duties | Notice **at the point of interaction**; a **plain-language explanation within 30 days** of an adverse outcome; consumer rights to **correction of inaccurate data** and to **meaningful human review and reconsideration** |
| AG rulemaking | Required **by 2027-01-01**; **cure period runs through 2030** |
| 🔴 enforcement status | A **US District Court ordered the Attorney General not to enforce** SB 24-205 *or any legislation amending it* until rulemaking concludes and the court rules in **`X. AI LLC v. Weiser`** |

🔴 **What was dropped matters as much as what was added.** The duty of reasonable care, mandatory
**impact assessments** and the required **risk-management programme** are gone; **disclosure, 30-day
explanation and human review** replace them. 🟢 **That inverts the sales motion.** The old statute
sold a *governance programme*; the new one sells an **explanation pipeline** — per-decision
provenance, a retrievable reason for an adverse outcome, and a human-review workflow that can be
evidenced. An institution that admits, places or streams students with a model now owes an
explainable record of **why**, within 30 days, to the individual affected.

🔵 **Read the stay correctly before quoting it.** Enforcement being paused is **not** a reason for a
client to wait: the **compliance dates are not stayed**, the cure period ends in **2030**, and the
order is tied to the outcome of pending litigation. A buyer who treats the stay as a reprieve has
2027 duties and no programme.

🔴 **Channel note, recorded rather than hidden.** `leg.colorado.gov` could not be fetched directly
from this environment (`curl` → **403 CONNECT tunnel failed**; `WebFetch` → DNS `ENOTFOUND`), and
`openstates.org` and `legiscan.com` were refused the same way. The legislature's own bill page and
fiscal note were reached **through the search channel restricted to `leg.colorado.gov`**, which
returned both documents. 🟢 **So this is a primary-source confirmation, through a secondary
transport** — stronger than p134's seven law-firm summaries, and weaker than a direct read. The
refusal is logged for `compose/code/p798-proxy-refusal-ledger`.

#### 🆕 p136 — the first district deadline has PASSED, and the bill counts disagree with each other

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** 🔴 **Ohio required every traditional public school district, community
school and STEM school to adopt a formal AI policy by 2026-07-01.** That date is **three months in
the past**, which changes the conversation from *"a deadline is coming"* to *"your policy exists;
can you show it works?"* — and the second question is the billable one, because a policy adopted to
meet a date is not an implementation.

| jurisdiction | obligation | date | status as of today |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ohio** | every district adopts a formal AI policy | **2026-07-01** | 🔴 **ELAPSED** |
| **Oklahoma S.B. 1734** | written district AI policy: approved and prohibited uses, data protection, family transparency, periodic review | **before 2027-28 school year** | live |
| **Maryland S.B. 720** | an AI coordinator per local system (non-instructional, central office); AI literacy in state standards | **2027-06-01** | live |
| **Idaho S.B. 1227** | state generative-AI framework (privacy, **procurement safeguards**, transparency, integrity, literacy, PD); districts adopt aligned policies | framework first | live |
| **California A.B. 1159** | bars student data for model training unless it directly benefits the school | pending | watch |

🔴 **Two source conflicts, recorded unresolved rather than averaged.** MultiState reports **134
bills across 31 states** this session; FutureEd is cited at **68 bills across 27 states** — a 2×
disagreement on a *countable* quantity. And a tracker claiming *"35+ states have official AI
guidance"* has an **own table showing 33 to 35.** 🔵 This is the same class as the market-size rows
above: a number is not a finding until its definition is visible. Note only one of these counts is
likely to be wrong about *bills*; they are probably counting different things (introduced vs
education-specific vs enacted), and neither says which.

🟢 **The buying unit this pass surfaced is the district, not only the state.** Named district
programmes: **Chicago**, **Denver**, **Charlotte-Mecklenburg**, with **NYC developing a citywide
playbook**. A district with an elapsed statutory deadline and no evidence layer is the sharpest
qualified lead on this page.

🔴 **And the informed gap: higher education returned NOTHING.** The North America probe this pass
came back with K-12 policy in depth and **no higher-education policy, no federal action and no
Canada** — which is a hole in this page's coverage, not a finding about those markets. It is
written down so the K-12 richness above is not mistaken for regional completeness.

### EMEA

#### 🆕 🟢 p153 — the clock INVERSION corroborated, and a date conflict carried into the room

🔴 **The clock INVERSION p152 found is the whole opportunity, and this pass corroborates
the dates that produce it.** Article 50 transparency obligations are **live now**; the
assessment-and-admissions high-risk regime moved to **`2027-12-02`**.

- 🟢 **Sell the transparency obligation today.** Labelling AI-generated instructional
  content and disclosing AI interaction is a **current** duty, small, and auditable.
- 🟡 **Sell the high-risk programme as a 2027 roadmap, not a 2026 deadline.** An admissions
  screener or a proctoring/cheating detector is high-risk because its output decides access
  to education — 🟢 **and a client now has 14 months, which changes the pitch from
  remediation to design.**
- 🔴 **Carry the date conflict into the room, do not resolve it.** One source has the
  Council approving **`2026-06-29`** with the act *"awaiting publication"*; the OJ date on
  this shelf is **`2026-07-24`**. 🟢 **A consultant who states both and names the
  unresolved one is more credible than one who picks.**
- 🟢 **Capability funding is real and dated**: DFKI **`KI-Kompetenzen HS`**, **Jan 2026 –
  Mar 2029, ≈€6.9M** (p152), plus Spain's draft **`Real Decreto`**.
- 🟡 **The OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook recommends purpose-built educational AI
  over general-purpose tools** — 🟢 **which is an argument for a composed vertical stack and
  against a generic chatbot**, i.e. for exactly what `compose/patterns.md` sells.

🔴 **No EMEA adoption RATE, sixth pass.** 🟡 **$2.64B for 2026 is vendor-class and is not
adopted.** 🔴 **No primary legal text, SIXTH pass** — `eur-lex` and the commentary hosts
refuse categorically; 🟢 **not retried host by host.**


#### 🆕 🔴 p152 — the Annex III clock MOVED, and p151's null is BROKEN

🔴 **The date this page has published for five passes is WITHDRAWN.** 🆕 **`T88`.**

| | |
|---|---|
| **instrument** | **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** — the *Digital Omnibus on AI* |
| **passage** | Parliament **`2026-06-16`** · Council **`2026-06-29`** · OJ **`2026-07-24`** · in force **`2026-07-27`** |
| 🟢 **Annex III high-risk — CONTAINS education and vocational training** | **`2026-08-02`** → 🟢 **`2027-12-02`** |
| **Annex I embedded products** | → **`2028-08-02`** |
| **national regulatory sandboxes** | `2026-08-02` → **`2027-08-02`** |
| 🔴 **NOT delayed** | 🔴 **Article 50 transparency.** 🟡 One publisher puts Art 50(2) watermarking at **`2026-12-02`** for systems on the market at `2026-08-02` — carried as one source |
| **already in force, unchanged** | 🔴 **emotion-recognition AI banned in schools since `2025-02`** · staff AI-literacy duty |

🔴 **In force six days before the date it replaced.** 🔵 **Why five passes missed it: the
omnibus never says "education"** — it moves an annex, and education is inside the annex.

🔵 **The opportunity INVERTS, and this is the pitch.** 🔴 **Assessment, admissions, grading
and proctoring are no longer a 2026 emergency — they are a `2027-12-02` programme.**
🟢 **What IS live now is Article 50**: a tutoring chatbot, a generated-content feature, a
synthetic-media tool. 🟢 **So the sequencing a client needs is transparency and AI-literacy
first, high-risk conformity second** — the reverse of the order this page has implied since
pass 146.

🔵 **The 35% internet figure is the one to carry into an engagement**, because it bounds every

estate. 🟢 **It is also why `P2` — offline-first — is the Africa-facing pattern, not `P1`.**

UNESCO **IICBA** and UNESCO-**ICHEI** co-published *Digitalization and the Use of Artificial
Intelligence in Higher Education in Africa: An Exploratory Study*, **January 2026**, built on
two online surveys across the five sub-Saharan regions. 🔴 **Its own authors caution that the
sample is small, recruited through online contacts, and not representative.** 🔵 **So Africa's
one continental instrument at this date is explicitly non-generalisable — a bounded gap rather
than a null, and a much more useful thing to tell a client than silence.**

| claim | value |
|---|---|
| institutions in Europe and North America with AI guidance **in place or in development** | **~70%** |
| schools and universities across **450+** institutions with **established formal** guidelines | 🔴 **10%** |

🔵 **They are different measurements — "developing" against "established" — and the gap is
too wide for that to be the whole explanation.** 🔴 **Neither is traceable to a named
instrument.** 🟢 **Recorded as a contradiction rather than averaged**, and 🔵 **the LATAM
numbers below are the reason to take the low one seriously: UNESCO IESALC measures 87%
adoption against 26% formal strategy on a named, sized sample.** 🟢 **A 3:1 adoption-to-

##### 🟡 EU AI Act — the education clock, and what moved

🟢 **Unchanged and load-bearing: stand-alone high-risk obligations at `2027-12-02`**, embedded
high-risk at `2028-08-02`. 🟢 **This is the date `P4` builds against and it is confirmed again
this pass.** 🔴 **The Digital Omnibus that moved them was still awaiting publication as of a
2026-07-03 schools guide**, and a second source reports the amendments in force **2026-07-27**
with enforcement from **2026-08-02**. 🔴 **Two readings, both secondary, Official Journal
unread** — 🔵 **and the discrepancy is about the *enforcement* machinery, not about the
high-risk education deadline, which both agree on.**

🟢 **The education trigger list, restated because it is what scopes a conformity dossier**: a
system that **evaluates learners**, **assigns them to programmes**, **detects prohibited
behaviour during assessment**, or **influences access to qualifications**.

🆕 **OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook** reportedly recommends moving beyond
general-purpose AI toward **purpose-built educational AI** — 🔵 **which is the institutional
endorsement `P1`'s "auditable tutor" framing has lacked.** 🔴 **Read from a secondary summary.**

🆕 **Reported K-12 integration leaders: Finland, Estonia, the Netherlands.** 🔴 **Aggregator-
sourced, no instrument named.** 🔴 **A reported UK £4M programme for AI lesson-planning and
marking tools could not be verified and is NOT carried as a figure.**

Education a priority sector; **Action Eight** = AI skills in educational institutions. Phase 1 **2025–2026**: governance structures and national strategies; horizon **2030** |
| 🟢 **Kenya** | **National AI Strategy 2025–2030** | Launched **2025-03**, active on OECD.AI; education a target sector. Implementation roadmap **2025-12**. 🔴 **The 2026 draft AI and Emerging Technologies Policy says AI is NOT yet systematically in formal curricula** |
| 🟢 **Nigeria** | **National AI Strategy (2025)** + 2025 curriculum reform | AI and digital literacy **in the 2025 curriculum reform**; **Naija Teacher AI** (TRCN + GMind AI, UNESCO SDG4 case study) targets **1.5M** teachers; **3MTT**; **FCT AI Club** pilot approved by FCT UBEB and SEB |
| 🟡 **South Africa** | **National AI in Education Framework — unpublished** | DBE's 2026 digital-education update refers to it. 🟢 **Coding and robotics in curriculum** (Grades R–3, Grade 7, from 2023). 🔴 **The 2024 draft national AI policy was WITHDRAWN over citations and journals that do not exist** |

🔵 **What an EMEA engagement can and cannot claim here.** 🟢 **Policy intent is documented and
datable in all four.** 🔴 **No adoption figure, spend figure or deployment count was found for
any of them** — so Africa moves from *"no evidence"* to *"policy mapped, market unmeasured"*,
which is a different gap and a smaller one. 🔵 **Kenya's own draft policy is the most useful
single sentence for a client conversation**, because it is a government document saying the
curriculum work has not happened yet.

#### 🟢 p148 — the Middle East gap is PARTLY CLOSED

the Middle East or Africa":**

| country | what | scale and dates |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **UAE** | AI as a **subject, KG–Grade 12**, public schools | from **2025-26**; standalone subject *Artificial Intelligence and Technology*; extended to **national-curriculum private schools** from **2026-27**; Cabinet approval **2026-09-02**; **22 000** educators trained |

Africa** explicitly returned nothing on any of the three. 🔵 **This is an informed gap, not
silence** — the distinction this KB exists to maintain — and it is the single highest-value

🟢 **Europe's clock is settled and unchanged**: Article 4 AI-literacy duty in force since
**2025-02-02**; emotion recognition in education **banned since 2025-02**; Annex III
high-risk classification **2027-12-02**, embedded systems **2028-08-02**, under

single aggregator. 🟢 **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** are named as K-12 integration
leaders by the same source — 🟡 **a lead for a named-account approach, not a measurement.**

🆕 🟢 **An institutional tailwind**: the **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** recommends
moving **beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational AI**. 🔵 **That is
an integrator's recommendation**, and it is the same conclusion this KB reaches from the
repository side.

studio reusing this connector for an EU institution is inside it, and the AGPL/GPL licence

providers in the delivery model, with Big Tech as delivery partners**. 🔵 **That is a
displacement risk for institutional buyers and an opening for an integrator who is neither.**

became generally applicable **2026-08-02**. 🔴 **`P144-U`'s sixteen-month conflict closes in

#### 🆕 p144 — the regime makes admissions screening high-risk, and 90% of institutions have no guidelines to meet it

🔴 **Only 10% of 450+ surveyed schools and universities have formal AI-use guidelines**
*(secondary)*. 🔵 **Against a regime where an AI tool affecting access to education is HIGH-RISK
by classification** — admissions screening is the named example — requiring a conformity
assessment before deployment plus human oversight.

🔴 **The gap is therefore not a maturity gap but a compliance gap with a legal trigger**, and it
is the single clearest services opportunity this page records in EMEA. 🔴 **But see the date
conflict above**: whether the trigger is **2026-08** or **2027-12-02** is unresolved here, and
`eur-lex.europa.eu` is unreachable from this environment.
