🔵 **So the regional reading stands unchanged and better grounded**: LATAM has the adoption (87%),
lacks the governance (26%), has enacted statutes this page now carries, and 🔴 **supplies no
institutional open-source rows to this shelf** — which is a services opportunity rather than a
sourcing one, and is the ninth pass to say so.

#### 🆕 p139 — two statutes this page did not carry, and the minors regime that is already in force

🔵 **The LATAM *instrument* picture is better than this page has had it; the LATAM *ownership*
picture is still empty, and the two should stop being conflated.**

🟢 **🆕 Peru has the region's only ENACTED AI statute — Law 31814, July 2023.** 🔴 **This page has
never carried it**, across every LATAM pass, while tracking Brazil's unenacted bill in detail.
🔵 **So the sentence "LATAM has frameworks but no enacted law", which this page has implied for
several passes, is wrong** — it has had one for over three years. 🟡 Law 31814 is a promotion-oriented
framework statute rather than a risk-classification regime, so it does not create education duties
the way Brazil's bill would; the correction is to the claim, not to the engagement model.

🔴 **🆕 Brazil's `Digital ECA` — Law 15.211/2025 — has been IN FORCE since March 2026**, covering
**age verification** and limits on **targeted advertising to minors**. 🔵 **This is the single most
actionable LATAM item on this page**, and it belongs with p138's North America age-gate class:
a Brazilian K-12 deployment faces a live minors regime **now**, regardless of where PL 2338/2023
ends up. 🔴 **This page has carried PL 2338 for five passes and missed the law that actually
applies.**

🟡 **🆕 Chile has asked to defer its personal data law from December 2026 to December 2027.** 🔵 Same
direction as the EU's Annex III deferral — 🟡 **a regional pattern of slipping data and AI deadlines
is forming, and a client plan that assumes 2026 dates hold is now wrong in at least three
jurisdictions.** Chile's AI bill remains in its first constitutional stage; an anti-deepfake bill
(labelling, 72-hour takedown) passed in general and returned to committee.

🟢 **Confirmed, not new**: the **UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for LAC** (launched
**2026-04-14**, Santiago) and **CENIA**'s finding that **13 of 19** countries studied do not teach
early AI in schools are both already carried here and in `intel/open-gaps.md`. **Brazil's PL
2338/2023** (Senate approved **2024-12-10**, now in the Chamber, education among its high-risk
classes) is unchanged. **Mexico**'s federal AI bills remain **unenacted**, with 2026 amendments to
copyright/labour/film law pending and deepfake provisions sent to the Senate; **Argentina**'s
government has proposed keeping AI largely unregulated; **Brazil signed a digital partnership with
the EU** in 2026.

🔴 **The LATAM ownership gap is now at NINE consecutive passes, and this pass did nothing about it
either.** Of every artefact measured this pass — 9 npm connectors, 11 PyPI packages, 5 Maven
artefacts, 3 repository trees, 7 new tutoring repos, 4 duplicate-pair members — 🔴 **not one is
LATAM-owned, and not one was even *tested* for LATAM ownership.** 🔵 **The honest statement is that
this is a property of where education-AI connector code is written, not of where this KB looks** —
but it is not *measured*, so it stays a declared gap. The one LATAM-owned row this KB carries at all
remains `academico-sis/academico`, whose holder (`Thomas Debay`) this pass read and did **not**
place regionally, because a name is not evidence.


#### 🆕 p138 — the eighth consecutive pass with no LATAM-owned row, and this pass declined a chance to fake one

🔴 **Nothing changed in LATAM ownership, and the honest version of that sentence is longer than the
dishonest one.** A registry-first search of the education-connector tier returned **21** packages,
and **two** carry owner names that a careless pass would have booked as LATAM rows:

| package | what is actually measured | what is NOT measured |
|---|---|---|
| `@nahuelalbornoz/moodle-mcp` **0.5.1** (2026-04-23) | 🟢 **MIT** with `LICENSE` in the payload, 6 paths. Author field *"Italicia"*, maintainer `nahuelalbornoz`, repository **`github.com/marcosnahuel/moodle-mcp`** — 🔴 **an address mismatch**. Description claims it can *publish and manage pedagogical content*, so it is **write-capable** | 🔴 **region. No country, institution, or affiliation appears anywhere in the package metadata** |
| `brightspace-mcp` **1.3.1** (2026-09-28) | 🟢 **MIT** with `LICENSE`, **547 paths** — the largest connector payload on this shelf. Author *"Jhostin Aleck"*, repository `JhostinAleck/brightspace-mcp` | 🔴 **region, on the same grounds** |

🔵 **A personal name is not a region, and onomastics is not evidence.** This KB has recorded a
LATAM-ownership gap for eight consecutive passes. Closing it on the strength of two given names
would convert a measured absence into a false presence — the exact failure mode `p137-E` caught when
two "empty" regions turned out to be probe artefacts, and the opposite error. 🟢 **Both rows are
carried with `region: UNMEASURED` and the measured facts attached**, so a later pass that finds real
affiliation evidence can promote them in one edit.

🔵 **What would actually close this gap**, named so a future pass can aim at it: an affiliation in a
`pyproject.toml`/`package.json` author field, a university or ministry in a repository owner, a
LATAM-domain homepage, or a LATAM institution as the copyright **holder** of a licence file. None of
the 21 packages has any of these.

🟡 **The demand side is unchanged from p137 and still the stronger half of this region's case**:
UNESCO IESALC's 2026 survey of **200 institutions across 19 countries** found **87%** using AI in at
least one area and **74%** in teaching and learning, against only **26%** holding a formal AI
strategy — and the 2024 TALIS figures for secondary teachers (**Brazil 56%, Chile 55%, Colombia
53%, Costa Rica 52%** against an OECD average of **36%**) say the classroom is ahead of the
institution. 🔵 **The region's opportunity remains governance-shaped: adoption is not the gap,
and a studio selling adoption here is selling what the region already has.**

#### 🆕 p137 — the regulatory picture now rests on the instrument's own words, and money appears for the first time

🟡 **Brazil, read in Portuguese (`ACTION BZ`): the national instrument exists and declares itself
non-binding.** The MEC published ***Inteligência Artificial na Educação Básica*** on **2026-04-08**
(launched with a webinar; the document sits under `gov.br/mec/.../escolas-conectadas/arquivos/`),
intended to *"orientar redes de ensino na construção de currículos, práticas pedagógicas e políticas
institucionais"*, with a teacher course — *IA na prática docente* — opened alongside it. A March 2026
**Referencial** by Segape precedes it and is 🔴 **explicitly described as having no normative
character**, while the **CNE** debates national diretrizes. 🟢 **So this KB's standing claim — that
LATAM teacher AI use runs 16–20 points above the OECD average with no binding education rule — now
rests on the instrument's own self-description rather than on an absence.**

🟢 **And the region finally has placed money, at deal level:**

| what | amount | date | placement |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Google → Raspberry Pi Foundation**, Experience AI for Latin America | **USD 5M** | **March 2026** | **nine countries**, target **1M+ students** and **24 000 educators** |
| **Luca** (Mexico) Series A, basic-education AI | **US$8M**, led by **6 Degrees Capital** (total funding **>US$13M**) | **January 2026** | Mexico and LATAM, basic education |
| **Colombia, MinEducación generative-AI roadmap** | not disclosed | **June 2026** | **180 000 teachers** trained, licences for **64 public universities**, with UNESCO and the **IDB** |
| LAVCA, all-sector context | **USD 2.431B** into LATAM startups in **H1 2026**; **55%** of first rounds went to AI companies | H1 2026 | 🟡 all sectors — context, not an edtech figure |

🔴 **What is still missing, and it is the same hole as the market section's**: there is **no credible
LATAM AI-in-education market size**. The only regional share available prints LATAM at a flat **5%
in 2026, 2030 and 2036**, which is a model constant. 🟢 **Deal-level figures with dates and lead
investors are what this page can defend, so they are what it publishes.**

🔴 **Supply: a seventh consecutive pass with no LATAM-owned standards row.** The only LATAM artefact
added this pass is `src/auth/javeriana-sso.ts` — a **Pontificia Universidad Javeriana Cali** SSO
module **inside** a US-owned Brightspace connector. 🔵 **The region appears in the supply chain as an
integration target, not as an owner**, and that sentence is now backed by a file path.

- **Adoption is ahead of governance, more starkly than anywhere else.** UNESCO IESALC reports
  widespread AI adoption across higher education in Latin America and the Caribbean with governance
  lagging. The IDB's review of 193 regional initiatives found 59% using generative AI. In Brazil,
  generative AI reportedly reaches 43% of teachers and 58% at high-school level (secondary source —
  verify before quoting).
- **Regulation is pending almost everywhere, and education-specific rules are essentially absent.**
  Brazil's PL 2338/2023 is advanced but not enacted. Mexico's AI amendments to date are in
  copyright, labour and audiovisual law — **not education**. Brazil, Argentina, Chile, Colombia and
  Costa Rica are each advancing their own frameworks. There is no regional instrument.
- **Capital and institutional momentum are real:** HolonIQ's 2026 LATAM EdTech 100 shows AI-native
  positioning quadrupling since 2020 and the cohort leaning toward instructional support rather
  than consumer tutoring; Brazil is ~45% of the regional cohort. Google.org is funding AI literacy
  across Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Colombia, the Dominican Republic, El Salvador, Mexico, Peru and
  Uruguay, targeting 1.25M students by 2028. UNESCO launched a regional AI-in-education observatory
  and, in August 2026, a pilot with CONALEP and DGETI in Mexico.
- 🟢 **`ACTION BE` DISCHARGED BY p133 — and "no LATAM supply" is REFUTED.** This KB declared a LATAM
  supply zero four times and pre-registered the reason it might be wrong: the probes had only ever
  used English category terms. Run with platform proper nouns, LATAM returns **12 addresses** —
  **SIGAA** (5), **SUAP** (3), **SIU-Guaraní** (4) — in `agents/top.md` and
  `evidence/verified-repos.2026-10-11-p133.tsv`. 🔴 **The claim immediately above this bullet, that
  `campus-cli` is "the only LATAM-origin row in the entire KB", is therefore retired.**
- 🔴 **And the LATAM rows are a different animal from the rest of the tier: they serve staff.**
  Three of the twelve automate a *teacher's* obligation — `expeditto` (AGPL-3.0, **org-owned**, with
  a product homepage, drafting a lecturer's **RIT** workload report from SUAP), `auto-sigaa` (MIT,
  grade entry from a spreadsheet) and `Auto_Grade_Guarani` (grade upload to SIU-Guaraní). 🔵 **Two
  countries, two national systems, the same unmet need: moving grades and workload into a system of
  record that a human is currently retyping.** That is the regional opportunity stated concretely,
  and it is `T9` with addresses attached for the first time.
- 🟡 **Licence shape of the LATAM slice, because it differs from the rest of the shelf:** **5 of 12
  MIT (42%)**, 3 unlicensed, 2 GPL-3.0, 1 AGPL-3.0. The staff-side builders here reach for copyleft,
  and the single most institutional project (`expeditto`) is the one that cannot be forked.
- 🟢 **The vendor consortium is reachable, which is new.** [`SIU-Toba`](https://github.com/SIU-Toba)
  — the Argentine university consortium that *publishes* SIU-Guaraní — has its own GitHub org
  (`docker-guarani`, 4★). 🔴 **Unlicensed and dormant since 2017**, so not a counterparty today, but
  it is the first named sanctioned-integration counterparty this KB has for pattern `P8`.
- 🔴 **The named instrument behind this page's 92% / 79% figures, finally identified.** They are the
  **Digital Education Council "AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026"**, run with **Tecnológico
  de Monterrey's Institute for the Future of Education** — not a Tec observatory release as earlier
  passes implied. New predicates from it: **50% of students** support AI-assisted feedback on
  assignments while **only 19% of faculty** currently give it that way, and **only ~30% of LATAM
  universities have published** a policy on AI use. 🔵 **The 50/19 gap is the most directly sellable
  number on this page: demand for a feature that the people who would operate it are not using.**
- 🟢 **IESALC's fieldwork, now with its denominator.** The **87% adoption / 26% with a formal
  framework** figures come from **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries, surveyed
  August–October 2025** (so they date to late 2025, as p132 established). By area: **teaching and
  learning 73.5%**, research **57.0%**, administration **34.1%**, community engagement **20.0%**.
  🔴 **Administration is the second-least-served area in the survey and the layer with the most
  open-source supply (above) — that mismatch is the LATAM engagement thesis in one line.**
  By institution type: private non-profit **84%**, public **68%**, private for-profit **52%**.
- 🟡 **Country-level regulation, which p132 said ILIA was needed for.** **Chile** leads — a National
  AI Policy since 2021 and a bill under discussion. **Brazil** and **Colombia** have national AI
  strategies but **no education-sector rule**. **Mexico**: SEP, ANUIES and the Observatorio IA have
  issued recommendations that are **explicitly non-binding**. 🔵 **The IDB's Latin American AI Index
  (ILIA) covers 19 countries** and remains the instrument to read for depth. 🔴 **Net: there is still
  no education-specific binding instrument anywhere in the region** — which is why the EMEA
  conformity work (`compose/patterns.md` → `P4`) does not transfer here as a compliance sale.
- 🔴 **Probes that returned nothing, named so the gap is informed rather than silent.** Per
  `agents/top.md` → clause 4, each is reported with the probe that produced it:
  | country | platform probed | result |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Chile** | `ucampus`, then `ucampus chile universidad` | 🔴 bare probe returned a **Chinese** homework tool and an Indian app (homograph collision); **qualified probe returned 0**. **No evidence found for U-Campus.** |
  | **Peru** | `siagie` | 26 results, **all** zero-star school coursework, no AI, no licences. SIAGIE has a national installed base and **no agent layer at all** |
  | **Brazil** | `q-academico` | 85 results, almost all 2018-era student UML exercises. Real installed base, **no agent layer** |
  | **Uruguay** | `bedelias` | 28 results, nothing relevant — student clones of a *bedelía* system, no connectors |
  | **Colombia / Mexico** | `simat`, `saes` | not reached this pass — **carried to `ACTION BJ`** |
  🟢 **Peru's SIAGIE and Brazil's Q-Acadêmico are the two cleanest unclaimed LATAM slots this KB has
  ever been able to name: a real system of record, millions of records, and zero open-source AI.**
- **Opportunity:** be the governance layer that regulators have not yet imposed. Institutions
  adopting at speed with no legal framework will face one within the engagement's lifetime — build
  for Brazil's PL 2338 shape now. Second, **offline and low-bandwidth delivery is a first-class
  requirement here, not an edge case**: Kolibri (MIT, offline-first) plus a small local model is a
  differentiated, legally clean offer. Third, **Blackboard and Chamilo matter in this region** and
  are unserved — `campus-cli` (ISC, Peru) shows demand with an 11-star answer.
- 🆕 **The IESALC figures are now dated, which changes how they may be quoted (p132).** The
  UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS working paper surveyed **200 higher-education institutions across 19 LAC
  countries**, and the fieldwork ran **August–October 2025**. 🔴 **So this KB's held *"87% adoption
  / 26% with a strategy"* describes late 2025, not 2026** — present it as a baseline, not as
  current state, because the denominator and the window are both now on record. 🟢 Tec de
  Monterrey's observatory shows movement since: **students 86% → 92%**, **teachers 61% → 79%**.
- 🆕 **A named instrument for the LATAM regulation gap.** The **Latin American AI Index (ILIA 2025,
  published by CEPAL)** carries a dedicated regulation sub-dimension within its governance pillar —
  the country-level detail this KB has repeatedly looked for and failed to find in news channels.
  🔵 **Next pass should read ILIA 2025 directly rather than re-running regional news queries.**
- **Supply-side gap, stated plainly:** LATAM-origin open-source education AI is nearly nonexistent.
  🔴 **This did not improve this pass, and the contrast is now stark.** p132 added **21** new
  SIS/portal agents: nine EMEA, seven APAC, four North America, two unplaced — and **zero LATAM**.
  `alejooroncoy/campus-cli` (ISC, 11★, Peru) remains the only LATAM-origin row in the entire KB.
  🟡 **And the regional school platforms that would be the obvious targets — Blackboard and
  Chamilo, both with real LATAM share — have one connector between them.** Globant's regional
  position is a genuine advantage precisely because nothing is being built locally; this pass makes
  that the single most consistent finding in the KB rather than a one-off observation.
- 🔴 **Nothing offline targets LATAM either.** The offline stack found this pass
  (`project-nomad`, Apache-2.0, 39 444★) is unplaced, and the one curriculum-specific offline
  project is Indian. Given that this page argues offline delivery is a *first-class requirement* in
  LATAM rather than an edge case, a Spanish/Portuguese curriculum deployment of that stack is an
  unclaimed, permissively licensed, and now much cheaper piece of work.


#### 🟢 p134 — the adoption/governance gap now has a denominator, and a fourth country shape

🔵 **The headline LATAM numbers this pass adds are about the gap, not the uptake**, which is the
sellable half:

| figure | source as reported | 🔴 denominator warning |
|---|---|---|
| **87% of LATAM universities already use AI** | UNESCO-channel reporting, 2026 | 🔴 the base is *institutions reporting use of AI in at least one area* — **not** institutional deployment at scale |
| 🔴 **only 26% have a formal strategy; only 9% evaluate its implementation** | same | 🟢 **This is the number to lead with.** A 61-point gap between use and strategy, and a **78-point gap between use and evaluation**, is a governance-and-measurement engagement described in two figures |
| **92% of students report using AI tools** | **Digital Education Council LATAM Survey 2026** (with Tec de Monterrey), **across 29 institutions** | 🔴 **29 institutions is a small base** — and this KB already holds the 92%/79% pair from p133. **Do not restate it as a regional census** |
| **Brazil: 53% of school administrators use AI, 22% report school guidelines on its use** | Brazil administrator survey, 2026 | 🟢 The same gap at K-12 administrative level — **31 points** — and it is the layer `T9` says is least served |

**Country regulation, extended:**

- 🟢 **Brazil** — **PL 2338/2023** is the vehicle. The **Senate approved versions on 2024-12-10** and
  it remains in the legislative process; **risk-based, with at least ten criteria for classifying
  high-risk systems**. 🔴 **Still no education-specific rule** — so the risk classification is the
  thing to track, not an education statute.
- 🟢 **Colombia** — **policy-led, and the most concrete planning horizon in the region**: national AI
  policy via **CONPES 4144, February 2025**, a government-wide programme with actions and **budget
  through 2030**. A separate AI bill would add **fundamental-rights impact assessments** and a
  **national registry of high-risk AI systems**. 🔵 **Pair this with the SIMAT dropout-prediction
  model** (`verticals/solutions.md`): a ministry-facing predictive model in a country heading toward
  mandatory FRIAs and a high-risk registry is **the clearest named compliance-plus-build opportunity
  in LATAM**.
- 🟡 **Mexico** — **no comprehensive AI law.** A 2026 criminal reform on AI deepfakes was pending
  Senate approval, possibly taken up **September 2026**; 🔴 **the 2026 amendments already enacted
  cover performer rights, not education.** Mexico's AI-in-education policy remains explicitly
  non-binding (carried forward from p133).
- 🔵 **Chile — a demand/supply split worth keeping straight.** p133 recorded Chile as
  *"no evidence found for this platform"* on the **supply** side (both `ucampus` probes, `P133-E`).
  🟢 **p134 adds a demand-side data point that does not contradict it:** a **2026 Chilean university
  reported AI adoption at scale while still expanding its measurement of learning outcomes.**
  **Adoption at scale with outcome measurement lagging is the single most common shape in this
  market** — and it is `P6`'s pitch, with a named instance.
- **UNESCO / IDB** — the **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** met
  **September 2026**; a **Mexico pilot with CONALEP and DGETI** is named; the IDB has published a
  study of **193 AI-in-education solutions** across the region. 🟡 **None of these was fetched in
  full this pass** — they are leads with names, not measured findings.

🟢 **Supply:** p133's 12 rows stand, all licence cells **re-confirmed by payload read** this pass
(`ACTION BI`), and p134 adds **Q-Acadêmico (Brazil, ~7 rows, best is Apache-2.0)**, **SAES (Mexico,
19 hits, nothing usable)**, **SIMAT (Colombia, one ministry-facing model)** and 🔴 **SIAGIE (Peru):
no evidence found, probe named**. See `verticals/solutions.md`.

🔴 **The structural fact for LATAM, restated after four countries were probed properly:** **Brazil
has four national academic systems with real builder communities; Mexico has scrapers and no
licences; Colombia's activity targets the ministry; Peru has no footprint.** **"LATAM" is four
markets, and only Brazil has supply this KB could staff an engagement from today.**

#### 🆕 p135 — `ACTION BM` PARTLY REFUTED here: "LATAM" is the one regional word that works, and the mechanism is now named

🟢 **The bare probe *"AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players"* succeeded** where the
identical construction failed for EMEA and APAC. It returned education-sector primary research with
denominators:

| finding | figure | source |
|---|---|---|
| Institutions using AI in at least one area | **87%** | UNESCO **IESALC** study of **200 institutions across 19 countries** |
| Institutions with **any formal AI framework** | 🔴 **26%** | same study |
| By sector | private non-profit **84%** · public **68%** · private for-profit **52%** | same study |
| Students actively engaging with AI | **92%** | Digital Education Council **LATAM survey 2026**, **30 000+ responses**, with Tecnológico de Monterrey's Institute for the Future of Education |
| Faculty actively engaging with AI | **79%** | same survey |
| Universities with a published AI-use policy | 🔴 **~30%** | regional commentary |
| Regional readiness | an **AI readiness index covering 19 countries** | **IDB** |

**National regulatory posture, as reported:** **Chile** has had a national AI policy since 2021 with
a bill under discussion; **Brazil** and **Colombia** have national AI strategies **without
education-specific rules**; **Mexico's** guidance from **SEP** and **ANUIES** 🔴 **lacks binding
force**. 🟢 **So the region's shape is: near-universal use, almost no binding rule, and a quarter of
institutions with any framework at all** — which is the governance gap of `T1` at its widest
anywhere in this KB, and the one place where the buyer is the institution rather than the state.

🔵 **The mechanism, which is the transferable part.** "LATAM" works as a probe because
**region-scoped education bodies publish under that label** — UNESCO's **IESALC** is literally the
institute for higher education in Latin America and the Caribbean, and the **IDB** publishes
education research for the region. 🔴 **"EMEA" and "APAC" have no counterpart**: no ministry,
university network or multilateral publishes education research addressed to "EMEA". 🟢 **So
`P134-H` generalises as a rule about INSTITUTIONS, not about words:** a bare regional probe returns
education material exactly when a region-scoped education body exists to produce it. That is
testable in advance of any search, and it tells this KB which regions must always be probed by
country.

#### 🆕 p135 — `ACTION BQ`: the ministry-dataset axis exists outside Colombia, and it has no institutional supply at all

p134 opened `ACTION BQ` to probe by national dataset name (C-600, SIMAT, Censo Escolar/INEP, SISTEC,
ENEM), predicting refutation *"if fewer than 3 such rows exist outside Colombia."* 🟢 **Not refuted
— Brazil alone has dozens.** 🔴 **But every one of them is an individual or academic analysis, and
not one is owned by a ministry, institute or university.**

| probe | rows | largest | owner class |
|---|---|---|---|
| `censo escolar inep educacao dados` | **7** | **3★** | 🔴 **Individual and academic throughout** — BI dissertations, quilombola-community analyses, a Rio de Janeiro schools explorer, a DAMA-DMBOK governance project. **Zero institution-owned** |
| `enem microdados inep` | **35** | **30★** | 🔴 Individual throughout, and the top of the layer is **a clone cluster** (below) |

🔴 **The clone cluster, and it is a measurement result rather than an impression.**
`api_enem_microdados` — an API over Brazil's ENEM microdata — exists under **four distinct owners**
with **byte-identical `README.md` (sha256 `fea59dc854f35eac9cb72f14`, 1 842 B)**, identical
descriptions, **identical GPL-3.0 licence bodies**, and `fork: false` on all four:

| owner | ★ | created | pushed |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`filipedeschamps`](https://github.com/filipedeschamps/api_enem_microdados) | **30** | **2022-07-25** *(the original)* | 2026-10-08 **05:37:35Z** |
| [`rendgentmars`](https://github.com/rendgentmars/api_enem_microdados) | 10 | 2025-12-29 | 2026-10-08 **05:37:46Z** 🔴 **11 seconds later** |
| [`livrunakcream`](https://github.com/livrunakcream/api_enem_microdados) | 7 | 2025-12-29 | 2026-01-12 |
| [`bluesky816`](https://github.com/bluesky816/api_enem_microdados) | 1 | 2025-10-28 | 2026-08-19 |

🔵 **What this means for the LATAM picture, and it is not only a data-hygiene point.** The
ministry-facing dataset axis is **real and active** — Brazil publishes ENEM and Censo Escolar as
open microdata and people build on them. 🔴 **But the supply is hobbyist, and its apparent size is
inflated by uncredited copying.** A studio sizing "LATAM open-source education supply" from star
counts or repository counts will overstate it. 🟢 **The underlying opportunity is the inverse of the
supply:** Brazil's national datasets are open, documented and unserved by any institutional tool —
which is a build, not an integration.

🔵 **It also upgrades this KB's own mirror detector.** p134's `ACTION BG` built a signal from
near-coincident `pushed_at` timestamps, which flagged this cluster correctly (11 seconds apart).
🟢 **But byte-identity of a NON-LICENCE payload is decisively stronger**, because a shared licence
body proves nothing — 12 unrelated owners in this pass's corpus share one MIT body. See
`compose/code/p135-licence-sha-identity` → cross-owner identity, and `agents/trending.md` → Pass 135.

#### 🆕 p136 — the governance gap gets a third denominator, and the region's regulation is moving on the general track, not the education one

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** 🟢 **LATAM's bare probe works for the second consecutive pass** — the only
region where it does — and this pass it returns the number that matters most to a studio.

| measure | figure | why it is the sellable one |
|---|---|---|
| universities using AI (UNESCO IESALC 2026) | **87%** | the demand is established |
| with a **formal strategy** | **26%** | the governance gap |
| 🔴 **that EVALUATE their implementation** | 🔴 **9%** | 🟢 **the third denominator, and the narrowest. Four in five institutions that HAVE a strategy cannot show it works** — which is an evidence engagement, not a policy one |

🟢 **And basic education has its own pair, from Brazil:** **53% of school administrators use AI**
while **only 22% report their school has guidelines** on it. Same shape as higher ed, one level
down, with the buyer being a secretaria rather than a rector.

🟢 **Teacher-level adoption, placed by country from TALIS 2024** — the first time this page can put
a *comparable* instrument's numbers side by side:

| country | secondary teachers who used AI in the previous year |
|---|---|
| **Brazil** | **56%** |
| **Chile** | **55%** |
| **Colombia** | **53%** |
| **Costa Rica** | **52%** |
| 🔵 OECD average | **36%** |

🔴 **Every LATAM country in that table is 16–20 points above the OECD average, and not one has a
binding education-specific AI rule.** That inversion is this region's whole commercial argument.

🟡 **Regulation: the movement is on the GENERAL AI track, and education rides it.**

| country | instrument | state as measured |
|---|---|---|
| **Brazil** | **PL 2.338/2023**, the EU-AI-Act-inspired framework — the region's most developed | 🟡 **Passed the Senate 2024-12-10, still in progress.** 🟢 Separately, **ANPD is running a regulatory sandbox for AI and data protection until December 2026** — a dated, nameable venue |
| **Mexico** | 🟢 **2026 reforms are the first wave of BINDING obligations** — rights for artists against AI simulation, criminal-law reform on deepfakes | 🔴 **The criminal deepfake provision is pending in the Senate**, and a general AI bill is still in discussion |
| **Colombia** | **CONPES 4144** (policy, **February 2025**); a bill would create **CON-IA**, a national AI council under the Presidency | 🟡 Policy first, statute later — and a 2024 analysis found Colombia's instruments **mostly non-binding** |

🔵 **Supply: the one LATAM lineage this pass measured is an attribution finding, not a supply
finding.** `ACTION BW` resolved the four `api_enem_microdados` repositories at source level: **the
root tree is identical**, the 20 commits carry identical messages and dates, and **not one commit
sha is shared** because every commit was re-authored — one of them to an identity that is not even
the owner's. 🔴 **So the top of Brazil's national-dataset layer is one project and three
history-rewritten copies**, which means the layer is **thinner than its row count**, and
`agents/top.md`'s *"no standards-native row originates in LATAM"* survives a sixth pass.

#### 🆕 p150 — the adoption-governance gap is the global pattern, and the open-source counterparty is missing

🟢 **One pattern holds across every region measured this pass, and LATAM is the only place it
is measured precisely:**

| region | adoption | governance |
|---|---|---|
| **LATAM** | 🟢 **87%** institutions using AI (UNESCO IESALC, n=200, 19 countries) | 🔴 **26%** with a formal strategy |
| **North America** | **60%** of K-12 teachers used AI in 2024–25 | 🟡 **~70%** "have or are developing" guidance |
| **EMEA** | — | 🔴 **10%** of 450+ institutions with **established** guidelines |
| **APAC** | breadth high (Singapore **60.9%** diffusion) | 🔴 **depth shallow**, Korea's penalties in a grace period |
| 🔴 **Africa** | 🔴 **no adoption figure** — 🟡 **35%** of sampled Kenyan schools have working internet | 🟢 **four national strategies, zero deployment counts** |

🔵 **The consistent finding is not that institutions resist AI — it is that they have already
deployed it without governing it.** 🟢 **That is a services sale, and it is the one this KB's
governance patterns (`P35`, `P28`, `P36`) are actually built for.** 🔴 **It is also a warning
about this KB's own shelf: `P35`, the pattern it recommends for exactly this gap, has an
approval gate that `ACTION FD` proved bypassable at the data layer and `ACTION FF` has not
closed.**

🆕 🔴 **The market-vendor layer has no open-source counterparty.** A commercial APAC market
report names **Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and BYJU'S**; the HoloniQ and sector reads name
platform consolidation and "purpose-built educational AI." 🔴 **Not one named vendor in any
regional source this pass is an open-source project**, while this shelf now holds **58
measured subjects plus 5 new rows** that are. 🔵 **The gap between what the market reports
name and what is actually forkable IS this KB's product**, and it is worth stating as the
reason the shelf exists.

🟡 **Market size: the spread is unchanged and nothing is adopted.** Research and Markets puts
2026 at **USD 10.6B** reaching **USD 42.48B by 2030** at a **41.5% CAGR**; a K-12 Academics
report runs from **~USD 6B (2024)** to **~USD 32B by 2030**. 🔴 **Two publishers, two 2030
figures 33% apart.** 🟢 **No market-size figure is adopted by this page** — unchanged from
p148 and p149, and restated so the silence is not read as absence of searching.

🟢 **The maturity read is the only figure this page adopts: 35/100, pilot mode.** 🟢 **Three
independent regional signals now agree with it** — Korea's explicit pilot year, APAC's
shallow depth, and LATAM's 26% strategy rate.

#### 🆕 p144 — the market size spread is unchanged, and the adoption figures are not comparable

| publisher | 2026 | horizon |
|---|---|---|
| Research and Markets | **$10.6B** | **$42.48B by 2030** |

and both figures are recorded without adoption.

🔴 **The adoption statistics circulating this year cannot be compared with each other**, and
stating why is more useful than averaging them:

| claim | population | problem |
|---|---|---|
| **54%** of K-12 students use AI | students | 🔴 different population from the next row |
| **92%** of university students use AI | students | 🔴 different education stage |
| **86%** of education orgs use generative AI | organisations | 🔴 **organisational**, not individual — and the same 86% appears attributed to North America alone elsewhere |

🔴 **The 86% figure appears both as a global organisational rate and as a North America rate in
two different publishers.** 🔵 **That is the `T36` lesson in a different medium**: a number is not
comparable to another number just because both are percentages, and the population is the part
that has to match.

🟢 **What the global channel does agree on, across four independent publishers:** the shift this
year is **from experimentation to GOVERNANCE** — clear policies, data boundaries, oversight —
together with **agentic use cases**, **purpose-built education platforms over generic tools**, and
**digital credentials** as the mechanism for skills-based learning and hiring.

🔵 **Three of those four are things this KB's connector shelf is already a supply map for**, and
the fourth — credentials — is the one layer it holds nothing on. 🔴 **Recorded as a declared gap.**


**Pass 142.** 🟢 **The three-pass null demand channel is broken** — six live repositories, three
region-placed, from eight queries plus one retry. 🔵 **And the retry is the lesson.** The eight
registered queries describe *industries* (*"AI education market"*, *"trends"*, *"adoption
regulation players"*) and returned market-research marketing pages. The ninth described an
*artefact* — `open source education MCP "agente IA" GitHub Brasil Argentina México universidad` —
and returned four repositories, two of them LATAM-placed. 🟢 **Same hour, same engine and
same region — the retrieval that finds rows names code, not markets.** `P138-H` recorded this for
registries; `T10` is amended for the search channel.

🔴 **Not one market FIGURE was verified, and the cause is now a two-line allowlist request.**
`api.uis.unesco.org` and `sdmx.oecd.org` answer `000`; `eur-lex.europa.eu`,
`artificialintelligenceact.eu`, `capitol.texas.gov`, `oecd.org` and `algoritmos.uniandes.edu.co`
answer `000` on `curl` **and** `ENOTFOUND` on `WebFetch`, while `raw.githubusercontent.com`
answers `200` on both. 🔵 **The positive control is what turns five passes of "sources blocked"
from an attribution into a measurement: both fetch channels share one allowlist.**

🔵 **The structural asymmetry, now precisely located.** This hour produced **10 artefact verdicts
and 2 refutations**, withdrew a ten-pass regional finding, and added 5 rows. 🔴 **The figure
channel produced zero verified numbers** — not because the demand side is saturated, but because
every primary statistical source is unreachable and every reachable source is re-quoting them.


#### 🔵 p141 — the asymmetry between the two channels is now the global finding

🔴 **Third consecutive pass in which the demand channel produced nothing** — eight queries, zero
new rows, zero corrections — 🟢 **while the artefact channel produced six verdicts, four of them
refutations of this KB's own prior findings.** 🔵 **That asymmetry is the global statement worth
carrying**: the governance gap this page calls "the market" is measurable at the artefact layer
and wider there than any survey reports.

Four measurements from this pass that no survey on this page captures:

| measurement | figure |
|---|---|
| 🔴 npm education connectors declaring **no repository** in any published version | 🔴 **5 of 8** |
| 🔴 Maven coordinates whose licence is **not in their own POM** | 🔴 **5 of 9 (56%)** |
| 🔴 tables in a flagship agent's schema **invisible to a `.sql` reader** | 🔴 **96 of 118 (81%)** |
| 🔴 licence/SPDX/copyright notices inside `openedx-core`'s six packages | 🔴 **0 of 237 files** |

🔵 **Each is a number a buyer would want before signing, none is in any market report, and all
four were measured in under an hour from published artefacts.** 🟢 **That is the global
opportunity in one line: the due-diligence layer for open-source education software does not
exist, and it is cheap to build.**

🟡 **One global caution, stated against this pass's own output.** `T30` records that three of
this KB's trends were published as claims about education software when they were claims about a
packaging tier. 🔵 **The market-level reading of any artefact finding on this page must name its
tier**, and the four figures above do.


#### 🆕 p140 — the demand channel has stopped producing information, and the supply channel produced five corrections

🔴 **For the second consecutive pass, the global search battery returned nothing new** — 86%
adoption, VC at ~$1B in H1 2026 (**−26% YoY**), 29% of students judging instructors equipped, and
US+Canada faculty intent falling **76% → 67%** are all already on this page. 🔵 **`T10` now reads
at full strength: the demand-side channel is returning this KB's own contents.**

🟢 **Meanwhile the artefact channel produced five verdicts, three of them corrections to p139.**
🔵 **That asymmetry is the global finding of this pass**, and it has a direct bearing on how a
studio should spend research time:

| action | verdict | what it changed |
|---|---|---|
| `CP` | 🔴 **NOT REFUTED** | A connector's tool surface is a property of its **deployment flags**, not of its version. `P18` step 5 amended |
| `CQ` | 🟡 **NOT REFUTED, weakly** | 1 of 5 foreign headers; 🔴 **3 of 5 rows carry no source header at all** — 287 files, zero |
| `CR` | 🔴 **REFUTED on both rows** | Maven's declared-but-absent rate rises **40–67% → 80%**; a grant has a *depth* (`T26`) |
| `CS` | 🔴 **REFUTED** | `openedx-learning` is a **rename shim**, not a broken release; the live row is `openedx-core` (`T27`) |
| `CT` | 🔴 **NOT REFUTED** | The agent tier holds `learners.email` **and** a per-session psychological profile |
| `CU` | 🟢 **DISCHARGED** | Two regions booked on compiled-in hostnames (`T29`) |

🔵 **The commercial reading: the governance gap this page has called "the market" for fourteen
passes is now measurable at the artefact layer, and it is wider there than any survey reports.**
Adoption is at 86% and venture funding is retreating 26% — 🟢 **which is a services market, as
p139 said.** 🔴 **What p140 adds is the substance of those services**: a shelf where 80% of Java
artefacts ship no licence text for a licence they declare, where three repositories in one project
give three answers to "who holds this", where a connector's capability list changes with a
deployment flag nobody documents, and where 6 of 8 packages cannot be placed in a region at all.
🔵 **None of that is visible from a market report, and all of it is billable.**

🟢 **And one genuinely encouraging global datum, measured rather than surveyed.**
[`ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp`](https://github.com/ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp) — **MIT**, 279 source
files against **286 test files** — ships a **one-way** high-stakes classification whose evidence
policy types `host_llm` and `external_service` evaluations as *insufficient*. 🔵 **The model cannot
certify itself.** 🔴 **That is the property EU AI Act Article 14, Annex III, Vietnam's Decision 33
and the Oklahoma and Maryland statutes are all reaching for, and it is being implemented by a
single alpha-stage maintainer before any vendor on this page.** It is now pattern `P19`.

🔴 **The global blocker, stated plainly.** No primary regulatory source has been read for two
passes. The cause is fully characterised: the proxy gateway answers **`403` to `CONNECT`** for
government and university hosts alike — an allowlist of package registries plus GitHub — and
separately, **executing an installed connector is refused by sandbox policy**. 🔵 **Neither is a
probe to re-register.** Both are requests to widen a policy, and they belong to whoever owns this
environment rather than to the next action list.

#### 🆕 p139 — the demand signal and the funding signal are now moving in opposite directions

🔴 **🆕 Venture funding into education AI fell to ~$1B in H1 2026, down 26% year on year**, while
institutional adoption is reported at **86%** of education organisations using generative AI —
🔴 **most without a policy.** 🔵 **The divergence is the datum**: adoption is near-saturation and
capital is retreating, which is the profile of a market moving from *buying tools* to *integrating
and governing what it already bought*. 🟢 **That is squarely a services thesis rather than a product
thesis**, and it is the clearest market-level support this page has for the pattern library's
direction.

🔴 **🆕 Two faculty-side signals cut against the adoption number.** Per the **Digital Education
Council**'s 2026 global survey: only **29%** of students believe their instructors are well equipped
to guide them on AI use, and 🔴 **faculty intent to use AI in the US and Canada FELL 9 points, from
76% (2025) to 67% (2026)**. 🔵 **A year-on-year *decline* in practitioner intent, against rising
institutional deployment, is the first reversal of its kind this page has recorded** — and it
predicts exactly the failure mode a studio gets called in to fix: tools bought centrally, unused
locally.

🟡 **Market-size figures were again not reconciled, and again will not be.** One publisher puts the
AI-in-education market at **$7.52B (2025) → $10.6B (2026)**; another at ~**$6B (2024) → ~$32B
(2030)**. 🔴 **These are different scopes, not different forecasts** — the reconciliation this page
attempted at p136 and abandoned at p138 is not reattempted, and the column is not widened further.

🔵 **The structural reading this pass adds, from its own measurements rather than from a publisher:**
the open-source education-AI supply chain is **recent, thin and under-documented**. Of the nine
published LMS connectors measured, **six disagree with their own documentation**; **62** registered
write tools are undocumented; one package is a **downloader with no integrity check**; one core Open
edX library currently **ships no code at all**; and the **LTI library this KB recommends** ships no
licence text. 🟢 **None of that is visible from a market report, and all of it is billable
diligence** — which is the most defensible global opportunity statement this page can make.


#### 🆕 p138 — the spread did not converge, and a second publisher now disagrees about the same year

🔴 **p137 recorded a 2026 column of USD 7.50B–11.4B plus a USD 1.94B outlier, and said the column
was widening rather than converging. It widened again.** This pass adds two more 2026 figures from
two more publishers:

| publisher | 2026 figure | growth claim |
|---|---|---|
| Research and Markets | **USD 10.6B** | **40.9% CAGR** |
| HolonIQ, as quoted by a vendor blog | **USD 12.3B** | — |

🔴 **USD 10.6B and USD 12.3B are a 16% disagreement about a year that is already three-quarters
elapsed.** 🔵 **This is now a stable, publishable finding rather than a recurring complaint: the
disagreement is about market *definition*, not measurement, and no amount of further sourcing will
close it** — p136 established that the widest figures come from firms defining the market broadly
and the narrow outlier from one defining it narrowly.

🟢 **So this page states the rule instead of chasing the number.** Quote a 2026 figure to a client
**only with its publisher attached and its growth rate alongside**, because the growth rates agree
far better than the levels do (40%+ CAGR appears across publishers who differ by billions on the
base). 🔵 **The CAGR is the quotable figure; the level is not.**

🔴 **Regional shares remain unusable.** p137 recorded one publisher printing LATAM at a flat **5%**
in 2026, 2030 **and** 2036 — a constant across three forecast horizons, which is a modelling
assumption rather than a measurement. 🔴 This pass adds **North America at 36% / USD 3.68B (2026)**
and **Europe at USD 2.64B (2026)** from a different secondary roundup, and the two cannot be
reconciled against each other or against any of the global levels above. 🔵 **No regional share on
this page should be used for sizing. They are recorded to show that the published ones conflict.**

- ~~Open-source supply is **not** regionally distributed in any useful way… Most agent repos carry
  **no location evidence at all** — 11 of the 17 agent rows are marked unplaced rather than
  guessed.~~ 🔴 **REFUTED BY p132 for the tier it did not know about.** The twenty-one new
  SIS/student-portal rows are **~90% placed on hard evidence** — a named university, a national
  platform, a country-coded homepage (`mine.ibu.edu.ba`, `qldt.hanu.edu.vn`) — giving
  **EMEA 9, APAC 7, North America 4, unplaced 2, LATAM 0**. 🔵 **The old claim was true of the tier
  it was measured on and false of the sector.** Famous repos are unplaced because they are
  international; small institution-bound repos come with their region attached as evidence. 🟢
  **Rule for later passes: if regional supply looks absent, the query was probably global.**
  Searching platform proper nouns (`librus`, `edupage`, `pronote`, `vtop`, `usaint`) returns placed
  rows that category terms cannot reach — see `agents/top.md` → the vocabulary rule.
- 🔴 **Correction to p130's "absent benchmark".** The benchmark layer is **not absent** — it is
  **ungranted**. `eth-lre/mathtutorbench` (43★, EMEA) carries no licence field and names two
  different licences in one README; `Khan/tutoring-accuracy-dataset` (57★, North America) carries a
  bespoke 2 690-byte evaluation-dataset licence and has been dormant since August 2024. 🟢 **What is
  genuinely absent is outcome measurement:** both measure tutor *behaviour*, neither measures
  durable learning gains — the standard the OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook asks for. The
  client who gets outcome evidence is still buying something no competitor can show; the build is
  just cheaper than p130 implied, because it starts from two real benchmarks.
- **The permissive/popular inversion** — the most-starred item in a tier is repeatedly the unusable
  one: Moodle connectors (most-starred `loyaniu/moodle-mcp`, 40★, **no licence**; permissive leader
  `peancor`, 43★ but quiet since February), Canvas (103★ `mcp-canvas-lms`, **no licence**, vs MIT
  `canvas-mcp` at 288★), agent skills (847★, **NOASSERTION**), benchmarks (both ungranted).
  **Licence screening is not a late-stage check in this sector; it reorders the shortlist.**
  🟡 **p132 qualifies the scope of this claim, which was overstated as "across every tier
  measured".** The new SIS/portal tier is **~80% MIT** — the inversion does *not* hold across it.
  🔴 **p133 qualifies p132's qualification, on the same error one level down.** The "~80% MIT" was
  measured on 21 rows that were EMEA/APAC/NA only. Adding the **12 LATAM rows** takes the tier to
  **42% MIT in that region** (3 unlicensed, 2 GPL-3.0, 1 AGPL-3.0) — and the single row p132 left
  `UNREAD`, `edwyj/ddl-killer`, reads **AGPL-3.0**, the most restrictive licence in the tier. 🔵
  **An unread row is not a random draw: the repos that are hard to read are disproportionately the
  ones doing something unusual with their licence. Measure before you average.**
  🔵 **But it still holds at the top of that tier:** its two most-starred rows are the weak ones —
  `librus-mcp` (10★) is mixed-licence (MIT 2.x over a **GPL-3.0-only** `legacy_reference/`) and
  `DAU-buddy` (7★) is unlicensed. 🟢 **So the accurate form of the finding is narrower and still
  useful: popularity does not predict licence quality, and the correlation is strongest among the
  biggest repos in any tier.** It is not a law about the sector.
- 🆕 **A third of this pass's licence findings came from reading a payload rather than a field.**
  `zavora-ai/mcp-student-records` reads **Apache-2.0** where the API says `NOASSERTION`, because its
  `LICENSE` is the **~745-byte Apache notice block** without the licence body. 🔴 **And one row is
  unlicensed by *declaration* rather than omission** — `h9-tec/graduation-project-advisor` (63★)
  states *"Private / TBD. Licensing terms will be finalized before any public release."* 🔵 Those
  are three distinct states a client must not be told are the same thing: **granted-but-unmatched**,
  **silent**, and **deliberately withheld**.


#### 🟢 p134 — the segment that is growing fastest is the one with the hardest compliance clock

| figure | source | note |
|---|---|---|
| **2026 market: ~$9.58B (Precedence) to ~$11.4B (Grand View)**; one report **~$10.6B at 41.5% CAGR** | market-research summaries | 🔴 **The spread is a definition difference, not a disagreement about the world.** This page's standing base-year warning applies; do not mix these with the NA-only series |
| 🟢 **Assessment and feedback agents: 44.36% CAGR — the fastest-growing segment** | segment forecast | 🟢 **The commercially sharpest number this pass found.** Driven by demand for automated grading of **open-ended** responses, and pushed by integrity concerns |
| **Intelligent tutoring systems held the largest share of agentic AI in education in 2024: 26.04%** | segment analysis | Largest *share*, not fastest growth — ITS is the incumbent, assessment is the mover |
| **AI tutoring outperformed in-class active learning, effect size 0.73–1.3 SD** | a randomised controlled trial | 🟢 **A real effect size from an RCT is rare in this market and worth carrying** — but 🔴 **this pass did not read the study**: the population, subject and duration are unverified, and an effect size quoted without them is a slogan |
| **UK university student AI use: 66% → 92% in one year** | sector reporting | The integrity pressure behind the assessment-segment growth |

🔴 **Why the fast segment is the hard one, and this is the synthesis a client needs.** 🟢 **Assessment
and feedback is the fastest-growing segment AND the most heavily regulated activity in every region
this page covers**, and three jurisdictions now converge on the same window:

| jurisdiction | obligation | date |
|---|---|---|
| **EU** | education uses in **Annex III high-risk**; emotion inference in education **already prohibited** since 2025-02-02 | 🔴 **2027-12-02** |
| **Oklahoma** | every district to adopt a written AI policy | 🔴 **before 2027-28 school year** |
| **Maryland** | AI coordinator per school system; AI literacy in standards | 🔴 **by June 2027** |

🟢 **So the offer writes itself, and it is not "an AI grader":** build the fastest-growing thing
(assessment and feedback) in the way the 2027 clocks will require — auditable, human-in-the-loop,
no affect inference, documented against a named framework — and the compliance work is the
differentiator rather than the tax. 🟢 **p134 adds the counterparty for it:** **UNIwise ApS**, a
Nordic/UK assessment-platform vendor publishing its **own MIT integration packages** including
**assessor allocation and grade passback** (`verticals/solutions.md`), and **Jisc's 38-institution
AI marking pilots** as the named sector programme already running.

#### 🆕 p136 — what a global KB learned about its own measurements this pass

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** Three of this pass's findings are not about education markets at all; they
are about how a market page accumulates wrong numbers, and they belong here because they apply to
every region above.

- 🔴 **A quantity needs a unit before it needs a source.** The $3.37B/45% row above is an
  **increment**, not a level (see *Market size*). p132 caught a base-year error; this is the same
  defect in a different axis.
- 🔴 **A count can disagree with itself.** Two trackers give **134 bills / 31 states** and
  **68 bills / 27 states** for the same US session, and a third claims *"35+ states"* over an own
  table reading **33–35**. 🟢 Recorded unresolved, with the likely cause named (different
  denominators — introduced vs education-specific vs enacted) and not averaged into a false middle.
- 🔴 **A probe's LANGUAGE is part of the probe.** Japan was "measured absent" for a pass because the
  query was English and the instrument is Japanese (`ACTION BT`, APAC above). 🔵 The same question
  now hangs over this page's **China**, **Korea** and **Brazil** cells, every one of which was
  built from English-language sources about non-English instruments.

🟢 **The one supply-side global fact worth a row.** The identity-broker register this pass found
(`agents/top.md`) is **six repositories, four of them MIT and two Apache-2.0, none education-specific
and five unplaced**. 🔵 **The component every sanctioned education-AI engagement needs was built by
the cloud-infrastructure community, not by edtech** — which is why an education-vocabulary probe
never found it, and why a studio can buy rather than build it.

## Declared gaps in this market picture

- **Africa and the Middle East are inside "EMEA" here but are not covered by it.** 🟡 **NARROWED by
  p132's dedicated probe, and still open.** The first MENA-targeted education AI project is now on
  record — [`h9-tec/graduation-project-advisor`](https://github.com/h9-tec/graduation-project-advisor),
  **63★**, Arabic-first, taking **Egyptian CS students** from an onboarding form to a
  supervisor-ready graduation project — and 🔴 **it is unlicensed by declaration**, so nothing can
  be built on it. [`mbzuai-oryx/ClimateGPT`](https://github.com/mbzuai-oryx/ClimateGPT) (80★,
  **MBZUAI, Abu Dhabi**) shows real institutional AI capacity in the Gulf but is not an education
  tool. 🔴 **Still missing entirely: any African or Gulf education-AI market sizing, any national
  regulatory instrument, and any Sub-Saharan row at all.** The 5-value region vocabulary keeps
  folding three very different markets into one bucket; this is a blind spot in the taxonomy, not a
  finding of absence.
- **No reliable open-source market-share data** for which LMS actually runs where, by region. The
  platform-share claims above come from vendor and roundup sources.
- ~~**The 2026 market-size spread (~$6.4B–$11.4B) was not resolvable** against primary sources from
  this runner.~~ 🟢 **LARGELY RESOLVED BY p132 — and it was not a source problem, it was a filing
  error here.** The low end was IMARC's **2025** base misfiled as a 2026 estimate; the genuine 2026
  figures agree within **1.19×** (~$9.6–11B). 🔴 **What remains open is the horizon:** 2035
  estimates span **~$70B to ~$1 169B (~17×)**, no primary report is readable from this runner, and
  one firm publishes two different CAGRs for its own forecast. **Treat any single long-range
  forecast as arbitrary.**
- 🆕 **No LATAM figure found this pass carries its country list.** A tracker reporting *"Rest of
  Latin America"* at **$18.2M (2024) → $36M (2029)** sits three orders of magnitude below this
  page's held LATAM figures purely on scope definition. A regional number without its denominator
  cannot be quoted.

### 🆕 Gaps this page declares after p136

- 🔴 **Higher education in North America is a hole, and so is Canada.** The NA probe returned ~100
  state bills, five dated statutes and four named district programmes — and **nothing** on
  higher-education policy, federal action or Canada. The richest region on this page is also the
  one whose coverage is most lopsided.
- 🔴 **No European procurement data exists on this page.** Four ministries' *intentions* are
  recorded above and not one framework agreement, tender or contract term. For a region whose buyers
  are public bodies, that is the gap that matters commercially.
- 🔴 **APAC has mandates and no market.** Dated curriculum obligations for five countries; **zero**
  vendor names, market sizes or private-adoption figures. The supply side of the fastest-growing
  region is unmeasured here.
- 🔴 **Three of this page's regional cells rest on English-language sources about non-English
  instruments** (China's hours, Korea's reversal, Brazil's PL). `ACTION BT` proved that shape can be
  wrong by a whole national policy. Until each is re-probed in its own language, read them as
  provisional.
- 🟡 **One quantity is unresolved rather than averaged**: China's minimum annual AI instruction —
  **6 or 8 hours** — and the US session's bill count — **134/31 or 68/27**. Both are countable and
  both are published differently by credible trackers.
